# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1984

## Śrīla Prabhupādadeva-saṅkīrtanāṣṭakam

*by Kuśakratha dāsa*

### Text 1

*govindadevāṅghri-saroja-bhakti-*  
*sudhārṇave yas tu mahā-taraṅgaiḥ*  
*aplāvayad yāvana-deśam eva*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*govindadeva*—of Lord Kṛṣṇa; *aṅghri*—of the feet; *saroja*—for the lotus flower; *bhakti*—of devotional service; *sudhā*—of nectar; *arṇave*—in the ocean; *yaḥ*—who; *tu*—indeed; *mahā-taraṅgaiḥ*—with tidal waves; *aplāvayat*—inundated; *yāvana*—of the *yavanas; deśam*—the country; *eva*—certainly; *namāmi*—I offer my respectful obeisances; *tam*—to him; *śrī-prabhupādadevam*—His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Churning the nectar ocean of** ***bhakti*** **for Lord Govindadeva’s lotus feet, he created great tidal waves that inundated the countries of the** ***yavanas.***

### Text 2

*āropayat keśava-bhakti-valli-*  
*bījān sahasreṣu śubheṣu hṛtsu*  
*gauropadeśokti-vareṇa yas tu*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*āropayat*—planted; *keśava*—for Lord Kṛṣṇa; *bhakti*—of devotional service; *valli*—of the creeper; *bījān*—the seeds; *sahasreṣu*—in thousands; *śubheṣu*—fortunate; *hṛtsu*—of hearts; *gaura*—of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu; *upadeśa*—of the instructions; *ukti*—of the words; *vareṇa*—excellent; *yaḥ*—who; *tu*—indeed.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. By preaching the message of Lord Caitanya, he planted the seed of the creeper of** ***kṛṣṇa-bhakti*** **in thousands of fortunate hearts.**

Note: This poem is written in *upajāti*, the same meter as Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s *Śrī Gurvaṣṭakam*.

### Text 3

*chittvā ku-tarkān ku-kavīśvarāṇāṁ*  
*śrī-kṛṣṇacandreśvaratā-pramāṇam*  
*saṁsthāpayaty eva kṛpānvito yo*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*chittvā*—refuting; *ku-tarkān*—the bad logic; *ku-kavi*—of bad philosophers; *īśvarāṇām*—of the leaders; *śrī-kṛṣṇacandra*—of Lord Kṛṣṇacandra; *īśvaratā*—the status as the Supreme Personality of Godhead; *pramāṇam*—proof; *saṁsthāpayati*—establishes; *eva*—certainly; *kṛpā-anvitaḥ*—merciful; *yaḥ*—who.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who refuted the host of impersonal and voidist theories and mercifully established the truth - that Śrī Kṛṣṇacandra is the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

### Text 4

*kaunteya-mitrasya su-satya-vācā*  
*māyā-kṛtaṁ yas tu vibhidya moham*  
*loke janaṁ rakṣati mugdhatāyā*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaunteya*—of Arjuna; *mitrasya*—of the friend; *su-satya*—transcendental; *vācā*—by the words; *māyā*—by *māyā; kṛtam*—fashioned; *yaḥ*—who; *tu*—indeed; *vibhidya*—breaking; *moham*—the hoax; *loke*—in this world; *janam*—the people; *rakṣati*—protects; *mugdhatāyāḥ*—from bewilderment.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. By speaking the transcendental message of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the friend of Arjuna, he exposed** ***māya’s*** **hoax, rescuing her victims.**

### Text 5

*śrī-kṛṣṇa-mantreṇa sudhauṣadhena*  
*kaler yuge loka-vimoha-rogam*  
*bhitte tu vaidyaḥ karuṇena dakṣo*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śrī-kṛṣṇa-mantreṇa*—with the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*; sudhā—nectar; *auṣadhena*—with the medicine; *kaleḥ yuge*—in Kali-yuga; *loka*—of the people; *vimoha*—of material bewilderment; *rogam*—the disease; *bhitte*—cures; *tu*—indeed; *vaidyaḥ*—the physician; *karuṇena*—mercifully; *dakṣaḥ*—expert.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. He became the expert transcendental physician administering the nectar-medicine of the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mantra*** **and curing the people of Kali-yuga from the disease of material bewilderment.**

### Text 6

*atyantikāpāra-punar-bhavābdhiḥ*  
*kṣaṇena go-vatsa-padāyate ’pi*  
*gaurāṅga-śikṣā-vadanena yena*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*atyantikā*—expanded; *apāra*—boundless; *punaḥ-bhava*—of repeated birth and death; *abdhiḥ*—the ocean; *kṣaṇena*—immediately; *go-vatsa-padāyate*—becomes like the puddle in a calf’s hoof print; *api*—even; *gaurāṅga*—of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu; *śikṣā*—the teachings; *vadanena*—speaking; *yena*—because of whom.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. By his preaching the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the boundless, impassable ocean of repeated birth and death immediately shrank to the size of a puddle in a calf’s hoof print.**

### Text 7

*prābodhayad yaḥ śukadeva-divya-*  
*sandeśa-vādena mukunda-prema*  
*suptaṁ tu śuddhaṁ hṛdayeṣu loke*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prābodhayat*—awakened; *yaḥ*—who; *śukadeva*—of Śukadeva Gosvāmī; *divya*—transcendental; *sandeśa*—instruction; *vādena*—by speaking; *mukunda*—for Lord Kṛṣṇa; *prema*—love; *suptam*—sleeping; *tu*—indeed; *śuddham*—pure; *hṛdayeṣu*—in the hearts; *loke*—in the living entities.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. By speaking the transcendental instructions of Śukadeva Gosvāmī he awakened the pure love dormant in everyone’s heart for Lord Mukunda.**

### Text 8

*mṛdaṅga-śabdaiḥ karatāla-nādaiḥ*  
*śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam utsavam yaḥ*  
*asthāpayad varṣa-sahasra-vṛndaṁ*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mṛdagga*—of *mṛdaṅgas*; *śabdaiḥ*—with the sounds; *karatāla*—of the *karatālas*; *nādaiḥ*—with the sounds; *śrī-kṛṣṇa*—of Śrī Kṛṣṇa; *saṅkīrtanam*—the glorification; *utsavam*—a jubilant festival; *yaḥ*—who; *asthāpayat*—established; *varṣa*—years; *sahasra*—of hundreds; *vṛndam*—a group.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Filled with the tumultuous sound of** ***mṛdaṅgas*** **and** ***karatālas*****, the festival he carried to the West will continue for many thousands of years.**

## The Meaning of Vyāsa Pūjā by Jayādvaita Swami

Vyāsa gave Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words,  
Resplendent with knowledge, complete,  
His followers carefully heard  
And faithfully worshiped his feet.

By passing down the essence,  
Each guru to the next,  
Invoking Kṛṣṇa’s presence  
Through adherence to the text.

It’s Prabhupāda’s kindness that he,  
With sure and infallible trust  
In the order of Sarasvatī,  
Brought Vyāsadeva’s message to us.

We therefore place upon our heads  
The message that we’ve heard  
And dedicate ourselves to spread  
His Kṛṣṇa conscious word.

At the feet of the servant of Vyāsa  
We reverently offer our heads.  
We pray to serve humbler than grass  
In doing as Prabhupāda said.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Introduction by Suhotra Swami

This collection of homages to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is ostensibly an “internal” ISKCON publication - meaning that it is something of such devotional intensity that a lay reader might have difficulty in understanding its importance. Why, year after year, do the members of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement produce such a lavish volume in such small numbers at such great expense? Some outsiders even sense something sinister here. A few years ago a Danish professor and noted critic of the new religious movements pulled a copy of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā off his shelf, smiled wryly at me, and gloated, “You see, I’ve been doing my homework on your movement. I’ve even got the book not meant for the public eye!”

Certainly this Vyāsa-pūjā book is not meant for the public eye, although obviously some members of the general public will have a chance to look through its pages. This book is intended solely as an offering of loving respect to the lotus feet of our spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in humble appreciation for the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness which he has so kindly bestowed upon us by his causeless mercy.

This offering is not something whimsical or recently invented. The Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is an ancient tradition, rooted in the spiritual civilization of Vedic India in which the mark of success in human life was the acceptance of a *guru* fully capable of delivering his charges from the cycle of birth and death. A disciple so saved by the divine grace of his spiritual master is thus eternally obligated to his *Guru* Mahārāja. The yearly offering in humble appreciation of the glories of the spiritual master upon the day of his appearance in the material world is, in one sense, almost an insignificant gesture in the face of the great debt owed by the disciple. Yet again, it is an offering of love, not of physical weights and measures. Since the very nature of the relationship between *guru* and *sisya* is one of love, acts of loving exchange such as the Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā celebration are transcendentally enlivening for all participants. Thus the bond between the spiritual master and disciple grows tangibly stronger with each passing year.

The loving bond between Śrīla Prabhupāda and his disciples and grand disciples is non-different from the loving bond between Kṛṣṇa and His devotees. Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, loves all living entities equally, even those rotting in this material world. But among all the living entities in various stages of consciousness, Kṛṣṇa reciprocates ecstatic feeling with His devotees. By dint of their pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Kṛṣṇa’s devotees are eligible to live with Him eternally in the spiritual world. Since active loving reciprocation with the Supreme Lord is something incomprehensible to the materialists, the merciful spiritual master, whose heart is churning with love of Godhead, appears in this world to awaken the sleeping souls and engage them in their constitutional relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda is especially merciful to the fallen souls in that for the first time in thousands of years he brought pure devotional service to Kṛṣṇa beyond the borders of India to the lands of the *mlecchas* and *yavanas.*

So this is ISKCON, the movement Śrīla Prabhupāda has created: a worldwide network of devotees bound in love to Śrīla Prabhupāda and thus bound in love to Kṛṣṇa. If we speak of the strength or weakness of ISKCON, we are ultimately speaking of the strength or weakness of that love. Indeed, the loving exchanges shared by Kṛṣṇa, *guru,* and disciple can only be weakened if the disciple breaks his contract with his spiritual master and again takes up his former affairs of ignorant sense gratification.

To guard against such fall downs, the disciple is instructed to worship his spiritual master as his life and soul, for he is our only link to Kṛṣṇa. Thus we honor and obey Śrīla Prabhupāda as our eternal father. For us, he is as good as God, for by his mercy alone are we engaged in devotional service to God.

Thus this book. One who is not very familiar with the principles of devotional service should try to appreciate the fact that it is the disciples themselves who benefit most from offering this book to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Such a loving offering draws Śrīla Prabhupāda’s merciful love to us, just like the simple, heartfelt words of a child attract the loving compassion of the father. Through this exchange of devotional feelings we associate with Śrīla Prabhupāda directly. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has explained that association with a pure *sādhu* such as Śrīla Prabhupāda is the root cause of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, and indeed the root cause of *prema,* pure love of Kṛṣṇa. Without Śrīla Prabhupāda’s continued merciful association, we would be lost.

If we speak of the spreading of ISKCON around the globe, we are ultimately speaking of the spreading of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s love by those devotees who have love for him. These devotees - among whose numbers I unfortunately cannot be counted - are awakening the sleeping souls by their inspired preaching of what they’ve heard from Śrīla Prabhupāda, and they are engaging them in his service, and thus in the service of Kṛṣṇa. The root of enthusiasm to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness on a large scale is also simply the loving exchange between Śrīla Prabhupāda and his sincere disciples. Thus this book.

Once every year, then, these stalwart preachers sit down before a photograph of Śrīla Prabhupāda, perhaps in a palatial temple in India, Europe, America, or Australia - or in a small back-street apartment preaching center behind the iron curtain, or in some remote corner of East Asia. Looking deeply into their hearts, they attempt to formulate words of praise and submission, remembrance and confession, avowal and supplication, perhaps while struggling to translate their feelings from Spanish or Japanese into the English language.

Once every year, the members of ISKCON stand humbly before the *mūrti* of Śrīla Prabhupāda and read, or speak from the heart, homages such are found in the following pages. And once every year, ISKCON worldwide is showered by an especially sweet rain of mercy - *saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam* - by which the fire of material existence afflicting this entire planet may be extinguished.

Suhotra Swami

## Vyāsa Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Each time you met me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted to convince me that Kṛṣṇa is the possessor of all opulences. I came to you with so much admiration. I expected so much from you. But every time you deflected my praise for you by pointing me towards that blue boy Kṛṣṇa. I wanted *you* as my object of worship, but the more stubborn I was in that desire, the more insistent you were to correct me. “If you are genuine in your feelings toward me,” you said, “you must hear my message of Kṛṣṇa’s.” For me, Kṛṣṇa was too far, too incomprehensible. You were so close, so available. But you refused to have anything to do with me unless it was centered around *your* object of love, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

After some time, I learned my lesson: I could not have you without Kṛṣṇa. And this understanding brought my appreciation of you to a still higher level. I noticed how you were even not willing to be known as a pure devotee. You simply loved Kṛṣṇa, and the by-products of that love manifested as your unlimited good qualities, like millions of aquatics being created in a great ocean.

You manifested your humility by announcing yourself on your arrival in America as “an insignificant beggar” of the Lord. You manifested your compassion by demonstrating your anxiety to give His mercy to everyone. You manifested your detachment by never hankering for anything other than what Kṛṣṇa provided for you.

You were fixed because you constantly prayed to Kṛṣṇa to protect you from His *māyā.* You led millions because you always saw yourself as a follower of your *guru,* rather than a leader. You were always careful and attentive because you knew you were always dealing with Kṛṣṇa.

You were expert, Śrīla Prabhupāda, not by your material experience and accomplishments, but by the purity of your desires. You were faultless, not by your ability to avoid making mistakes, but by your readiness to take risks in the service of Kṛṣṇa. You were steady, not because you never changed your mind, but because you absolutely depended on Kṛṣṇa in every decision.

You forgave us our foolish mistakes, because you knew how He forgives. You called us to move forward each time we reached a crossroad, because you heard Kṛṣṇa calling you. You started so many “impossible” projects, and you made them part of your legacy, by your unflinching faith that Kṛṣṇa’s will must be done.

You actually saw Kṛṣṇa face to face, through the medium of your beloved *Guru* Mahārāja. And that made you our *jagat-guru*, our *paramahaṁsa*, the purest of pure devotees. You always had more love to give us than we could demand, more than we could conceive. This wealth of love made you our ever well wisher, a person completely self-satisfied and forever beyond imitation.

Your genuineness was what convinced us to follow you, to hear your message. You were so absorbed in delivering that message that you never cared about the impression you made on others. Neither were you concerned about the number of disciples you made. You simply cared that whoever *did* hear you, they should hear you clearly and receive your message of service to Kṛṣṇa. You wanted us to follow you in transmitting that message to others. Follow you, never imitate.

After you planted that message in our hearts, you engaged us. You engaged us not because you found us qualified, but, through your mercy, we had a little desire to help you in your service to Kṛṣṇa. As a loving father, you cautioned us against pride in carrying our your mission. You wanted us to cooperate together to maintain it. With this quintessential instruction, you entrusted your movement to us.

In your final days, you gathered us to your bedside. You wanted your movement to go on, and you gave us our most significant and most painful lesson. You lay in your bed in an apparently helpless condition. You acted as if you were totally dependent on us. This was the final glory of your *līlā* as *jagad-guru*, because you forced us in our desperation to keep you amongst us, to become humble and helpless ourselves. You, the cause and life-force of our spiritual existence, were exhibiting degrees of surrender far beyond what any of us were capable of. This revealed to us the reality of our helpless condition. It brought us, more than ever before, together as your children.

The humility you showed us during those days and nights pulled on our hearts, pacified our egos, and gave us more of that elusive love and trust you had requested from us.

While you gave up your physical body, you were teaching us how we could preserve your body of ISKCON. Your exhibition of complete surrender and detachment in those last days in Vṛndāvana was your unprecedented lesson to us to give up our petty doubts, our speculations, our attempts to be “the best” or most successful disciple.

My prayer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my beloved spiritual master, is that you help me fix my mind on your divine body lying in your bed in Vṛndāvana. Let me remember you as you were surrounded by your soon-to-be-orphaned children, and let me remember my own helplessness and humility at your bedside. This, I feel, is your true legacy to me. Not the buildings or the titles, but the mood of desperate humbleness I felt in the face of losing you. If, by your mercy, you allow me to live in this mood, then I shall forever remain the most blissful, insignificant servant of your will and of your body, ISKCON.

*Your servant,*  
Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

## Balavanta dāsa

Worldwide renouncer,  
Magnificent and free.  
Benedicting the masses  
Once glanced upon me.

Spiritual warrior  
Fight for the right  
Give earth direction  
Transforming the night.

Internal, external, marginal,  
Three  
The Lord’s diverse powers  
All known by thee.

External illusion,  
We’ve all lost our way  
Helplessly hoping  
To dawn a new day.

Our precious link  
With internal affairs  
Lost and concealed  
By historic err.

We’ve searched o’er the earth  
For fortune and gold  
Gained the whole world  
Neglected the soul.

But like Lord Maheśa  
Who bears Gaṅgā’s force  
You open the world  
To internal discourse.

That internal potency  
Which you partake  
Can alter our fortune  
Reveal heaven’s gate.

From your lotus lips  
And into our ears  
It cleanses our hearts,  
Brings trembling and tears.

Real satisfaction  
Touching the soul,  
Bounteous rewards  
Fulfilling life’s goal.

That nectarean message  
Emanating from you  
Will cover the earth  
Like pure morning dew.

It makes no distinction  
’Tween stones, grass, or gold  
But cleanses each surface  
As dawning unfolds.

So come rich men, come poor men,  
Come fools and come sages  
Your truth is for all  
The same through the ages.

Old paths may be narrow,  
But your was is great.  
There’s space for all ashrams;  
None you forsake.

In your pristine palace  
The whole world can live  
Since you’ve nothing to take -  
You’ve just come to give.

Balavanta dāsa

## Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

You are the all-auspicious limb of Śrī Kṛṣṇa extended to the people of this world.  
Had you remained here longer what is there you could not have done?  
For thousands of years to come, the destines of millions will have been marked by you,  
And you will have changed the course of the ocean of humanity.

In the wake of your powerful descent your raised up a cloud of effulgent dust like devotees.  
By the breath of your order you scattered them across this planet.  
Thus they have surpassed the sun, whole light still leaves half the world in darkness.

I am one of those innumerable pieces of dust that was uplifted in the whirlwind of your lotus feet.  
Now you have left me behind. O mountain like and unsurpassable master, what can such an insignificant particle of dust do for you now?  
Your footsteps have covered this universe and beyond, and only those true souls attached to you can find direction in them,  
As only fire can enter into fire.

If you had remained longer, you would have given the entire *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and gone on to the *Mahābhārata, Rāmāyaṇa* and others.  
Who will finish that work for us?

If you had remained longer, all the Vedic sciences would have been revived.  
Who will extract them now?

If you had remained longer, we would have seen the full lotus of the  
Māyāpur city unfold.  
Who will build it now?

If you had remained longer, your books would have been translated into thousands of dialects.  
Who will translate them now?

If you had remained longer, your books would be distributed in geometric  
progressions.  
Who will distribute them now?

If you had remained longer, Darwin and his followers would have  
become curiosities in Māyā’s zoo.  
Who will defy them now?

If you had remained longer, the greatest scientists would be relieved from  
their speculation about the nature of reality.  
Who will enlighten them now?

If you had remained longer, varṇāśrama education and training would  
have produced a new race.  
Who will understand that science now?

If you had remained longer, politicians would have taken their direction  
from you.  
Who will capture them now?

If you had remained longer, you would have guided us through the  
inevitable war and social madness.  
Who will lead us now?

If you had remained longer, the organization of worldwide Vaiṣṇavas  
would surpass any establishment of Kali-yuga.  
Who will organize like that now?

If you had remained longer, masterfully designed temples of Rādhā and  
Kṛṣṇa would be constructed all over the world.  
Who will decorate the world like that now?

If you had remained longer, our farms would be able to give shelter to  
thousands.  
Who will be able to prepare for that now?

If you had remained longer, our Indian members would have become a  
powerful force behind us.  
Who will encourage them now?

If you had remained longer, a Kṛṣṇa conscious army would be left  
walking the earth.  
Who will they obey now?

If you had remained longer, all cheating religions would have been  
exposed, and bhagavata-dharma would have become the world  
religion.  
Who will preach like that now?

If you had remained longer, our own daily newspaper would be read  
throughout the world.  
Who will edit and print that now?

If you had remained longer, our art, music, theater and literature would  
have ignited a cultural revolution to capture the world.  
Who will inspire that now?

If you had remained longer, cities modeled after Māyāpur would rise up  
all over the world.  
Who will construct them now?

If you had remained longer, your BTG would have been full of interviews  
with your meetings with world heads of states.  
Who will meet them now?

If you had remained longer, the chanting of the holy name would be a  
daily occurrence in every town and village of the world.  
Who will spread saṅkīrtana like that now?

If you had remained longer, scholars would be flocking to you to learn  
the infallible logic and reason of the Vedic conclusion.  
Who will reason with them now?

O lord and master - we are helpless and incapable without your mercy;  
as the sun ray depends on the sun, as the rain depends on the ocean, as  
the embryo depends on the father, and as dharma depends on God, our  
existence depends on you.

Whatever we may accomplish in this or other lives is nothing other than  
you working through us.  
As useless wire becomes alive by electric current, so insignificant conditioned souls can liberate universes simply by Your Divine Grace.  
Let us live only to see your ecstatic achievements of Kṛṣṇa consciousness unfold, for even though you have gone, your fame is spreading eternally all over the three worlds, who take shelter at your lotus feet.

Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my lord and master,  
Kindly give me shelter at your lotus feet.  
The influence of the modes of nature  
Only your divine mercy can cleanse.  
Fortunate are those whose hearts were  
Touched by your gentle glance.  
Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja sings of your glories:  
”Guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa haña śāstrera pramāṇe  
Guru-rūpa kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe.”  
The revealed scriptures enunciate  
The spiritual master to be nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa;  
Kṛṣṇa in spiritual master’s form  
Showers blessings upon the devotees.  
Gurudeva, I have nothing to offer, but beg  
Please bless me, that I may sing  
Your glories birth after birth.

Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhaktitīrtha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

For about the last ten years I have written you a letter on the average of one letter every three days. Of course, even when you were on the planet I did not mail you these letters, but I kept them as a diary. My writing you in this way has always been one of the greatest aids in my devotional service. Although I had very little of your personal service, and even less direct instruction from you, I have found that I could always be with you not only through your lectures, books, devotees, etc., but also by each day setting aside a distinct time when I could write you a letter. I wanted to be sold out to you (even though I can’t, or perhaps I cannot do so due to many lifetimes of making *vaiṣṇava-aparādha*). In taking inventory of my mind and senses, I can appreciate that I am doomed, but my intellect can appreciate that everything is based on our desires. So my plan has remained that even though I am so weak, if I can increase my genuine desire to surrender to you, then Kṛṣṇa would give me the ability to serve you unconditionally. So I write in the mood of reporting directly to you each day about my services and service attitude, and still I am trying up to now to remain very accountable to you. It is as if at the end of each day I submit to you a report about how I have tried to do my small part for your mission.

In these diary notes I am unfortunately sometimes seeing how day after day and even year after year I am holding on to certain *anarthas*. Therefore my actual desire to surrender is not very wonderful. At each writing I go over accomplishments and especially mistakes and problems as I beg your forgiveness for being so poor in my area of responsibility for your movement, and I normally vow to do better the next day. I never want to start taking my contract with you for granted, as you have accepted me as a disciple (despite the fact that I am practically an *asura*). Now it is my duty to behave as a disciple of such a *jagad-guru* as yourself rather than as an immature servant, although I write such offerings regularly, trying to reveal my mind and attempting to glorify you. It is once a year during your Vyāsa-pūjā that I try to make an even stronger commitment to dedicate my life fully to you. So I write you this year feeling that I have a little better understanding about profundity of your tremendous, astronomical contribution to the universe and your causeless mercy, but also never before have I been more embarrassed as I am now, reporting to you, as I am still so far from the standard that you have requested and given your entire being for. As I pledge to you each day to put me in those situations that will literally force me to become as you would, and as I beg (unfortunately insincere) that you force me to become pure, still I do not see how even after many lifetimes I will give up my attachments, especially to sex life. Somehow I have some small understanding that if I can serve your actual genuine disciples, that somehow year after year you will continue to allow me to serve you.

While riding through the African villages I am writing you this offering. I find myself thinking about how wonderful but how difficult my field of service is. I am thinking about how far these people are from knowing the Absolute Truth, but then I also must reflect on how far we all are in your vision from appreciating the Absolute Truth, but still you are engaging us. Just as you preached to your last breath, following your *Guru*’s order, we, your disciples, are wanting to represent you with such similar dedication. Let us all go on increasing our services multifold according to our fields, so that very soon no one on the entire planet will be able to say that he was not informed that Kṛṣṇa is God, and everyone on the planet will be given full opportunity to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. It is your causeless mercy that has made and is making the seemingly impossible an intense reality.

I am the lowest of your chief assistants; therefore I am a burden for you and them. However, today once again I try to honestly put my case before you, begging you to never kick me away and pleading for your forgiveness for being such a burden to the Vaiṣṇava community.

Today or perhaps tomorrow I die, but still I have a little time left to serve you, so please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me surrender fully to you (even though I can’t).

*Your lowest servant,*  
Bhaktitīrtha Swami

## Bhavānanda Swami

On this glorious day to all we declare  
The appearance of Prabhupāda, appearance so rare.

When all else has crumbled defeated by time,  
His words sing out clearly, a message sublime.

Stop birth and death in a world full of fear;  
Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, the Lord will draw near.

For God is a person with senses of love  
Who returns our affection from Goloka above.

On this glorious day we should all stop and hear  
How Prabhupāda’s life was to Kṛṣṇa most dear.

For he sacrificed all to preach far and wide,  
Enduring all hardships as our ultimate guide.

For to lead such as us with our sinful desire  
Is no easy task and great love does require.

So let us rejoice in the love, which he gave,  
And fulfill his desire, the whole world to save.

On this glorious day let us renew our pledge  
To work as one man, between us no wedge.

For by working together none can defeat,  
Rallying round his divine lotus feet.

Defeat opposition with truth bold and pure  
Before which no lust or deceit can endure.

A new order of life where preaching will thrive,  
For that will keep Prabhupāda with us alive.

Bhavānanda Swami

## Brahmānanda Swami

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for taking the words of you *Guru* Mahārāja so seriously.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for going out from Vṛndāvana, the stage of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s eternal  
pastimes, of which you are a part.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for being so determined that you journeyed to America by the only  
means available - a boat.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for risking your frail life and being able to somehow withstand two heart  
attacks that would have felled any ordinary person.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for not being content to sit in living rooms and parlours chatting with  
middle America.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for not even being content to be sheltered  
in the swanky *yoga-āśramas* of uptown Manhattan.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for following Lord Kṛṣṇa as He led you to us, who were waiting in the  
depths of the Lower East Side just for you, although who could have  
known it?

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for sitting with eyes closed, playing a bongo drum and singing in deep  
ecstasy Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, which made us get up and dance  
for an entire afternoon and forever.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for miraculously transforming a tiny storefront into the spiritual world  
where everything was made of effulgent touchstone, filled with wish-  
fulfilling *kalpa-vṛkṣa* trees and hundreds of thousands of *surabhi* cows  
yielding all spiritual desires, where every one of us was trying for the first  
time to be a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for cooking *kichaurīs*, your personal favorite, and offering them to Lord  
Kṛṣṇa and then giving out the remnants with your own golden hand onto  
the plate of me, a stranger, and unto my heart and soul.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for noticing that the new boy Bruce had washed your pots and pans  
better than the others had done.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for handing us a string of wooden beads that connected us to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is more than God, because He is a person, and He lives, and you taught us how to live with Him.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for writing your books by sitting in front of your metal box and at first  
typing out the words on the hand typewriters we gave you, which we had  
used in college.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for handing over the manuscripts to us to publish and for giving your  
*Back to Godhead* magazine for us to write after you had delivered to us  
your seed-planting lectures on the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*, the great  
*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and the sublime *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for suddenly going out from our warm little group far away to California,  
thus making us stand up by ourselves, forcing us to walk in your lotus  
footsteps in service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, even though we had been crippled for  
lifetimes and had never walked before.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for coming back to the storefront to perform a unique ceremony over two  
shiny brass Deities of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, then ordering us to bathe, dress,  
feed and worship Them every day in your upstairs apartment as you left  
to go preach in some other place.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for again returning to America, after even another heart stroke had taken  
you back to India, where your health was most acclimated; still you came  
back to us to try again to risk your life yet again.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for instructing your new eager devotees how to preach, perform  
*saṅkīrtana* on the streets, distribute your books, canvass people, worship  
in the temple and observe festivals and manage things properly, and thus  
establish you Los Angeles world headquarters.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for chastising me and then handing me a *sannyāsa-daṇḍa*, while sending  
me out alone in the world to just preach your message and thus be with  
you always.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for surprising us by going on further to a completely new field - modern,  
secular India - with your young but now successful movement to show  
what they were trying so hard not to remember.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for although under the stresses and hardships we all folded up like  
wooden chairs, you stayed there, year after year, until you had  
established three wonderful transcendental berths in the harbor of India  
upon the sea of godlessness, where we could all take shelter and develop  
more and more, forever.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for journeying in old age to every corner of the earth, bearing the same  
transcendental message, never changing or compromising; of all your  
God brothers you were the only one to do what Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta  
wanted, ever injecting the medicine of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and  
saving the fallen, both big and small.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for wiping away all doubts and defeating all arguments to convince us  
scientifically of a truth so simple and obvious that we never knew, despite  
our American education: life comes from *life*, not from matter, with the  
conclusion that life can go *back* to life - such a reassuring realization that  
removes all the bleak existential bewilderments, giving us the clear,  
bright light of spiritual truth.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for repeatedly, in every lecture and in every purport, carefully countering  
and deftly defeating the sly arguments of the impersonalists, who always  
say the Absolute Truth is one, therefore everything is God (but if  
*everything* is God, then *God* is nothing), who say that they are God but  
cannot answer how it is that God has come under the influence of *māyā*  
and forgotten that He is God, who silently meditate, “I am God, you are  
God, and Kṛṣṇa is not God” - all of them exposed by you as atheists.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for gathering us around you in Vṛndāvana to witness your most intimate  
pastime, the leaving behind of your body and this world to go back again  
to Godhead, while we could only cry out like orphans for fear of being  
left behind in this material world, a wasteland where not one other  
person on the planet knows Lord Kṛṣṇa as you did.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for leaving behind all your hopes, plans, programs, projects, teachings,  
zones, *deśas*, books, and even our own disciples, all to develop for  
lifetimes together; and for somehow ever remaining amongst us to guide  
and inspire while we try to fulfill your wonderful vision.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for giving us something that one could never dream of even if he could  
dream for ten thousand years - Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the Absolute  
Truth.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for making it all possible, because without you there was no hope for this  
misguided world, nor even for myself, even while some amongst us fell  
back into private hells from which we had come.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for teaching me how to thank you and to want to follow your pure  
example with greater determination this year than ever before, and to try  
to make the continent of Africa Kṛṣṇa conscious, which you personally  
told me you wanted to see.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for making me a happy person.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
for allowing me to continue to serve you.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you.

*Your spiritual son and servant eternally*,  
Brahmānanda Swami

## Girirāja Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

At this moment, as I sit to write you, I feel myself utterly unfit and incapable of doing anything. Fortunately, you have formed the G.B.C., a transcendental clearing house of devotional service, so whatever little service we have begun can be presented to the Vaiṣṇavas. They can utilize it, fashion it, or finish it in whatever way they see fit. Without your mercy we are incapable of doing anything. Bereft of your mercy, we can simply sit and weep.

Your mercy is manifest to us through the strong arms and helping hands of the G.B.C. They can lift us up when we are falling down. They can pull us along faster when we are going ahead. They can embrace us and encourage us in whatever condition and position we are in. They are the manifestation of your mercy upon a poor soul, struggling, crawling, dragging himself to the shelter of your lotus feet.

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Hari bol.

I am very glad to know that you are still present - in a very personal way - in your instructions.

For a very long time I have been lamenting your separation, the absence of your personal presence. This reluctance to accept your separation, which meant my inability to appreciate your presence in the shape of your instructions, was even affecting my relationship with your beloved master, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

I had a grudge because you were taken.

Of course, it was Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa who sent you in the first place, and it was He - you said in your humility - that was attracting us to Him through you, His transparent via medium.

Still some feeling was there.

Now by your mercy I have come to know that you are still present - not only you alone, but along with your eternal master, guide and friend, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

I thought I had lost something - the personal presence of Your Divine Grace, my only shelter, guide and friend, my very life itself.

But now I see that not only are you still with me, but Lord Kṛṣṇa is also present with you, in the form of His instructions in your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*and His pastimes in the *Kṛṣṇa* book.

So by reading your books and following your instructions, I find that rather than having lost anything, I have actually gained to an unlimited degree.

”One who gives to Kṛṣṇa is not the loser, but he is the gainer by millions of times.”

“Brahman \[Kṛṣṇa, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness\] is by nature blissful. It is unlimited and always expanding.”

I pray that no matter what happens to me, either in this life or any of the many more to come, I pray for only one benediction - no matter what happens, I pray that I may always remember your lotus feet (and never forget them) and keep unflinching faith in the order of my spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

*Your worthless servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

*Dear Prabhupāda,*  
I offer my most humble obeisances at you lotus feet. Once again we are celebrating your divine appearance on this planet, and once again I find myself totally inadequate in expressing my debt of gratitude to you. As Uddhava expressed his inability to properly glorify the Supreme Lord, and as Ananta-śeṣa with his many mouths could not describe the transcendental qualities of Kṛṣṇa, similarly, with a million mouths your transcendental qualities and achievements cannot be described. You were always a Vaikuṇṭha man.

*yāṅra citte-kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*

**”Even the most learned man cannot understand the words, activities , and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead.” (*****Cc. Madhya*** **23.39)**

Sixteen years ago you picked me up from the ocean of nescience and gave me the gift of transcendental vision. By your mercy the divine knowledge of devotional service has been revealed within my heart, bestowing *prema-bhakti* and destroying ignorance. Were it not for you, I would be rotting in the endless cycle of birth and death in different species.

On this auspicious day, I would like to reaffirm my loyalties to your spiritual instructions. You taught us that there are two ways of associating with the spiritual master: *vapuḥ* and *vāṇi*. *Vāṇi* is more important. In dedicating the First Canto to your spiritual master, you stated, “He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.” I am praying for your mercy so that my love and devotion for your instructions will develop and increase unlimitedly. I pray that I may never deviate from your instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a Vaiṣṇava of the highest order. You are a part of Lord Caitanya’s own spiritual family. You are the personification of determination. You struggled against all odds to establish this Kṛṣṇa consciousness to guide humanity in this dark age of Kali. You gave us the spiritual framework to run the Society. It is by your potency that this movement is growing and increasing daily. You taught us that the Supreme is a sentient being and not impersonal, thereby giving us Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa worship. You taught us how to worship the Lord’s *arcā-vigraha* form. You gave us the Bhaktivedanta purports to Śrīla Vyāsadeva and Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja’s works. Prior to your appearance on this planet their works were not available in the English language. Your Bhaktivedanta purports will be the sole guide to humanity for the next ten thousands years. You taught us the importance of *saṅkīrtana*. You emphasized the importance of book distribution as a means to purify the fallen souls of this age. Just like the leper *brāhmaṇa* Vāsudeva was cured by Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you have cured millions from the abominable condition of material suffering. Your Bhaktivedanta purports have been converting millions of crow like men into swans. You have not only cured us of the disease called materialism but also given us a very happy life to live.

You once stated that the disciple can never repay the debt to the spiritual master, but can attempt to do so by preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You taught us that to be recognized as a devotee by Kṛṣṇa, one must take to preaching seriously. Because of your tireless efforts, the entire world is now given a chance to be saved from the horrible onslaught of repeated birth and death. You created so many wonderful Vaiṣṇavas, who are sincerely following your instructions and in turn are producing so many first-class grandsons. You gave us the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* as the most important business. The sound of *karatālas* and *mṛdaṅgas* is familiar in cities all over the world. Your gifts cannot be listed. You were perfect and complete in everything you did.

Thank you millions of times for showing me the path of devotional service. Please forgive me for my offenses. Please bestow your mercy so that I can go on working with my God brothers and disciples to spread this great movement that you have started. Your causeless mercy is all that I am made of.

*Your eternal servant*,  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Harikeśa Swami

*My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept my humble obeisances thrown in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, the all-perfect founder*-ācārya* of this wonderful International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, the most important branch of the Caitanya tree for the next ten thousand years.

By your grace and kindness, you are mercifully engaging me in your glorious *saṅkīrtana* movement. I feel greatly honored to be a part of this great movement as it is the prime benediction for humanity at large. Although my part in this vast movement is small and limited, still I can taste the eternal nectar by your mercy. The smallest portion of it is intense and deep.

At first, I did not completely understand the importance of this *saṅkīrtana* movement. It took some years before the import of your movement made its full impression on me. But when I fully understood the urgency of serving you through *saṅkīrtana*, I realized that there is nothing else even fractionally as important for humanity or as pleasing to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

The production of your transcendental literatures is our primary duty, for production precedes distribution like a cloud before a rainfall. It is only through the distribution of your books that there will be any peace or real happiness in this world. You began your *saṅkīrtana* movement in America, since you saw that country as the most important factor in world change. If they would only take up this movement, then they could influence the world to change. The whole world is in the grips of demoniac and atheistic social systems that are meant to destroy anything good in the human society. Unfortunately, America is degrading faster than one can imagine. If we do not redouble our *saṅkīrtana* book distribution efforts there, then America may soon fall totally into the depths of demoniac mentality. It is painfully close now. Please give us the strength to do that for you and the world.

From America the book distribution effort spread throughout the world, and I am presently serving your *saṅkīrtana* mission in Europe and South America. I am pleased to be your eternal servant, and nothing please me more than printing and distributing your books in every language I can. Although I am personally filled with many bad qualities and faults, I get great strength and enthusiasm simply by thinking of your mission. I have made your mission my mission, and I beg you for your help to enable me to spread that mission to the hearts of all those who are following me. I am very happy to engage others in your mission, as it is spotless, without the slightest tinge of doubt.

I very much want to attain your mercy, and I know that your mercy is easily attained when I please you by producing and distributing your books throughout the world. In Vṛndāvana, in 1976, you ordered me as follows: “I have so many banc accounts full of money. You empty all of them and fill up all the temples with books. When the temples are simply filled with books, there will be no place for the devotees to stay. To take rest, they will have to distribute all the books to make place. But then print more books.” This is a competition beyond compare. You especially wanted your books distributed by the millions in India, because the atheistic trends you saw there forebode disaster. Your books would reestablish the real religious principles in the land of *dharma*.

Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, nothing would please me more than to simply spend the rest of my life printing and distributing your books. Please grant me the blessing to be able to do so regardless of the external circumstances of the world. It does not matter where one is in the world - east or west, north or south - for the mission is the same everywhere, and the urgency is the same everywhere: an urgency so great that the order is to be shouted from the rooftops with all the power at one’s command, “Print and distribute Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books now. Do not delay. Time is short. Do not delay.” Everywhere throughout the world there is a unity of purpose, printing and distributing the books of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder*-ācārya* of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust and ISKCON.

Some people think that this is nothing but fanaticism. I agree with them completely. It is most ecstatic to be totally fanatical about printing and distributing your books. The ecstasy of having a full set of your books to present to people in a foreign language is beyond compare. What will I do when we run out of languages? I beg you to make me more fanatically fixed in this mission in the future. No matter what happens, I wish to be always engaged somehow in producing and distributing your books all over the world.

This is the perfection of all activity. Generally one thinks of brahminical activity as the most perfect. The *brāhmaṇa* is the teacher of all *varṇas,* and a *sannyāsī* of all orders, and no one is a better *brāhmaṇa* or *sannyāsī* than you, since no one on this planet could teach others as perfectly and relevantly as Your Divine Grace. The perfection of teaching, therefore, is to teach others what you have taught us. There is certainly no better way to teach others what you have taught than to give out your teachings directly in the form of your books, and I am praying to you to please always engage me in this most perfect activity for the rest of my days. Simply giving out your books gives to mankind your most important teachings, and therefore you called your book distributors the *brāhmaṇas* in society. I want to help train and inspire these book distributors eternally, for that is the perfection of intelligence.

Others may talk about perfection, and some may attain it as well, but you have given this world the most perfect example of speaking perfectly, acting perfectly, and engaging nonperfect persons in the most perfect activity, the production and distribution of your books all over the world. You therefore are as munificent as Lord Caitanya, because you are giving the perfect message of the perfect master. It is like a shower of life-giving rain on the desert. I bow down to you again and again and offer you my life and soul.

*Your foolish, fallen servant, waiting for a drop of nectar at your door,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. On the occasion of your most blessed Appearance Day, I respectfully beg to offer two poems, about you, at your lotus feet.

**Reflections on Salvation, Even in Modern Times**

I

Executive, peon  
shapely, deformed  
fabulous, hideous  
all, all adorned  
with the fabrics  
of earthly complexion

stepping out all ghostly cadence  
the beat of highly drugged drummers  
Time, time, the tide  
moving clay feet  
to the funeral beat  
for the fool, the wise fool  
the savage,  
the bosom and hairy breast  
ravage  
the drummers, their beat  
and their cadence repeat:  
Time, time, and the tide

II

But darkest clouds  
split and drift  
sky blue, blazing beams  
break through  
the world’s gloom  
killed by light, people  
sigh and smile

Thus the sun, sun, glaring glory, God’s  
own ray, right arm  
His eyes and tongue  
O Prabhupāda! Light of God!  
To kill the world’s doom -

Through the clouds  
Your honest arms  
wrapped around our maddened chest  
Close, close to the precipice  
Thwarting the hell-bound drummer

The vulgar pit  
held foaming at bay  
The bloody dagger  
tucked neatly away  
by your mercy  
O lord! O master!

And on, with you  
To the Orchard of Love  
Home and abode  
With Kṛṣṇa above  
Forever bliss, foreverness  
All known and nothing unknown

**Meditation on Prabhupāda in His Māyāpur Room**

One month in 1976  
February  
I was your secretary  
I came to you  
in this very room

That day  
I arrived, I ran  
to you  
with my impulsive poem  
jubilant, singing  
the glories of green  
golden Māyāpur

I didn’t know  
how to cook for you  
to massage you  
to prepare your correspondence  
to handle your affairs  
that bothered  
you, to relieve you  
that I didn’t know.

But you let me come  
to you, to be  
with you  
February, 1976  
in this holy, holy room.

O Prabhupāda!  
How gracefully,  
aristocratically  
you moved  
your ruling hands, rolling  
deftly your silver and  
hardwood cane, but more  
your godly eyes, piercing  
perfect words, in this  
holy, holy room.

Like God’s own  
omnipotent, serene  
decisions, conclusions,  
stately just, justice,  
spoken quite  
like God, by you,  
to the godly men  
and ladies, there  
in holy Bhārata land,  
in this very holy room.

How far has time  
taken me from you?  
O Prabhupāda!  
How very much I worshiped you,  
I loved you Prabhupāda,  
but heartless wheels,  
cold time’s gears,  
took me far from you.

Rudely rolling on,  
graceless age  
hardens the simple heart,  
and rolling farther  
breaks the heart it hardened,  
humbles grave conceit  
of one who came to you,  
to give his youthful love  
when it was soft, only to  
you, because you  
were everything to me  
in this tearful, holy room.

Amends, amends repent  
repent! Time, ironic  
to supply a precious  
moment to undo the  
darkening drift of time.

*Your insignificant, fallen servant*,  
Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**Desire Become One**

Hotter! O fire of desire.  
Reduce these mountains of sinful seeds to ashes.  
When will I offer my life on the altar of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire?

Jagadīśa Goswami

## Jagajīvana dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around.  
\- Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura

By estimates of mundane calculation, the termination of one living entity’s sojourn in this temporary body denotes a certain cessation of that particular individual’s pastimes or activities. In his pure state the living entity engaged in transcendental activity knows no such cessation. Being governed by the divine will - *daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ* - his activities may be visible or not on the mundane platform. To demand visibility as a prerequisite for surrender or service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the realm of neophyte.

”He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.” And as you said, “Everything is in my books.”

So it may be that some *sophisticated* and *scholarly* devotees think I am but a fanatical sentimentalist for identifying myself as one of “Prabhupāda’s men,” that I cannot understand the “oneness” of the *guru*; but you had your vision of how to spread and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, a special vision, a *successful* vision. I am a follower and a worshiper of that vision, that method. I am also a worshiper and follower of those who are worshipers and followers of the special vision. While others, being too much absorbed in philosophical meditations on the Absolute, were playing the Indian *choṭā mṛdaṅga* and “ringing the bells,” you were spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world - playing the *bṛhat* (big) *mṛdaṅga*, which has not ceased its sound till now.

Of course, I am a coarse fool, unsophisticated and not very intelligent, so it may be that it takes me this whole life to absorb a drop of the ocean of nectar in your books. Others, who are more advanced, who are *“beyond”* your books - maybe they can go on to *“higher”* topics, to *“higher”* authorities, but for me, the fallen, simple soul, I can just barely absorb *your* teachings in *your* books. Someone once said, “Special, divine power worked within him. So much success is otherwise not possible.” That “special, divine power” is still working through your instructions, through your books, through your representatives and followers. I beg to remain at their lotus feet, at your lotus feet, living with the followers who live by your divine instructions.

I remain,

*Your most fallen servant,*  
Jagajīvana dāsa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

*prabhupād-caraṇāśraya, śuddha-bhakti-bhāvodaya,*  
*praṇamāmi śaraṇa laye*  
*bhakta-goṣṭhi jāhār deho, sarva-jīva-āśraya geho,*  
*gaurāṅger pāś āmāro ni jaye*

**1. The divine lotus feet of His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda provide shelter for the conditioned souls of this material world. The shelter of his Lotus Feet is the transcendental cause of the rising of the all-blissful attitude of pure devotional service. I humbly offer my prostrate obeisances at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda and surrender my life in his service.**

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental body is the personified assembly of pure devotees (i.e., ISKCON, the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness). Thus, His Divine Grace has manifested a house to provide spiritual shelter for all of the conditioned souls in this material world. Kindly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, take us also with you to serve eternally the Golden Avatāra, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu wherever He or His movement are.

*kṛṣṇa-kathāmṛta-likhak, gaura-tattva-jagat-śikhak,*  
*kari tuār nitya-saṅger āśā.*  
*prabhupāda-patha bahire, kali-kaler māyā, bhai re!,*  
*uddhār pāibar na hi kono āśā.*

**2. His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the author of transcendental literatures glorifying the nectarean name, pastimes, and teachings of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. His Divine Grace is the spiritual master for the whole universe in the science of the teachings of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya. He is educating everyone in this transcendental science.**

My dear brothers! Outside of the path of devotional service enunciated by Śrīla Prabhupāda is the material illusion (*māyā*) of the present age of Kali. There is not even the slightest hope for deliverance (in this age of Kali) outside of the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness given by Śrīla Prabhupāda.

(NOTE: This could be sung to the tune of Śrī *Guru*-vandana by Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura)

*pracār-amṛta dilo je, guru-gaurāṅga-prāṇa se,*  
*kīrtane koraibe rādhā-dāsa*  
*prabhupāda-dibya-dṛṣṭi, saṁsāre gaura-prema-vṛṣṭi,*  
*hok prabhu! Tomar ājñā - chilo dāsa*

**3. Śrīla Prabhupāda has given the eternal nectar of preaching (the glories of the holy names, etc. of Śrī Kṛṣṇa). He is the transcendental life of** ***Guru*** **and Gaurāṅga, empowered by Their mercy. By performance of the** ***saṅkīrtana-yajña*** **He instates all living entities as confidential servitors of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī.**

His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda’s transcendental vision (i.e., his plan and program for establishing Lord Caitanya’s transcendental desire in this world) is causing torrents of rain in the form of Lord Caitanya’s mercy of pure love of Kṛṣṇa to flood the entire material creation.

O eternal master! Let me be ever the surrendered servant to your command!

*paścad-deśa śūnyavādī durācārī māyāvādī,*  
*uddhār pāilo keval tuār doye,*  
*prabhupāda doyā koro, kṛṣṇa-bhakta e-bār koro,*  
*tuār doyā - asambhav sambhav hoy, re!*

**4. The western world is filled with voidists, impersonalists and persons addicted to illicit activities against the Vedic principles of civilized human life. Although it is almost impossible to deliver such fallen souls, simply by your transcendental mercy even such souls have been delivered from material conditioned life.**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Kindly give your mercy to me! This time, (in this lifetime), give me the benediction that I may become a pure devotee of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. By your causeless mercy even the *impossible* can become *possible*.

*Offered by the insignificant servant of His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,*  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kirtanānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Another year of devotional service has passed, and by your grace there is still more devotional service to be performed. Indeed, it appears that the fields are more ready for harvest than at any time in the past. All over the world we hear the reports of our God brothers who are doing such wonderful service for you. Even in the most unlikely and difficult places your mission is being accomplished. We have personally seen how in Pakistan people are literally begging us, even in remote villages, to come and teach them about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In Africa they are lining up at our doors waiting to become your devotees. Everywhere conditioned souls are anxious to hear the good news of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

It appears that this great ocean of nectar can never be exhausted. The only lack is surrendered souls, preachers who will be field hands for your lordship. Although we feel exactly like the little bird who was trying to dry up the ocean by her insignificant pecking, we are also aware that it was that same insignificant effort that attracted Garuḍa, who was fully competent to actually do the job. Similarly, we can only hope that somehow or other we can please you and attract your divine blessings, and then all our attempts will be successful, for you are fully empowered to save the whole world.

Please continue to show us your mercy, that we may always be engaged in this service, birth after birth.

*Your insignificant son,*  
Kīrtanānanda Swami

## Mukunda Goswami

Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet.

Your kindness is infinite and beyond mere description. Even in the absence of your physical form, your kindness continues to reach out beyond all limits. Just as no one can imagine the boundless expansions of the *viśva-rūpa* form of Lord Kṛṣṇa, no one can measure the scope of your compassion.

During your sojourn on earth, you sometimes appeared to allow us to control you just as a loving father occasionally submits himself to the desires of his beloved child. We know from your words that you actually felt indebted to us even though you are indebted to no one but your dear Lord Kṛṣṇa and His disciplic succession.

We read in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Lord Nārāyaṇa’s statement to Ambarīṣa Mahārāja, “I am completely under the control of my devotee,” and we may cite a parallel in the unfathomable love you demonstrated when you sometimes acted as if you were controlled by one of your dear disciples. You always perfectly reciprocated according to our extent of surrender even though we may not have always realized this.

As time goes on and we work to fulfill your instructions all over the world seven years after your departure from this world, we feel an even greater response from you - a response which is in one sense even more rewarding than the blessings you gave us when you were here in your *vapuḥ* form. As we strive to carry out your eternal orders to raise millions of conditioned souls from the depths of illusion to the supreme platform of human perfection, we find you mercifully present every step of the way. Our successful endeavors are always rewarded by you, in your *vani* form. You never *really* left us at all!

Each word that emanated from your lotus mouth, every thought, every idea, contained volumes of meaning. An inspired inventor may in an instant conceive of a wonderful invention which then takes many years to reach its ultimate fulfillment. Similarly your every word was and is perfect inspiration. The millions of words you wrote and spoke have thus provided us with an unlimited number of infallibly conceived goals for us to achieve for your pleasure. As we work to fulfill your instructions our inspiration is that you will be pleased.

Your spotless purity is the driving force behind your gloriously successful mission. It is your purity that is constantly pushing ISKCON to greater heights and beyond all frontiers.

So merciful and so tangible is your continuing presence that we seek no other reward or recognition beyond what you are continuously giving us out of your causeless mercy. We need look no further than within our own hearts where you reside eternally as our everlasting guide and preceptor.

Mukunda Goswami

## Pañcadraviḍa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

At the dawn of a new year, I find myself fighting to protect your movement. This has made me contemplative, and I see this as being Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, so that I am able to meditate on the greatness of your ISKCON movement, your own branch of Lord Caitanya’s tree. The snake with the hot venomous teeth slithers in the cooling sandalwood tree, hoping to find relief from the fever that burns within. An envious person is like a serpent who will strike out at those who innocently walk nearby. But your movement is the path of the *paramahaṁsas*, and is for those who are *nirmatsarāṇāṁ param,* cent-percent pure at heart, non-envious beings. I pray to you that I may some day keep your ISKCON movement faithfully in my heart of hearts, and learn to love those who are most dear to you.

We walk in the company of great souls, my God brothers, and also our disciples. And they are all your descendants, members of your own family. You are such a great father. Although I am not worthy to live in this home of your *bhakti* movement, I will never go away, but promise you to qualify myself. That I feel is my greatest duty, to simultaneously qualify myself for the responsibilities entrusted to me and to work tirelessly to push on your ISKCON movement. My life is your satisfaction, and if there is anything which I have learned from all the years of your association, it is that real bliss exists in being able to please you.

Sometimes persons deluded by their own nefarious minds try to reform that which is under the protection of Lord Caitanya’s internal energy, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Although one may feel his intention to be noble, how can one criticize in the open forum what you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, fought to protect? And when those innocent followers have their faith dashed into the dust of the public arena, to be trampled underfoot by a hysterical mob, who can speak of good intentions? But our fate is more noble, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As Lord Caitanya cleaned the Guṇḍicā temple first with water and then his own tears, so I am also endeavoring to wash the temple of my own heart, in anticipation of receiving you within. Let he who has erred know that although it may be painful to have been wrong and difficult to have been seen so by others, in another sense it is a benediction when it helps one to purify himself. It is much worse to never acknowledge the errors that are within or not to try to redeem oneself by protecting your movement. To publicly prostrate oneself at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is never ignominy.

I am dismayed, as I am also a war-tested soldier, having fought for you before. And I am not afraid of the battle and know myself when the fighting is upon us. And I am certain of the final victory for your ISKCON movement, even were we to be bested initially. There is not even the slightest glimmer of doubt in my mind. And I am confident that your movement will shine triumphantly like the newly risen sun in Māyāpur. But my prayer in these moments is not for victory, because that is assured. That is not my concern. My intense desire is that I may never displease you, that I always remain very fixed upon your instructions, and always have the opportunity to serve you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the lord of my heart, and I will always remember you and will never leave you, who are eternally present in this ISKCON movement.

I prostrate myself in the dust of your lotus feet.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Pañcadraviḍa Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Śrīla Prabhupāda, before you came  
We’d never heard of Kṛṣṇa’s name,  
Our hearts were full of lust and greed,  
Yet you planted there the devotional seed.

As a mother loves her babies dear,  
You nurtured us with infinite care.  
With words of chastisement or pat on the back  
You taught us to keep on the Kṛṣṇa track.

You engaged us in spreading Mahāprabhu’s mission  
To deliver the world, your spiritual vision.  
Within such a short time so many came forward;  
With you at its head your ISKCON marched onward.

Throughout the world, so many centers,  
So many books in so many languages.  
At that age most people are thinking of dying,  
But you were preaching with the strength of a lion.

Vṛndāvana and Māyāpur, the holy dhāmas,  
Vaiṣṇava cities, your spiritual plans.  
Fulfill them we must, it’s your life’s goal,  
For service to you is real food for the soul.

”Books are basis, preaching the essence,  
Utility the principle, purity the force,  
In every village and every town,  
The holy name we must spread around.”

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you’ve gone  
To Goloka Vṛndāvana, your eternal home,  
You’re still here with us through your instruction,  
If we keep in our heart we will know no destruction.

Please give us the strength to do your work,  
In the face of adversity we shall not shirk.  
Wherever we go the glory will spread  
Of your movement ISKCON, with you at its head.

Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Prabhupāda-kṛpa Goswami
