**V** yāsadeva’s disciplic representative, books rewritten for all people of the earth,  
**Y** ajña for the intelligent his mercy performed, by those of lowest birth  
A vatāra of *bhakti-śakti,* love’s real face made manifest in a cruel age,  
S iddhānta Sarasvatī’s most powerful successor, with guise of humble, gentle sage.  
A stra of the holy name, an irresistible, devastating spiritual force,  
P olarization by sound vibration - defeating demons in due course.  
U nited nations the fortunate’s destiny, his grace delivered under the flag of Mahāprabhu,  
J ivas of the Kali-yuga - forever their debt to him is due.  
A cclaim, acclaim the name and fame of Śrīla Prabhupāda; be a miser not, cast in thy lot with his dearest of the Lord!

Prabhupāda-kṛpa Goswami

## Rāmeśvara Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You were sent to earth directly by Lord Kṛṣṇa to execute His mission. Thus, even among great Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas*, you are unique.

In the history of the Western countries, you are the first to come who had actually seen God, talked to Him, known Him intimately. Thus your message that all souls can directly see the Personality of Godhead enjoying His confidential pastimes while still in this world is unique in Western thought.

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu now rests on you. It will be built on your name, teachings, and pastimes. Preaching about you is as important as preaching on the other great topics of the *Bhāgavatam*. Broadcasting knowledge about you is now part of the essence of our mission. When people everywhere know about you, our mission will become successful.

Impersonalism and voidism is the essence of all modern thought. You came to earth to free the world of this darkness which has spread everywhere. Just as Kṛṣṇa appeared to remove the universal darkness caused by the birth of the demons Hiraṇyākṣa and Hiraṇyakaśipu, so you had to appear by Kṛṣṇa’s will to free mankind from this great darkness caused by the birth of many *asuras* in the guise of certain philosophers, scientists, politicians, wealthy mammonites, etc.

Perfectly pleasing Lord Caitanya depends on our perfectly surrendering to your mission. Through dedication to your movement, one blissfully renounces materialism and mundane sense gratification. This renunciation becomes the decoration of those who follow you seriously. Preaching the message of Kṛṣṇa and glorifying you to the world has become our life and soul, not something to plan for in the future, but to live each day, now! Printing and distributing your books, and books about you and your teachings, is the essence, not merely a luxury, or “just another way.” It is your *only* solace from your anxiety to save the world. It is you heart. Therefore, it is our life, our service and offering.

You are our master. We are your eternals servants, ready to go at once wherever you send us to carry out your every order. We pray for this position at your lotus feet, birth after birth.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Rāmeśvara Swami

## Rūpānuga dāsa

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I hardly knew your greatness. When I follow the line of your signature, I see the trace left by a visitor from another, spiritual world. When first I saw you, beneath that old tree, leading the chanting and speaking something I cannot recall, my first impression was this person has come here from another planet completely different from our own. My materialized mind and senses could only stare in amazement; my straining intelligence could not explain why I was attracted to you, who spoke a different language.

During those early lectures you sometimes said you were an old man, but I could plainly see that you were ageless. You were like a flower that always remained fresh and lovely. You frightened me when you said you could leave this world at any moment. I couldn’t understand why you might go, since you had just arrived.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, had I been more serious I could have served you better. I realized you were my real father who always knew me more than myself. When you spoke, it was to me, the spiritual being who sat before you in his embarrassing material form. As your son I wanted to please my new father and do something to make you proud of me. (I tried then, and I’m trying again).

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you appeared in my life when you gave me birth, my second, spiritual life, and you left when I was barely eleven spiritual years old, too immature to understand you. But now you come into my heart and give me inspirations, new chances to serve you. How merciful you are to let me meet you all over again! This time I will love you better. Now I am getting older, and I can appreciate my spiritual father. Now I will carry our your order like a good son.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. I thought I would write my homage this year in the form of a letter, just as I used to write to you my monthly reports while you were personally present.

I will not attempt to tell you much how ISKCON is going on all over the world, because my God brothers will be reporting to you about that from many different places and viewpoints. You once quoted the saying, “The sun never sets on the British empire,” and you said that it had also become true of your network of ISKCON temples around the world. What single devotee can know all of it? But as I see it, the worldwide ISKCON is healthy, growing, and fulfilling your wishes, despite the inevitable discrepancies. Yes, many disciples have left, but the remaining devotees are working together more maturely and recruiting many new grand disciples of yours. We are working in a unity that is strong and dependent on the basic premise that we must stay together in ISKCON if we are truly your sincere followers. Therefore, although Kali-yuga is worsening, I see ISKCON growing stronger.

For myself, I am trying to represent you especially in your ISKCON zone which the G.B.C. has assigned as my *prabhu-datta-deśa*. My God brothers and your grand disciples who are kindly working with me in your temples are very dedicated and hardworking, and although I am not a great leader or manager, we are always engaged in the attempt to maintain and improve your basic programs, such as book distribution, the spiritual life of the devotees in temples, recruiting, etc. And we are also seeing regular, improved results, despite concerted efforts by the demons against us. Surely this is all your mercy and Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.

Recently, while gathering research materials to write further about your life, I was overwhelmed by your many-sided achievements and your glorious personal attributes, and yet one result was that I became depressed when considering myself by comparison. You have all good qualities, and it seems I have no Vaiṣṇava qualities, except one saving grace - that I remain attached to your order and to writing about you. Please allow me the privilege to stay attached to you, somehow or other, since I have no other hope of redemption.

I pray to continue all my assigned duties with hopes of improvement by the gradual process of devotional service. I know it is only by your blessings and protection that I can keep up the steady program of dutiful service and not be affected by the powerful illusory energy.

What else shall I report? After all, it is supposed to be your Vyāsa-pūjā homage. In the Vyāsa-pūjā spirit, therefore, I enclose the following praises.

Through the śakti of twelve mahājanas  
I see the powers of that one  
who carried the mahājanas’ teachings  
to where they never went before -  
the savage Western worlds,  
where now they rule  
in Kṛṣṇa conscious temples  
and their teachings are widespread  
through the books of Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is the essence of the mahājanas -  
the sampradāya head;  
greatest ascetic;  
distributor of the Vedas;  
traveling as youthful boys;  
teaching the Saṅkhya system;  
bringing in Manu’s rule;  
protected by Nṛsiṁha-deva;  
exemplary in duty;  
teaching how the soul should pass away at death;  
giving all he has to Kṛṣṇa;  
making the Bhāgavata sweeter;  
and teaching the fools  
that there is a road of death.  
All these qualities and many more  
are found in jagad-guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
I wish to go on singing his glories  
and always serving among his men.

As always, I am looking forward to meeting you again.

*You eternal servant,*  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

**Equal Faith**

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**”Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

*Dear God brothers and God sisters, disciples, and guests*,  
Today we are celebrating Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, the appearance day of our beloved spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakāśarya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder*-ācārya* of the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. We are offering exalted worship to the lotus feet of our spiritual master in a way which is usually reserved only for God himself. The uninformed may question how we can elevate any person to such a high degree of veneration. Are we not diminishing the importance of God, who cannot be equaled by anyone, not even the greatest of demigods, what to speak of a human being?

In defense of our action we may cite from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān*  
*nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta*  
*sarva-devamayo guruḥ*

**”One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.”**

The Supreme Lord comes in the form of the spiritual master to deliver the conditioned souls. In worshiping the *ācārya* on an equal level with God we are not committing any offense, for we are worshiping the Lord in His mercy incarnation, Śrī *Guru*deva.

In the prayers of Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura it is declared:

*sākṣad-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhavyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**”The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord, because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and is followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari (Kṛṣṇa).”**

Religions in general have not arranged very satisfactorily for the equal worship of the spiritual master and God. Either they elevate the Lord’s pure devotee to a position of such prominence that God is entirely obscured, or, aversely, they neglect to give him proper importance at all. Even in our own Gauḍīya tradition at present, there are those who foolishly suggest it is excessive to offer *guru-pūjā* on a daily basis. But scriptural evidence does not indicate that worshiping the spiritual master daily is an extravagance. Rather worship of the *ācārya* is inseparable from worshiping the Lord. In fact we must worship the Lord and His pure devotee at every moment in our lives.

Those who elevate the representative of God to a status higher than God Himself become fanatical cultists. In so doing they disregard the most essential fact, namely that no one is equal to or greater than God. The spiritual master is actually part of the Absolute Truth, and is therefore known as *guru-tattva*. God is the supreme standard or purity, mercy, truthfulness, and all other qualities. The bona fide spiritual master as His representative must embody all of these standards qualitatively, though quantitatively to a lesser degree. Without a full understanding of God’s supremacy, foolish persons may accept an imposter *guru* and thus cheat themselves of the benefits of perfect association.

No less fortunate are those who minimize the importance of the bona fide spiritual master. Their minimization of his worship creates hardheartedness and arrogance. By neglecting to duly worship the *ācārya*, they enviously drive him from their lives and thus fall into the ultimate snare of impersonalism, imagining that they have a direct link with God, and finally that they are God. Such offences against the pure devotees are never tolerated by the Lord.

The spiritual master, as the unalloyed pure devotee servant of God, is always held in intimate embrace by the Lord. The Lord accepts the pure devotee spiritual master as an equal, allowing him to engage in His loving pastimes free from any awe and reverence which would create superior or inferior roles. These transcendental pastimes being enacted with Kṛṣṇa in His supreme abode show us that the Lord enjoys His position of equality with His loving associates. Thus Kṛṣṇa wrestles with His cowherd friends, and when defeated, carries them upon His shoulders. As a submissive child He depends upon Mother Yaśodā’s breast milk for His sustenance, and cries in fear when she threatens to chastise Him for His naughtiness. In the conjugal *rasa*, the Lord enjoys playing as a henpecked husband and frankly admits His inability to repay the *gopīs’* unequaled love. In fact, Kṛṣṇa’s appearance as Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to personally taste pure devotional service testifies to the exalted nature of the servant of God.

When staying within this world, the *ācārya* is enabled to perform extraordinary activities, being empowered by God. In the case of our beloved spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājak*ācārya* Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, these activities were magnified to such a degree as to clearly show him to be an incarnation of God, *śaktyāveśa-avatāra*.

In the span of only fifteen years, Śrīla Prabhupāda compiled the translation of all the essential Vedic texts into English and synthesized all of the important *ācārya’s* commentaries, thus providing the international spiritual law books to guide remaining human era of the Kali-yuga. This feat was no less wonderful than that performed by the Lord Himself when, wishing to insure the redemption of the fallen souls in the Kali-yuga, He incarnated as Vyāsadeva to perform the work of compiling the Vedic literatures. Prabhupāda also simultaneously organized and inspired translation of his work into all other major languages of the world. Then, as the commander-in-chief of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, he commenced the massive distribution of these transcendental books at such a furious rate that in America, the capital of materialism, within hardly fifteen years twenty-five percent of the population have given up cow slaughtering and forty percent have accepted the principal of reincarnation.

The Boar incarnation swooped down into the muddy mire and lifted the fallen Earth planet upon His tusks, returning the Earth to its orbit. His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda performed no less a Herculean task by dipping down into the murky cesspools of the Kali-yuga to uplift us from the God-forgotten depths to which we had fallen. Varāhadeva battled Hiraṇyakśa; Śrīla Prabhupāda battled scientists, impersonalists, demoniac politicians, *sahajiyās*, caste *brāhmaṇas,* welfare do-gooders, and sense gratifiers of all types. In shooting his arrows of truth, his prowess was no less than that of Bali Mahārāja, as he smashed the illusory phantasmagoria of *māyā’s* agents.

In Dvārakā, Kṛṣṇa provided palaces for 16,000 queens. Similarly Prabhupāda began construction and ordered the completion of unlimited palaces all over the world for Kṛṣṇa and His eternal consort Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Who can say in the centuries to come what the total number of such palatial temples will come to?

Kṛṣṇa’s personality is unique in that it combines apparently opposite qualities which are not usually present within the same one person. Therefore Lord Kṛṣṇa is sometimes *dhīrodātta, dhīra-lalita, dhira-praśānta* and *dhīroddhata*. The same uniqueness is observed in remembrance of our spiritual master’s wonderful personality. As *dhīrodātta* he was naturally grave, gentle, forgiving, merciful, determined, humble, highly qualified, chivalrous and bodily attractive. Lord Kṛṣṇa especially exhibited such qualities in regard to the incident of lifting Govardana Hill and in His later dealings with King Indra. The same characteristic features were displayed by His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda in the incident of battling for Hare Krishna Land at Juhu. He maintained a grave, determined, chivalrous spirit throughout the two-year battle until the land was at last secured. In the end, in the mood of forgiveness and mercy he pardoned the offenders who had inconvinced Rādhā-Rāsavihārī so greatly.

A person who is very envious, proud, easily angered, restless and complacent is called *dhīroddhata*. For example when Kṛṣṇa challenged the demon Kalayāvana, Ha called him a sinful frog who should find a dark well to hide in because there was a black snake named Kṛṣṇa who was very eager to devour him. Such a boastful, proud mentality is characteristic of *dhīroddhata* persons. Although ordinarily such qualities might be considered demeaning, since they were used by Kṛṣṇa to protect His devotees they are considered exalted traits. In much the same mood, Śrīla Prabhupāda often challenged the material scientists that he would kick on their faces with his boots by exposing their bluff that life comes from matter. In debating with his disciples, some of whom were actually qualified scientists, he would at times become greatly angered at their cheating, post-dated checks, promising to create life from atoms. Though stereotyped spiritualists might try to fault Śrīla Prabhupāda for his passionate indignation, the devotees would stand by and cheer as their spiritual master challenged big Nobel laureates to produce from their laboratories even a small egg as any ordinary chicken could do.

Persons who are naturally very funny, always in full youthfulness, expert in joking and free from all anxiety are called *dhīra-lalita*. And that person who is very peaceful, forbearing, considerate and obliging is called *dhīra-praśānta*. Such features were often visible in the personality of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Those who were fortunate to associate intimately with him got the opportunity to observe all of these apparently contradictory features in his divine personality. Śrīla Prabhupāda was not of this world, but was a resident of Goloka Vṛndāvana and as such had all of the pleasing varieties of nature found in the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By associating with His Divine Grace, one experienced the transcendental pleasure of living within the spiritual energy of the Lord with a most intimate associate of the Supreme Lord. *“Mahātmānas tu māṁ pārtha daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ.”*

Śrīla Prabhupāda in speaking of his own realization once said, “What do I know of Lord Kṛṣṇa? I simply know His Divine Grace, my spiritual master.” We understand that within this statement lies a great unspoken truth; that to perfectly know one’s spiritual master means that Kṛṣṇa has also become known. For the disciple, the spiritual master represents Kṛṣṇa in all respects and by his transparent *via mediacy*, connects the devotee to the Supreme Lord. Therefore on this most auspicious of all holy days, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, I bow down at the lotus feet of my spiritual master and pray that he eternally allows me to hear, praise, and remember his unparalleled glories under all circumstances, at all times, wherever he may situate me in his devotional service.

Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Vāsudeva dāsa

Please accept our obeisances at your lotus feet again and again

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to me this one time. You are the savior of the fallen; without you there is no one. Just by being in your presence, sins go far away. Where can anyone find such kindness? By the touch of the waters of the Ganges one becomes liberated, even if he is the lowest of mankind, but just by seeing you, the same effect is achieved. Such is your quality. If one commits an offense at the feet of Lord Hari, he can be forgiven if he chants the holy name. But if one offends you, there is no salvation for him.

Please, when will that day be mine when my offenses end and a taste for the pure holy name is infused within my heart?

Your humble servant,  
Vāsudeva dāsa

## Vyāsa Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers around the world.

## Accra, Ghana

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
when thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
to spread the holy name around.

*nāma-śreṣṭhaṁ manum api śacī-putram atra svarūpaṁ*  
*rūpaṁ tasyāgrajam uru-purīṁ māthurīṁ goṣṭhavātīm*  
*rādhā-kuṇḍaṁ giri-varam aho rādhikā-mādhavāśāṁ*  
*prāpto yasya prathita-kṛpayā śrī-guruṁ taṁ nato ‘smi*

To our eternal father, grandfather, protector, spiritual master, and grand spiritual master,

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You have given us yourself, your words of kindness and instruction:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are no ordinary man, but the sum total of the demigods:  
How many of us have accepted?

Your body is completely spiritual being totally dedicated in the service of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and the disciplic succession:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are giving the dust of your lotus feet and the opportunity to turn our dead bodies into living ones \[*Bhāg*. 2.3.33\]:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are giving us the chance to become the most obedient servants of the servants of the Lord:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are giving us the six Gosvāmīs, their prayers, teachings and realizations:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are giving us the original spiritual master, Lord Nityānanda, Balarāma, the Personality of Servitor Godhead, simultaneously one and different:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are giving us the knowledge of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal love in the form of the beloved son of Śrīmatī Sacidevī:  
How many of us have accepted?

You are giving us pure unalloyed, unmotivated, uninterrupted loving devotional service, which in personified form is Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī:  
How many of us have accepted?

You have given us Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, which gives further realization of Kṛṣṇa’s form, qualities, and pastimes:  
How many of us have accepted?

In your mercy you have given us everything, but some of us have not accepted... our hands have been closed.

We pray on this, your appearance day, that we may be given your mercy and the intelligence to open our eyes, ears, and hands and accept the gifts which you are so kindly giving to us, and we pray that we may also, by your mercy, be able to give these gifts to others.

Even if the results of our service efforts may not be so materially impressing, may our devotion to your ISKCON be that of the spider to Lord Ram.

*We beg to remain your eternal servants,*

**ACCRA, GHANA**

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble and worthless obeisances at your lotus feet.

Every year at this time when compiling a Vyāsa-pūjā homage for your pleasure, I get out previous Vyāsa-pūjā books and go through them. Looking through, I try to find inspiration by reading offerings by other devotees.

This year I am doing the same thing, sitting here and appreciating al the amazing statements and sentiments being expressed, all of them glorifying you, your achievements, your qualities, your pastimes, etc.

How wonderful, how reassuring to see that you are being appreciated by so many nice devotees from all over the world. And so you should be glorified, not by just a few, but by the whole world. You are the savior of the whole world.”

It is the duty, and in fact a natural thing, for every disciple to glorify his spiritual master, because the spiritual master is giving the greatest gift: freedom from the repetition of birth and death and awakening of our normal, healthy, peaceful condition of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore small wonder that your Vyāsa-pūjā book is full of so many appreciations!

How can anyone ever pay back the debt he owes to you, even after millions of lifetimes? But it is still the duty of the disciple to endeavor to please you. Hence we give you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our life and soul, and every year at this time we approach you and say, in so many different ways, thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you so much for giving us new life and for giving us your service.

*Your disciples and grand disciples of ISKCON Adelaide, Australia,*

## Ahmedabad

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which are worshipable even by the greatest demigods like Brahmā, Śīva, and Indra. Your glories are sung daily in the assembly of demigods and sages, who are waiting to take birth in your movement.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said that an *uttama-adhikārī* Vaiṣṇava is recognized by his ability to convert non-devotees into devotees. It is certainly not just mere scholarship, or renounced order of life, or austerity that one can make devotees. One requires unbounded, unfathomable compassion for those fallen souls whose hearts have become so dirty that no saintly persons would even touch them. “Preaching is the essence, purity is the force, utility is the principle, and books are the basis.” This is the summary of your transcendental personality.

Your Divine Grace remained equipoised in all circumstances. Your program was so simple wherever you went: you would translate for six to eight hours daily, thus absorbing yourself in the nectar of *kṛṣṇa-kathā.* Then you would greet the Deities and give a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, take a little breakfast, and dictate letters, or discuss briefly general managerial problems within your movement. Then you would sometimes be seen reading your books, or chanting on beads. At around 11 a.m., you would take massage, and while taking massage also you would be constantly preaching in a light humorous mood. Certainly to witness you in all your daily activities is such a priceless privilege of your blessed servants.

You would take bath and then honor prasādam, always alone in a very meditative mood. Then you would take a short nap and be ready again to preach. From 4 p.m. to 9 p.m. you would meet all kinds of people: press reporters, priests, professors, police officers, parents of devotees - everyone would have profuse opportunity to have your darśana, to actually hear you, to see you laughing, sometimes chastising like an affectionate father, sometimes begging, and other times ordering. You were always seen to be above all problems. Nothing deterred you or bothered you. Like Lord Viṣṇu, who maintains the universe and who resides in every atom, yet who is completely aloof from all His creations, you created a worldwide society of devotees, and you maintained it by your instructions and by your example, and yet always found time to meet any guests, devotees, and friends who wanted to meet you. You constantly preached, and when necessary you showed by your example and made preachers, managers, accountants, cooks. You instructed us on the minutest details of civilized human life, and what to speak of the topmost confidential spiritual truth about the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa that you revealed to the most wretched and fallen people of the world.

All this became possible because Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu selected you to bestow this mercy. Such power is not by accident or by chance, but by lifelong determination and perseverance to preach the message of Kṛṣṇa. Yes, you received the power of God as Lord Śiva received mother Ganges on his head. Only a very powerful person such as Śiva can receive such powers, so anyone who tries to minimize or take lightly such achievements of a Vaiṣṇava certainly becomes condemned by the *ācārya-paramparā.* On the other hand, one who truly appreciates and glorifies such an exalted Vaiṣṇava as you, becomes liberated himself.

All your activities, every word that emanated from your lotus mouth, every kīrtana that you led, and every song you sang were all totally divine. We just cannot imagine our fortune to have witnessed you, an angel from Goloka, an apostle of love, compassion, purity, determination, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Even though you remained on the earth for 82 years, and every second of your life was an example of an eternally liberated associate of Godhead, you manifested to us your full opulence of *jagad-guru* only for 12 short years, from 1966 to 1977, a very, very short period indeed, and we witnessed you during this short period. This is our only good fortune. Just the memory of your divine activities and our witnessing it gives us a solace that at least we saw a cent percent perfect living entity.

Now as we proceed on the most difficult path of spiritual perfection of attaining pure love of Kṛṣṇa, with all our weaknesses and imperfections, we beg at your feet to please let us remain at your feet life after life; to please let us always remember our eternal positions as your servitors; and please let us have full love and affection for all those who accept you as the one and only savior, the one and only master, the one and only friend of their lives.

*Begging to remain at your feet, the devotees of ISKCON Gujarat*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Yaśomatīnandana dāsa / Vasu Ghoṣa dāsa / Kṛṣṇanāma dāsa / Rādhākuṇḍa dāsī

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda and present ISKCON ācāryas**

## Alachua

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All year I’ve been composing a Vyāsa-pūjā offering for you in my mind, but now that it’s time to send it, I’m ashamed to find myself sadly lacking in every way; I can’t even write a proper glorification for you. As your disciple, I am supposed to do everything for your pleasure, but I’m sorry to say I’ve never yet done anything to please you. Much to your credit, however, you’ve very patiently waited for me, helping, guiding, and protecting me every step of the way. You are my eternal father, and you love me the way only a pure devotee could. Prabhupāda, you know my heart - how I want to serve you, to do something wonderful for you. Please give me the strength, free me from my offenses and enviousness, keep me - my only life is serving you.

*Your loving disciples at New Ramaṇa-retī,*

## Almviks Gard

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**”I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Your undeviating desire to carry out the order of your spiritual master was the basis of your every action. Therefore, everything you did was eternally significant and transcendentally potent. The way you left everything and came to America, how you attracted the fallen souls and engaged us in hearing and chanting - all of this speaks of your purity. How you risked everything to give us unqualified followers a chance. You are surely our ever well-wisher. Won’t you kindly be pleased upon us?Toward the end of your manifest pastimes on this earth, you were on your way to the Gītā-nāgarī, Pennsylvania, farm, where you wanted to establish *varṇāśrama* farm communities. When you were confident that the books were there and their importance understood (at least to some extent) and their distribution going on profusely (or at least sufficiently), you turned your lotus face to farm communities, where Kṛṣṇa’s social system was in seed form :the *brāhmaṇas* as the head, the *kṣatriyas* as the arms, the *vaiśyas* as the belly, and the *śūdras* as the legs.

*śamo damas tapaḥ śaucaṁ*  
*kṣāntir ārjavam eva ca*  
*jñānaṁ vijñānam āstikyaṁ*  
*brahma-karma svabhāva-jam*

*śauryaṁ tejo dhṛtir dākṣyaṁ*  
*yuddhe cāpy apalāyanam*  
*dānam īśvara-bhāvaṣ ca*  
*kśātraṁ karma svabhāva-jam*

*kṛṣi-go-rakṣya-vānijyaṁ*  
*vaiśsya-karma svabhāva-jam*  
*paricaryātmakaṁ karma*  
*śūdrasyāpi svabhāva-jam*

*sve sve karmany abhirataḥ*  
*saṁsiddhiṁ labhate naraḥ*  
*sva-karma-nirataḥ siddhiṁ*  
*yathā vindati tac chṛnu*

**”Peacefulness, self-control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, knowledge, wisdom, and religiousness - these are the natural qualities by which the** ***brāhmaṇas*** **work.**  
**”Heroism, power, determination, resourcefulness, courage in battle, generosity, and leadership, are the natural qualities of work for the** ***kṣatriyas*****.**  
**”Farming, cow protection, and business are the natural work for the** ***vaiśyas,*** **and for the** ***śūdras*** **there is labor and service to others.**  
**”By following his qualities of work, every man can become perfect. Now please hear from Me how this can be done.” (Bg. 18.42-45)**

Everyone should work according to that nature he has acquired to satisfy Kṛṣṇa. How frustrated we have become by speculating so many “isms,” only to create an imaginary *nirviśeṣa* for sense gratification. To divide society according to natural divisions is the way to organize a peaceful, prosperous society. Without a brain in good order, one is crazy. Therefore there must be a brahminical class of teachers, who with their “high-sounding words” can kill the demoniac mentality of the impersonalists who claim that amorphous oneness is the shelter we seek. These *brāhmaṇas* worship the transcendental form of the Lord in the temple, and they train up the future generation to do so in the *Varṇāśrama*-college. Similarly, someone has to administer these instructions. Since the living entity is part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa, everyone has a duty to perform. But who spontaneously performs his duty without guidelines? Mostly, people will be *gṛhasthas* and will engage in producing necessities and assisting, since by nature the people are fruitive in their activities.

In this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given everyone a way to make a contribution to society and be happily engaged according to his own nature. ISKCON needs all four services, and although devotees are above classification, for the purpose of material management division is there. Candidates for becoming devotees can advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness by accepting a favorable position. Thus we follow in your footsteps. You are above all designations and material modes, as you prayed on the *Jaladuta* at Boston Commonwealth Pier in September of 1965:

”My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, you are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do no know why you have brought me here. Now you can do whatever you like with me... Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as you like... O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands, so if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as you like.”

In conclusion, we beg the benediction of Your Divine Grace that we may clearly hear your instruction as to our duty, so that we may purify ourselves and render favorable service unto your ISKCON society under the direction of your G.B.C. representative Śrī Viṣṇupāda.

*The most insignificant servants of your servant from New Candrodaya Kṣetra (Almvik),*

## Ann Arbor

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
On the most auspicious occasion of your appearance day, we prostrate ourselves before your divine lotus feet and beg to offer you a few insignificant but sincere words of glorification and gratitude.

*kaler doṣa-nidhe rājann*  
*asti hy eko mahān guṇaḥ*  
*kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya*  
*mukta-bandhaḥ paraṁ vrajet*

**”My dear King, although Kali-yuga is full of faults, there is still one good quality about this age. It is that simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra*****, one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom.” (*****Bhāg.*** **12.3.51)**

This planet earth has never known a greater well-wisher than yourself, for by your guiding the conditioned souls to take shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, more and more the earth is being relieved from the heavy burden of sinful activities. Just as the full moon causes the tide of the great oceans to recede, similarly, as the pure devotee representative of Lord Caitanya, your books are causing the ocean of Kali to retreat. Therefore, you are also the greatest chastiser.

It is the most wonderful thing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how you descended into the foul depths of the most sinful and materialistic culture of America to save us unfortunate children of Kali-yuga. We were so busy being kicked by *māyā,* drinking her fatal potion of sense gratification - the walking dead. By your unbounded kindness and compassion, you took hold of our hands, leading us out of the darkest forest of ignorance. Giving us new life and enlightening us with the transcendental message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, you opened our eyes to the road back home, back to Godhead.

We had no qualifications to receive your mercy. But according to śāstra, that is the qualification for receiving Lord Gaurāṅga’s mercy in the age of quarrel, because He is *patita-pāvana,* the deliverer of the most wretched. So although we were thoroughly addicted to the four pillars of sinful life, you distributed the medicine to cure our spiritual disease: *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam, hari-nāma*, Vaikuṇṭha temples to live in, beautiful *āratis* and Deity worship. You thus demonstrated practically how Śrī Kṛṣṇa is “the higher taste,” and you made us all dance and chant like jubilant peacocks.

Dearest friend and master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you are no longer physically present, the instructions you left in your books and implanted in our hearts are keeping us strong and healthy in spiritual life. Certainly there was no greater loss in this world than when you departed, especially during these darkest hours of crisis. However, you predicted that you ISKCON would go down in history as the movement which would save the situation. Therefore, on this special day, we are humbly begging you to empower us more and more to see your mission through and to build a house in which the whole world can live! Jai Jagat *Guru* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*You most insignificant servants,*

Daśaratha dāsa / Apūrva dāsa / Daṇḍī dāsa / Bhakta Steve / Kamalinī-devī dāsī / Duḥśalā-devī dāsī / Lakṣmī-devī dāsī / Pañca-tattva dāsī

## Argentina

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Again a year has passed, and we find ourselves meditating on you, writing our Vyāsa-pūjā offering. This year your movement has taken a very auspicious turn in this country. After years of prohibition and “underground preaching” we are again allowed to come out in the open and even to print your books.

Who can stop your plan? It is only due to your pure desire to serve your *Guru* Mahārāja that this movement is ever increasing and cannot be checked by anyone. Temporarily there might be some obstacles by māyā, but ultimately she cannot remain before your powerful preaching. You are so great, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are the seed of all the wonderful devotees and amazing projects which unfold before our eyes and that will still unfold in the future. It is inconceivable how many conditioned souls have become affected, and still will, by your preaching. You have given this world new hope. If it wasn’t for you, it would be lost. By meditating on your transcendental līlā we become enlivened and confident that this Kṛṣṇa-consciousness culture will spread all over this planet in due course of time. If we only follow as you have done and show our love to you by how much we cooperate with each other, success will be guaranteed.

Although you have disappeared from our sight, you are still guiding us. You have left us perfectly protected with your books, your ISKCON society, and your senior disciples, the GBC. Please let us never be so foolish as to think that these things are different from you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this occasion of your appearance in this world we stand before you like beggars. Please let us forever continue taking part in your plan in whatever capacity. Never let us become neglectful in fulfilling your instructions. Otherwise our life has no meaning.

Your humble servants,

**BUENOS AIRES**

## Athens

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this glorious day of your appearance. Your mercy is all that we are made of, and your instructions are the guiding beacons in this dark age of Kali.

From you we learn everything, and because your life was glorious, ours can also become glorious by following you.

Before we met you we knew nothing. We had no idea of what to think or what not to think, what to do and what not to do. We knew not from whence we came nor to where we were heading, and indeed, who we even were! We didn’t even know how to wash and what to eat! We were marching full speed into the slaughterhouse of materialistic life, with its snares and traps of society, friendship, and love. On the road to hell, while busily running after our dreams, by some great fortune we met Your Divine Grace, and you warned us of the dangers that lay ahead. Indeed, you reset our course on the proper path.

So Kṛṣṇa has sent you to submerge us and train us in real life. Like a mother, you are carefully and patiently nurturing us from inability to full spiritual maturity. You tolerate our nonsense like the mother tolerates the child’s persistent crying, its inability to do anything right, its ungratefulness, and its tendency to mess up even a clean and tidy place.

Despite the fact that you are no longer physically present, you are still giving us directions through your instructions that you have kindly left behind.

You have taught us to give up our *kṛpana* mentality and think big for Kṛṣṇa. We have to take over not just a country but the planet. Cāṇakya Paṇḍita tells us that even the whole world can be given up to save our souls. So if on one Vyāsa-pūjā day we can offer you the whole world, it would be a little gift in comparison to what you have given us.

Thank you for giving us the opportunity to partake in the great mission of Lord Caitanya. As you have desired great things for Kṛṣṇa, so we should also desire great things for the Lord’s pleasure. And as your desires were pure, Kṛṣṇa is still eagerly fulfilling them, even though you have left this world. So if we make our desires pure, we may also have something great to offer you.

Your instructions have to be taken seriously, and your warnings carry just as much weight. You warned us that nothing can stop this movement but internal quarreling. You in fact told us that we will prove our love for you in the manner we cooperate together. Those who claim to be your true disciples will heed this warning and not fail to cooperate; otherwise they are guilty of tearing down your lifetime’s work.

Your movement is the only army which can defeat *māyā,* whose task of conquering the world and the hearts of all living entities appears to be almost complete. Sometimes we see that when there is a common enemy, different factions unite and are ready to overlook small differences for the benefit of attaining a higher purpose. Similarly, we have to fight, never, as you have warned us, to underestimate our common enemy.

Sometimes devotees may be perfect in behavior but they may not preach, and sometimes they preach but are not ideal in character. But in your case your words are backed by your actions and your life was perfect in every way. So we consider ourselves fortunate to have met you by the will of providence.

Please help us to fully appreciate our good fortune and not let it pass us by.

*You lowly but otherwise fortunate servants*,

Gokulānanda dāsa / Citraka dāsa / Nandagopa dāsa / Ātmarati-devī dāsī / Bhaktibhāva-devī dāsī / Lalitāpriya-devī dāsī / Prema-bhakti-devī dāsī

## Atlanta

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Certainly each and every one of your disciples has understood, possibly more in recent years than during you physical presence, that your appearance on this auspicious day blessed the whole earthy planet, connecting its population with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You are the fulfiller of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s desire that the holy name be spread in every town and village of the world.

After your disappearance, many of your disciples feel themselves thrown into the ocean of lamentation. Not only are we feeling your separation, but we are also so much bound up by the vicissitudes of material nature that without your constant personal reminder of what Kṛṣṇa consciousness is, we are only staggering forward, and we are so much sunk into the bodily concept of life that remembering your instructions in the form of sound vibration (*vāṇī*) proves nearly impossible to us. We tax our empty brain (you often used to say that some people’s skulls are filled with cow dung) with the question “What did Śrīla Prabhupāda really want?” All the many thousands of tapes and volumes of books seem to render little help. “We don’t study that much,” our minds say, “and after all, the person of the spiritual master is necessary to explain scripture.” *Yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau:* “Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.23)

As during your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have to study your personal example, like a young pupil, who, unable to see through the teacher’s plan, blindly copies whatever little step he writes on the black board. How did you take to heart the *vāṇī* of *your* spiritual master? How did *you* understand *his* instructions?

Among the many pieces of literature you left behind, we find some outstanding poems through which you communicate your deep realizations. In 1959, you published your “Viraha-aṣṭāṣṭakam.” Two decades after Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s disappearance, you prayed: “If only he could come back, the conditioned souls could all be delivered. If at this time he were to come again and again preach all these things, again the whole world would be set into order.” Thus you expressed your feelings of separation for your spiritual master.

Eventually, however, we can see that it was *you* who were able to execute the mission of your *Guru* Mahārāja, who ordered you, “Preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English-speaking countries. That will be the best for you as well as for everybody on this planet.” You yourself set things straight again, and even your God brothers had to admit that you are the actual successor of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja in the disciplic succession.

What do you teach about the spiritual master? In the “Aṣṭāṣṭakam,” you pray to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, “Anyone who accepts the holy name from you is qualified. If everyone who has attained this qualification goes and makes disciples, then the suffering conditioned souls in the material world will all be delivered.”

Six years later, you chanted the holy name on Tompkin’s Square Park, New York. *Tṛṇād api sunīcena*... thinking yourself lower than the grass and more tolerant than the tree, thus attracting the suffering conditioned souls through the pure sound of the holy name. A little while after that, you ordered your closest disciples to form a G.B.C. for the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness and continue the tradition of the disciplic succession.

Many others have misunderstood and are still misunderstanding the term *guru,* lacking due humility, and thus the puny pocketsize *gurus* and streamlined *swamis* stream forth, both in the past as also in the present, and introduce anarchy into the worshipable *guru-paramparā* that leads back to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. However, things are not as easy as they seem, and often the misguided imitator finds himself fallen out of the clouds of his own imagination, into much harsher reality. He commits some serious offense not only in the eyes of scripture, but many times also against State laws, and finds himself in the prison of material nature—*maithunyāgāra*, the shackles of sex life—or even in a State prison, on a diet of water and bread.

Still, the instruction stands, given by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to each and every member of His *saṅkīrtana* movement:

*yāra dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru haña tāra’ ei deśa*

**”Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. In this way become a spiritual master (*****guru*****) and try to liberate everyone in this land.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **7.130)**

*Guru* - a significant word. All too easily the mushrooms of false religion sprout from our brains, well manured with cow dung. For guidance and clarification, again let us look at what *you* write on the blackboard, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.21.28-29, you explain, “The word *guru* refers to one who gives proper direction under the authority of the Vedic injunctions and according to the lives of great personalities.” And, in your Bhaktivedanta purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.15.16, you define very mercifully, “Everyone is in the darkness of ignorance. Therefore everyone needs to be enlightened with transcendental knowledge. One who enlightens his disciple and saves him from rotting in the darkness of ignorance in this material world is a true *guru*.”

We pray for your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our true spiritual master and *guru!* Let us all become your qualified disciples, by understanding scripture as well as your own exalted example. Let us give up false ego, and let us take your mission to heart to spread Śrī Caitanya’s mercy everywhere!

What can we offer you on this auspicious day of your appearance? Rather, we beg you: Please keep praying to the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa to give us intelligence, so that one day we may understand your desire and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every living being on this planet, thereby becoming your true servants.

*Your disciples and grand disciples at New Śrī Pānihāṭi Dhāma,*

## Auckland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You came to the West with a matchless gift  
The influence of māyā you did lift  
From the hearts of so many fallen souls,  
Who had no knowledge, no aims, no goals.  
With determination and courage rare  
You came with qualities beyond compare,  
The fortune you brought with you knows no bounds;  
You’ve filled the world with ecstatic sounds.  
Your smile has lit up the universe  
Annihilating the material curse,  
You’ve awakened, enthused, and enlivened me,  
And given me ability to really see.  
Bliss we experience at the sight of your face,  
You, savior of the human race.  
Throughout the universe is spread your fame,  
As we dance and chant Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.  
It is actually not possible to sing your praise,  
Even if I talk for the rest of my days.  
Your glories are limitless and unperceivable,  
Your love for God is inconceivable,  
I hope your servants I can always serve.  
Mercy which I do not deserve.  
The great souls who surrender completely to you -  
Please may I learn to be like them too?  
Thinking about your love so sweet,  
I beg to remain at your lotus feet.  
My prayer is that when I breath my last breath,  
You will be my shelter at the time of death.  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories unto you,  
Beloved of Kṛṣṇa and Caitanya too!  
I can never repay you the debt that I owe,  
So wherever you want, I will gladly go.  
Dear Gurudeva, I humbly pray,  
While meditating on this auspicious day,  
To fully understand who you really are,  
And to learn to love you with all my heart.

*Your disciples and grand disciples, the servants of Rādhā-Giridhārī,*

## Baltimore

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Another year has passed, and yet  
We have not thought to come to you.  
Please help us advance in the understanding  
That everything we are depends upon you.  
Without your glance we are doomed to failure.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, our desire to serve you  
Increases every year, but still  
The pain of separation has not diminished.  
Some say you are gone, it’s different, worse.  
We feel you are always here, it’s the same, better.  
By entering deeper and deeper  
Within our hearts  
We realize that everything must be offered to you.

We don’t want to hold back,  
But māyā - the power of illusion -  
Pins us to the ground,  
Trying to make us forget you  
And just serve our merciless minds.  
Hopelessly bound, we cry for release,  
But no one responds.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, please lift us up,  
Make us walk behind you,  
As proper servants should.  
Don’t forsake us for our lack of surrender.  
We really want to follow you  
And do as you have done;  
Give body, mind, and words to Kṛṣṇa  
As offerings in the saṅkīrtana-yajña.

Now we are thinking rightly,  
For by this enthusiastic preaching spirit,  
We are sure to come to you.

*Your fallen disciples,*

## Bangkok

**He Built a House in Which the Whole World Can Live**

A lifetime in preparation, and then you began the struggle alone.  
You came up against seemingly impossible odds  
As you strived to preach to those who were less than cats and dogs.  
But your faith and determination could never be shaken,  
For you carried the greatest gift,  
And thus you built a house in which the whole world can live.

To so many lost souls overexposed to material life,  
You had planted the seed,  
The seed of Bhakti, love of Kṛṣṇa.  
There is no more glorious deed,  
And as you enthused us in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness,  
You also inspired us to give  
Free access to this house in which the whole world could live.

An astrologer once foretold that you would emerge  
As a leader in the world.  
And Caitanya Mahāprabhu predicted,  
”In every town and village My name will be heard.”  
Thus India’s greatest spiritual exponent came to give the name in a way  
that no other did,  
By building a house in which the whole world could live.

India’s greatest folly, to forget Kṛṣṇa in the face of rampant material desires,  
And America’s, to plead that ignorance is bliss.  
So East has become lame and West is blind,  
Then let there be a temple so the two may combine.”  
Now by your divine will, there are so many farms, schools, and temples,  
living manifestations of your bold spiritual vision.  
With Kṛṣṇa at the center it’s the perfect structure,  
A house in which we all can live! - peacefully.

*Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda*,  
Since you are so perfect in every respect, it would be useless for us to try to think we could ever properly glorify you or fully appreciate your magnanimous character by our mere play of words. You are not of this world, and the fact that you have come to extricate us from this seething ocean of material miseries in the form of birth, death, old age, and disease, by giving us this priceless, unforgettable and irresistible knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, leaves us eternally indebted to your lotus feet.

We remember when you hadn’t translated *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for many, many weeks, until one dark night, during your last days in Vṛndāvana, the news gradually spread by word of mouth that you were once more miraculously translating. Many devotees had begun to rise from their sleep (this was around 11 pm) and had hurriedly made their way to your quarters. Despite your appearing to be seemingly incapacitated, lying horizontally on your bed, we saw that while one devotee operated the dictating machine, holding the microphone closely to your mouth for fear of missing one word (your voice was barely audible, no more than a whisper), another devotee carefully read the Sanskrit verses from which you could glean your purports. We simply looked on in wonder to see you bathed in the glory of the message of *Bhāgavatam* (a stirring testimonial to the fact that your body was functioning on pure spiritual energy alone). So absorbed were you in your transcendental task that you failed to notice the growing number of devotees around your bedside. When you did finally pause and look up, you seemed surprised, and, motioning to one devotee, you inquired, “Oh, how have they come?”

One devotee who stood behind your bed silently massaging your head dutifully replied, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, they have come to catch some nectar from your lotus mouth.” *Prema-bhakti yāha hoite, avidyā vināśa jāte:* “From him ecstatic *prema* emanates, by him ignorance is destroyed.”

Just as you had written on your sea-voyage to the States how the nectarean life is *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, the source of all your vitality, similarly your causeless mercy in the form of your instructions provides the nectar for which we are always anxious. Actually, to meet the Lord or the Lord’s representative is no ordinary incident. As you have described in the purport to the ninth verse in the chapter “Vidura Approaches Maitreya,” Maitreya’s meeting with Lord Kṛṣṇa may have been due to *ajñāta-sukṛti,* or some unknown devotional service. Similarly, our meeting Your Divine Grace (according to our minds) could only be due to some extraordinary accident. There is no valid cause or reason why we should have been allowed to get your *kṛpā,* save and except to be instrumental in helping you to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore your mercy is quite appropriately termed “causeless.” Let us aspire to always be humble recipients of that causeless mercy and simply execute your divine will to the best of our ability. Please help us to always remember our position and enable us to cultivate this immense feeling of obligation to your lotus feet by allowing us to always remain engaged in your devotional service. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your servants at ISKCON Bangkok,*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. I would like to recall some of your important instructions. I understand that whatever you say is coming directly from God - Kṛṣṇa.

As you said, without the mercy of the spiritual master, one cannot chant the holy names of the Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. But you are so merciful that you came with your wisdom and gave us the light of life.

But I am so much a great fool that I have not taken this wisdom as my life and soul. I don’t know what will be my future for this foolishness.

I am falling at your lotus feet and begging you to forgive me and be merciful to this poor rascal boy.

## Bhaktivedanta Ashram

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you Śrīla Prabhupāda.

On this, your appearance day, we wish to offer the following prayer.

Your character and activities are spotless. Whenever you speak, you speak Vedic knowledge for the benefit of every living entity. Every single aspect of your existence, namely from your toe to the hair on the top of your head, is always engaged in the service of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are a teacher by example. You have taught us that actions are superior to precept; therefore simply by being a witness of your pure devotional service, learned persons have given you the title *Ācārya*, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are the founding father of our ISKCON family. For more than a decade, up until your very last breath, you fought against all odds to establish our spiritual Society for the benefit of its members and the world at large; therefore men who are wise know and understand you as their well-wisher, Śrīla Prabhupāda. By elevating unfortunate, condemned persons like ourselves, by your mercy that purifies the hearts of the lost conditioned souls, by your clear words that establish the existence of God and therefore service to God, there is no doubt that devotees and scholars alike call you Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants at ISKCON Sydney,*

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our worthless obeisances at your divine lotus feet. We take shelter of you, who are the dearmost confidential associate of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā,* 9), you describe the mission of Lord Caitanya in terms of “plant of *bhakti*.” Lord Caitanya is Himself the tree of transcendental love for Kṛṣṇa, its gardener, and also the bestower and enjoyer of its fruits. On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we may meditate on your relation with the tree of *bhakti* and the Supreme Gardener.

Lord Caitanya considered that his name, Viśvambhara, would be actualized if He could fill the whole universe with love of Godhead. Thinking in this way, He brought the desire tree of devotional service to earth and began to grow a garden in Navadwip, sprinkling on the seed the water of His will. The seed first fructified in the person of Mādhavendra Purī, then Īśvara Purī; then Lord Caitanya Himself became the main trunk of this tree. With Paramānanda Purī as the central root and eight other *sannyāsīs* as other roots in the eight directions, the tree stood firmly. The two main branches of Advaita *Ācārya* and Lord Nityānanda came from the trunk, and from these two came innumerable branches, which cover the entire world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you comment that the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness is one of these branches of the Lord Caitanya tree, but we can factually observe that it is the only branch which is nourished from the trunk. It must be understood, therefore, that even amongst other descendents of Lord Caitanya, you are exceptional as that personality who is delivering the Lord’s mercy to all conditioned souls.

The fruits of devotional service ripened, and Lord Caitanya distributed handful upon handful in all directions, without consideration of who was qualified to receive them. But when these nectarean fruits became so plentiful, the Lord lamented, “I am the only gardener. How many places can I go to? How many fruits can I pick and distribute? It would certainly be a very laborious task to pick the fruits and distribute them alone, and still I suspect that some would receive them and others would not. Therefore I order everyone within this universe to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and distribute it everywhere.”

Lord Caitanya, as the fully independent and self-sufficient Supreme Personality of Godhead, has no need of assistance of others; yet He orders everyone to engage in the *saṅkīrtana* mission so that we, who “were leading confused, chaotic lives,” will eagerly take part in distributing love of Godhead and by that distribution taste the fruits and feel transcendental happiness. Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are “finding peace in *saṅkīrtana*,” and we do “acknowledge the supreme benefit of this movement.”

Then Lord Caitanya specifically requested all the inhabitants of India to take responsibility for this mission; but just as Lord Caitanya lamented that without assistance the fruits could not be distributed to everyone quickly enough, you also lament that no one, not even your God brothers, took pains to assist you. You say, “This is only one Indian’s humble service to the world.” In one sense we relish the fact that you were on your own, because we can then offer you exclusive credit and glorification. You are the unique object of the world’s gratitude.

We meditate how the Lord Himself transported from Kṛṣṇaloka this transcendental tree with branches such as Advaita *Ācārya* and Lord Nityānanda, and whose fruits are the only precious commodity for the material world, and we thus appreciate that because you have expanded the Lord’s pleasure immeasurably, you are without doubt the most empowered assistant gardener.

Considering these points, it is obvious that the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness is no small-time affair; it is destined to encompass all living entities. At Bhaktivedanta Manor in 1973, George Harrison commented, “In the future ISKCON will be so large it will require executive management.” You replied, “I have divided the world into twelve zones with twelve representatives. As long as they keep to the spiritual principles, Kṛṣṇa will help them.” This is your greatest humility, that you bequeathed your movement to your disciples, and your greatest glory is that despite your physical absence, you are present in their hearts, guiding and enlightening them.

May this vision of you inspire us in constant endeavor. Today we vow that your movement, as the only productive branch of the Caitanya tree, will flourish. We will cultivate more and more fruits and see that every one of them is distributed and that every conditioned soul receives.

All glories to you, the chief of all transcendental gardeners and the most bountiful of all the branches of the Caitanya tree. Out of all its fruits, service to you is the most nectarean.

*We beg to remain, your eternal servants, the devotees of Bhaktivedanta Manor,*

## Bhaktivedanta Village

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā, one finds a great challenge to properly describe your glorious attributes, which are as many as the waves on the sea. Your unceasing preaching work, you literary accomplishments, and you total purity in devotional service are in themselves hard to enumerate, but it becomes even more difficult to render into words the love and compassion that you gave freely to all of us. In your absence, one might think we are left with only the memories of your wonderful pastimes and qualities, which, while certainly sustaining us and giving us fresh enthusiasm, could also dim with the relentless passage of time. However, while memories may be finite and limited, the fact is that your causeless mercy upon us is eternal, and once we have accepted the shelter of your lotus feet, this gift is ours for eternity. This spiritual linking with the transcendental domain has arisen for no other reason than by your unexplainable compassion upon us.

This relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple is not hindered by the limitation of time and space, nor diminished by physical absence, nor subject to forgetfulness or change, but rather it is eternally existent and infinitely increasing. Our relationship with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, need not be limited to fading memories that can risk crossing the thin line of sentiment, but can continually deepen as our understanding of your instructions and desires increase. Actually, many of us experience that our appreciation for your mission and your divine qualities has grown over the years since your disappearance. Perhaps we did not fully appreciate who you were and the enormity and universal scope of your service to the cause of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa. And, of course, we still cannot begin to grasp the degree of the purity of your devotion and the full impact of your powerful preaching in changing the course of the world. A small glimmer of appreciation, however, does arise within us when we take shelter of your instructions and remain steadfast in the devotional service that you have given us. Our real link with you is through those words and instructions and by our full dedication in attempting to fulfill your desires and instructions and by our full dedication in attempting to fulfill your desires and plans.

Observing the continuing growth of ISKCON throughout the world, the increasing book distribution, devotee-making, festivals, and praṣadam distribution, we take this as more evidence of the reality of your eternal presence. We realize that none of this could go on, what to speak of increase, if it were not for the continual inspiration and guidance that your impart to your disciples. Your disciples are spiritually advancing and are developing preaching programs throughout the world by your ongoing mercy. Whatever small achievements we can accomplish are seen as a glorification of you and testimony to the truth of your timeless instructions.

Just as you, in your humility, gave all credits to you Guru Mahārāja, we, as a matter of absolute reality, give all credit to you that we are able to remain at all on the auspicious path of devotional service. We must constantly pray that we might remember you always as the eternal source of all of our hope, ability, knowledge, and devotion, and that we may try to somehow repay your mercy by dedicating our lives to your mission.

Your eternal servants at Bhaktivedanta Village,

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**
