## Bhubaneswar

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this glorious day of your appearance, we offer our humble obeisances at your lotus feet again and again, because you have blessed us with your causeless mercy, giving us secure shelter at your lotus feet. We have no words to express your mercy. How merciful you are! When we were suffering in the burning fire of material existence, you came to shelter us, out of compassion. As stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.5.3),

*janasya kṛṣṇād vimukhasya daivād*  
*adharma-śīlasya suduḥkhitasya*  
*anugrahāyeha caranti nūnaṁ*  
*bhūtāni bhavyāni janārdanasya*

**”O my Lord, great philanthropic souls travel on the earth on behalf of the Supreme Personality of Godhead to show compassion on the fallen souls, who are adverse to the sense of subordination to the law.”**

So you came out of compassion for fallen souls like us and travelled all over the world with the mission of bringing souls back to Godhead, back home. It is a great mahātmā’s or Vaiṣṇava’s duty. You are such a great Vaiṣṇava.

We had forgotten Kṛṣṇa, and you taught us how to become Kṛṣṇa conscious and serve Kṛṣṇa. We are very wicked fellows; we have so many lusty desires. But you saved us, you made us taste the nectar of Kṛṣṇa-prema. How nice is the taste of that nectar you have given us! We have nothing to pay you back, and we cannot pay you back. Please shower your causeless mercy upon us that we will be able to fulfill your desire and serve you eternally.

As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda sings, *yogyate vicāre kichurai pāi tomāra karuṇa sāra.*” We have no qualification; only your mercy is required. Really, if you just shower your causeless mercy on us, we will be able to fulfill your desires here in Bhubaneswara, otherwise not possible.

You have the desire to build a nice Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple and establish a nice (famous) ISKCON center here. And you have also the desire of printing your books in Oriya.

But we are such unfortunate and unworthy persons here that we have not yet done anything tangible (spectacular) to fulfill your desire and make you happy. We have just made a humble beginning. Please make us your fully surrendered servants to fulfill your mission here in Bhubaneswara by making us as your instruments in thy hands. Moreover, we humbly pray on this auspicious day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you give us strength to realize your mercy and appreciate your benediction more and more.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Bhubaneswar,

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda,**

Gaura Govinda Swami / Tejiyas dāsa / Durgama dāsa / Madirā devī dāsī

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda and present ISKCON ācāryas**

Vaiṣṇava dāsa

**Grand disciples**

Vanamālī dāsa / Pratāparudra dāsa / Narottama dāsa / Trilokanātha dāsa / Śuddha-citta dāsa / Sādhuhṛdaya dāsa / Śaṅkara dāsa / Pañcaratna dāsa

## Bombay

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Our most heartfelt obeisances do we offer to you on this most important of all holy days, the appearance celebration of your Divine Grace. “For those who are condemned to rotate in the cycle of birth and death within this material world, association with pure devotees cent percent engaged in hearing and chanting the nectarian topics of Śrī Vaikuṇṭhanātha is the supreme benediction.” Please give us your darśana. Allow us to properly glorify you by meditating on your transcendental activities. Allow us to remember you. Grant us that for at least a few moments, we can fix our feeble minds in contemplation of your divine qualities, pastimes, and instructions. Actually, it is the duty of the serious disciple to always be fixed in remembering his spiritual master - dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhayaṁ vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam - to always be fixed in the service of his lotus feet. Our position is so fallen, however, that we are remembering and doing so many other things. In the Nectar of Devotion it is explained that the special days for remembering Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master are the cause of ecstatic love. At least for ourselves, this Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is your special appearance, to remind us of our duty and service to you.

You once said, “Vṛndāvana is my home, Māyāpur is my place of worship, and Bombay is my office.” Any devotee who visits Vṛndāvana - and Govardhana Hill, its temples, Rādhā-Dāmodara and Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma - will appreciate “Here is my home.” Vṛndāvana is transcendentally such a nice place. Likewise, if we go to Māyāpur, the birthplace of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the place where the Lord inaugurated the saṅkīrtana movement with His associates, where the Māyāpur Candrodaya Mandir and the Golden City will arise from the plains of the Gaṅgā, we can also understand, “This is our place of worship.” Then we come to Bombay, where the air is thick and heavy from heat and humidity. The acrid burning smell (that is unique to India) almost makes you cough and choke. The sewage stench pervades practically everywhere. The traffic dirt and cosmopolitan commotion rivals even New York City. The squalor of the jopadpattis makes even the Harlem ghetto look good. How did you choose this place? What could you even mean, “Bombay is my office?” Why did you have to leave Vṛndāvana to come here?

The question is answered if we but think of what you were doing all those years in Vṛndāvana before coming to America. You were not simply enjoying the peaceful atmosphere of Vṛndāvana in retirement. There was no tinge of self-comfort or even concern for your own maintenance. Once when you fell ill, a vrajavāsī inquired, “Swamiji, why don’t you buy a bed?” You quickly replied, “I need every paisa for my books.” You were busy preparing. Alone and without assistance, you were writing and publishing the first three volumes of the Bhāgavatam, staying in your rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple and churning to perfection your scheme to fulfill the order of your Guru Mahārāja - to spread the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. Again, if we consider, what is the real mood of Vṛndāvana? It is the place where Kṛṣṇa’s only business is to please His devotees, and the only business of His devotees is to please Kṛṣṇa. When Kṛṣṇa descended, He came to teach the highest love of God, as exemplified by the gopīs and by His dealings with the residents of Vṛndāvana - pure love, untinged by any personal desire. No desire for one’s self - not for any of the kinds of liberation nor even for personal association with the Lord in His own abode. Kṛṣṇa’s desire was your desire. There was no difference.

*anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyaṁ jñāna-karmādy-anāvṛtam*  
*ānukūlyena kṛṣṇānuśīlanaṁ bhaktir uttamā*

You write in that purport from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya-līlā*19.167), “The criterion is that the devotee must know what Kṛṣṇa wants him to do. One who is serious in wanting to render pure devotional service to the Lord must take shelter of the spiritual master who comes in disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa. Without accepting a bona fide spiritual master coming in disciplic succession, one cannot find out the real purpose of devotional service. Therefore, one has to accept shelter of a bona fide spiritual master and agree to be directed by him. The first business of a pure devotee is to satisfy his spiritual master, whose only business is to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

When Kṛṣṇa came, He spoke *Bhagavad-gītā* and made His request:

*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad yājī māṁ namaskuru*

”Everyone should become Kṛṣṇa’s devotee and engage in devotional service. Everyone should offer obeisances to the Supreme Personality of Godhead and worship Him... These are the desires of the Supreme Lord, and one who fulfills His desires favorably is actually a pure devotee. Kṛṣṇa wants everyone to surrender to Him, and devotional service means preaching His gospel all over the world.” (*Cc Madhya-līlā* 19.167) So it was on the order of Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, as well as your own causeless mercy, that you left Vṛndāvana to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the entire world.

Factually you never leave Vṛndāvana, Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you carry Lord Kṛṣṇa within your heart. You are a moving temple and a moving Vṛndāvana. Wherever you went, that place became Vṛndāvana.

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ prabho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni svāntaḥ-sthena gadā-bhṛtā*

**”Saints of your caliber are themselves places of pilgrimage. Because of their purity, they are constant companions of the Lord, and therefore they can purify even the places of pilgrimage.” (*****Bhāg*****. 1.13.10)**

So how is it that you chose Bombay *as your office*? An office is the headquarters for a business organization. And what is the best place for a business? In a big city, of course. You cited Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s establishing the precedent that temples should be in the cities as bases for preaching and contacting the mass of people. Why Bombay in particular, then? This was your master plan. You noted that Vedic culture in India was practically dead, “but alive or dead, an elephant is very valuable.” You said, “If we can take over India, we can take over the world.” Bombay, of course, is preeminent, the most important city in India. All the major industries, finances, and prestigious people of India are in Bombay. Just as New York City is the most important city in the world (you first came there so strategically in 1966 to start this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in the West), so when you returned again to India in 1971, you came to Bombay. In your supreme genius you planned very carefully. This would be your “place of business.” As we read in the *Līlāmṛta*, you spent so much time, personal effort, and concern, even from afar, in securing the land and in building a temple for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsavihārī. On your way to see Śrīmatī Sumati Morarji, you took notice of the land here in Juhu and prayed to Kṛṣṇa, “If you just give this land, I will build You a nice temple.” Kṛṣṇa personally signed with you in a business contract, fron whence your whole Bombay pastimes unfolded. In your twelve years of ISKCON world preaching and travels, nowhere else did you spend so much time as in Bombay - more than in Vṛndāvana, more than in Māyāpur, and more than in Los Angeles. You stayed here for months on end. From just a segment of your *Bhāgavatam* classes here, the whole *Teachings of Lord Kapila* was composed, a partial memorial of your preaching in Bombay. So tirelessly you preached throughout the day: From the morning walks by the ocean at daybreak, to the *Bhāgavatam* class, to encouraging the devotees, and sending them out in life-membership making, to speaking with all the important VIPs of India: government officers, industrialists, religionists, professors, or anyone else who came, and then, in the evening, to the *paṇḍāl* programs at Cross Maidan, where you had your “white elephants” dance and chant, which drew such huge crowds and were so incredibly successful. You were the supreme *saṅkīrtana* general, right on the front lines, leading the charge. You were a *mahārathi*, battling *māyā’s* forces by the tens of thousands and soundly defeating them. Always poised and fresh like a rose, you were constantly in transcendental bliss. India was your personal preaching zone, and Bombay was your headquarters.

When you took this land in Juhu, in your depth of vision you could see its future development. What was only an overgrown jungle of trees and thatched huts is now the most affluent of all residential areas in Bombay - the Hollywood of India. Even today we can see so many expensive hotels being constructed. You alone could see all this. Despite all obstacles, setbacks, lack of enthusiasm, and even the noncooperation from your own disciples, you had your mind set on this Hare Krishna Land. Since your business was only to please Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa’s only business was to give it to you. He did, of course, in a way just to bring out our devotion (or to see how poor it was) and to glorify your devotion (and how great in was!). It was on your behalf that Kṛṣṇa stood out and waited in a makeshift shanty, tolerating so much austerity and abuse, just to fulfill the promise of His pure devotee.

Business is generally a cutthroat affair. Everyone knows to make it in business, one has to be tough. It is not an uncommon thing for someone to cheat and kill to make it to the top. In that sense you were not a material businessman. As businessman you were perfect. Everything you did was successful. Above all, however, you were a devotee; your business was for Kṛṣṇa. It was of a different nature. Politeness and humility were your “trademark.” Businessman may display some superficial symptoms of etiquette or cordial behavior, but it is only a facade for their selfish and fiendish contrivances. Your exalted qualities were as deep as the ocean. You had no duplicity, since you were seeing everyone as part of Kṛṣṇa and everything meant for Kṛṣṇa’s service. You were the perfect gentleman, the truest friend, and everyone’s well-wisher. A Vaiṣṇava can be soft as a rose and also hard as a thunderbolt. You were uncompromising, undaunted, and perseverant to the end to get this Juhu property for Kṛṣṇa. You stood firm. You ordered the devotees to take the demons to court. You were even prepared to fight. You weren’t sentimental in the least. Despite your warlike mood and the atrocities of Mr. N., when Mrs. N. came to meet you personally, you accepted her as your own daughter. You were fighting only for Kṛṣṇa - nothing for yourself. In *Bhagavad-gītā* (12.13-14 purport) you wrote, “A devotee does not become his enemy’s enemy. He is always calm, quiet and patient in spite of many distressful conditions. A devotee is always kind to everyone, even to his enemy.” In all activities, your business was only to act for Kṛṣṇa; therefore whatever you did was pure and exemplary.

Sometimes the karmīs here, and elsewhere, accuse us of being “commercial” or even “fraudulent.” Though there was never a more expert businessman than yourself, no one ever accused you of this. Even if they tried, it couldn’t be taken seriously. Your motives were so unquestionably pure. Obviously our motives are so impure and misguided. Businessmen are some of the most selfishly motivated people. (“Actively foolish,” you would say.) But we should not be like them. We should be like you. We should follow your instructions very carefully. In one initiation letter to the temple president in Brooklyn in 1975, you wrote, “Make sure all the devotees follow the program of rising early, attending mangal-arati, chanting sixteen rounds, attending *Bhagavad-gīṭā*and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*class. Don’t try to be a better paramahamsa than your spiritual master. In Vrindavan, daily I am attending class and seeing how nicely my disciples are speaking. So daily morning and evening class. In morning and evening no business, just hearing and chanting. Don’t be a better paramahamsa than your *Guru* Maharaja. Strictly follow this program.” This is our “business training.” Then we become qualified to go out on *saṅkīrtana*. You caution us: “One who has not properly heard from the spiritual master or who does not follow the regulative principles is not fit for chanting. One who has not listened carefully to the instructions of the spiritual master is unfit to chant or preach the cult of devotional service.” (*Cc. Madhya* 19.152) If we, as your disciples, honestly examine ourselves, it is clear that too much of the bad publicity or poor public opinion we are receiving is due to our own nonsense. We are in the public spotlight, and a black mark on a white sheet stands out clearly. We are not following your instructions to the “T.” We are disqualifying ourselves to be your representatives. “It is not such a cheap thing to be a Vaiṣṇava,” you told us.

*śruti-smṛti-purāṇādi-pañcarātra-vidhiṁ vinā*  
*aikantikī harer bhaktir utpātāyaiva kalpate*

Devotional service that ignores your instructions will be a cause of disturbance to society and to your ISKCON. If we keep on, not becoming lax or slack in our *sādhana* and our service, in due course of time we can fulfill your mission of making the entire world Kṛṣṇa conscious. This was you desire: “Become Kṛṣṇa conscious yourself and give it to others.” As you were successful in your endeavors of speaking this philosophy in English to the West, we will be successful in making the entire world Kṛṣṇa conscious - just by following the programs you have given us, even though it may seem to us so far away and impossible. In your prayers on the *Jaladuta*, you revealed that you did not know if you would be successful, but you had a strong faith in Kṛṣṇa and your spiritual master. Similarly, if we just adhere to your order - get it clear in our minds what it is - then it is guaranteed that you business (and our business as your disciples and grand disciples) will be a success. There is the traditional name “such and such & Sons.” So this is our family business, coming down for generations, *paramparā.* If we simply go on in this way with your business (making it our business), even in the hellish cities of Kali yuga, sticking fast to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, then it is also assured we will continue our business with you and Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world, Goloka-Vṛndāvana. *Nitya-līlā-praviṣṭa.* Thank you for this greatest boon of the goal of life.

Here in Bombay the atmosphere is very potent from all of your years of preaching work. Drinking the nectar of those activities is quickly effective in weaning us from the taste of *māyā’s* illusions. The remembrance of those pastimes is so intoxicating that we are desiring nothing else. Everyone comes to look for the “Vedic cure” you have already given to us. You have given us everything. We have to simply take your mercy. You live forever in your books, in your instructions, and in all your activities. ISKCON was, and continues to be, your pastime. Engage us in your *līlā.* As your deputed agents at your Bombay office, we want to become more and more absorbed in your business of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. While you were with us, you were relentlessly pushing us on. Please stay with us. We pray that we can feel the weight of your instructions and that you will give us the strength to carry the burden of your mission more and more, until it bears down into our lives and souls as our only source of thinking, feeling, and willing. This is our happiness. This is our ecstasy. Please make us your servants.

*Your clerks and staff at your Bombay office*.  
All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

## Boston

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**”When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the great gardener, sees that people are chanting, dancing, and laughing, and that some of them are rolling on the floor and some are making loud humming sounds, He smiles with great pleasure.” (*****Cc. Ādi*** **9.50)**

When Your Divine Grace, the chief gardener-assistant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, first arrived in the West, you brought only your gardening tools: the pure chanting of the holy name and the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Some who thought they knew you said, “You are too old. The Swamiji is going to die there.” But like a great warrior going into battle on the order of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you had absolute faith in the order of your *guru* and in your transcendental weapons. Seeing the populace deeply rooted in activities of passion and ignorance, you began to plow the fertile fields of suffering humanity, uprooting demoniac mentalities by your pure chanting and planting seeds of the *bhakti-latā* by your association and forceful preaching, even in the hard lands of voidism and impersonalism.

Endlessly weeding out materialism, and lovingly nurturing every creeper, your rain of mercy made no discrimination where to fall or where not to fall. Thus the Caitanya tree grew and spread its branches out all over the world, giving cooling shade, shelter, and sweet fruits to all who would take. Everyone tasted the nectar of the holy name as Caitanya-deva had predicted, and thus we saw you smile with great pleasure as the devotees danced, chanted, and laughed. By your mercy you have provided a Vaikuṇṭha oasis within this global wasteland, where everyone can drink the cool waters of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa and take shelter in the shade of your lotus feet.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please do not allow us to again become ensnared in the weeds of material desire, but shower your mercy upon us so that we may serve you as you desire, and make you smile. Pleasing you is our life and soul.

*Your servants at ISKCON Boston,*

## Boulder

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**”I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances thousands of times in the dust of your lotus feet.

Just as before the rising of the morning sun all of this world is dark, cold, and fearful, for one born destined to suffer in the darkness of ignorance this world is a cold, frightening, and uninhabitable place.

Just as in the dead of night the eerie silence is broken only by mischievous aggressors moving solely to stalk their pray, so the loneliness of this material existence (born of our separation from our eternal Lord and master) is only more intensified by the taunts of false friends and lovers, who seek only to prey upon our naivety.

And, just as the only hope for relief from the entrapment of darkness is the graceful appearance of the sun, so the only hope for the jīvas imprisoned in ignorance is the holy appearance of Your Divine Grace.

”All living entities,” Lord Kṛṣṇa pronounces, “are born into delusion, bewildered by dualities arisen from desire and hate.” Thus deluded, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we struggled to satisfy our innermost hankerings via material relationships and allurements. But we were destined only to unhappiness, unfulfillment, and failure - eternally. Yet by your causeless mercy, you chose to deliver us, to awakens us from the darkness of ignorance. We had not even asked for your help, impudent as we were. Yet you still came, and you still helped us.

You told us why we were unhappy, and you told us who we were. You told us why we were here, where we were going and, most importantly, whom we should serve and whom we should love. You gave us a new family, a new society, and a new mission. You gave us knowledge, you gave us hope, and you gave us yourself, tirelessly. You gave us a cause to live for, a goal unequaled in history compared to those given by any other leader, reformer, politician, or saint. And you gave us a perfect example to follow - your own glorious and unflinching pure devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

And then you left us, or so it would seem. But you left behind your vāṇī, your disciples, your ISKCON, and all of your other priceless gifts. And, of course, your unlimited and ongoing mercy accompanying us always.

Just as before the daylight there is practically no life, before your divine appearance there was no life. And just as all the universe owes the debt to its very existence to the sun, so we, your disciples and grand disciples, owe to you the debt of our existence. Therefore it is said this debt to you is so great that it can never be repaid. Indeed, no matter how much we may offer you in service and devotion, never will a time come when we should not, or must not, offer you more.

As you state in the *śāstra* (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, 4.28.51), “A devoted disciple of the spiritual master would rather die with the spiritual master than fail to execute the spiritual master’s mission... It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. Otherwise the disciple should decide to die along with the spiritual master. In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations.”

If we cannot fulfill your mission Śrīla Prabhupāda, if we cannot use our every breath, our every word, and our every deed just to spread your mission - the sun of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*, the sun of ISKCON, the sun of your books - then it would be better to give up our life now. Yet that life already belongs to you, for you gave it to us. Therefore what choice have we but to surrender it all to you? Please give us the power to surrender to you utterly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to your mission, and to you ISKCON. There is nothing else to do. There is nothing else to aspire for. There is nothing else to pray for. Please give us this, your greatest mercy. Let us surrender to you utterly Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your lowly servants at Boulder Yātrā,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Anuttama dāsa / Pālikā-devī dāsī / Raṅgavatī-devī dāṣi

**Granddisciples**

Jitāri dāsa

## Brisbane

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

With eyes like the plumes of a peacock,  
With ears like the holes of a snake,  
With a mouth like a blacksmith’s bellows,  
I came to you in distress.

”What is the problem?” you replied,  
Your voice so grave and sincere.  
”Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa... take some prasad...  
Engage in service to Kṛṣṇa,  
And your life will become sublime.”

Your method is perfect,  
But how can we penetrate  
The intoxicated mind...  
Conditioned by Kali ...  
By unbridled senses...  
Lust, anger and greed!

Like an expert physician,  
You gave the prescription -  
”Distribute my books -  
A chapter, even a page or a line  
Can change a person’s life.”

”Kṛṣṇa is the heart of all.  
Look beyond the material form.”  
A dose of this medicine  
Will shatter our illusions,  
Protecting us from inevitable fate.

As brilliant as the sun,  
Your books disseminate the rays.  
I beg from you a blessing,  
For just a part of your potency  
To pass your message to the world.

*Your servants at ISKCON Brisbane,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Cairns

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances,  
All glories to you,  
For it was a time of great opportunity,  
Offered and arranged by the causeless mercy of the Supreme Lord.  
I speak of the time of meeting with you, our beloved Gurudeva,  
And I speak not just of myself,  
But all of us, those who are still with you  
Now, your disciples, your eternal servants,  
All sold out to you.  
Certainly we all experienced, in many different ways,  
The unique features of your transcendental mercy.  
O Prabhupāda, at whose feet we all sit,  
You are the unalloyed servant of God.  
Through you, that which was previously unknown, or at least forgotten,  
Those deep secrets of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava-sampradāya,  
Became known,  
Revealed to us in many ways  
According to your mercy,  
And of course, our own willingness.  
And as for my own wretched self,  
What can be said?  
I also came to you,  
So conditioned,  
Embarrassed,  
Exhausted by the effects of māyā.  
And perfectly complete with stifled intellect,  
Weak mind,  
Useless body,  
And crippled soul.  
These were my original disqualifications.  
But in your merciful presence these disqualifications became, somehow,  
qualified.  
And I remember clearly  
That precious sequence of events that followed:  
Your every movement,  
Your manipulative genius,  
Your merciful glance,  
Your grave, yet meaningful speech.  
And from that point onward...  
(The transcendental occurrence of even the first meeting)  
You became, certainly, my guide,  
My well-wishing friend,  
Eternal master,  
And the sole object of affection.  
But there is even something more,  
Though I speak of it with great caution,  
So as not to confuse, or bewilder,  
But to tell of your glories only  
And of the inconceivable potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

So there came a time when,  
Due to your immediate mercy and kindness,  
I was able to fall down at your lotus feet in humility.  
Consequently, a small group of the ocean of your glories fell upon my  
head,  
And drinking it,  
I became absorbed in the ecstasy of faith and love for you.  
The questions put forth were submissive and relevant.  
And the answers received were full of the most perfect sense and  
direction.  
And then,  
In your person,  
And in your voice,  
In your face,  
And in your physical gestures -  
Indeed, in all these four places -  
There appeared wonderful, divine transformations.  
But they were not those ecstatic symptoms usually seen in the bodies of  
pure devotees.  
These transformations were exhibitions of unique spiritual opulence.  
And they were coming directly from the Supreme Personality of Godhead  
Himself, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya.

Intense transcendental beauty,  
Glorious fame,  
Full cognizance,  
All-possessive wealth,  
Inconceivable potency,  
And perfect renunciation:  
Like the sun, revealing his glory in a crystal jewel,  
The Supreme Personality of Godhead, unknown to mundane senses,  
Yet whose fame is unsurpassed,  
Visible, through the transparent via medium of His most perfect servitor,  
Your Divine Grace.

So we have now, only this much more to say:  
We need,  
Long for,  
And cherish your association  
And the dust of your lotus feet,  
For there is nothing else that we want.  
We’re in this great darkness,  
But you are our light.  
You took us by hand,  
You extended your love.  
You gave us a glimpse.  
A ray of great hope.

And it’s an open secret!  
Please, therefore, let it be known.  
Let them all know it.  
Let us bring them to you,  
Although now not physically present.  
Your books continue to speak (the bṛhat mṛdaṅga).  
They carry your potency.  
They carry Śrī Kṛṣṇa.  
And they also carry us, again,  
Into your association  
And the association of that supremely attractive Lord.  
We have therefore nothing more to ask  
Than you please allow us to stay in your service,  
Because it’s your direct association,  
And connection with the Lord:  
Inconceivable,  
But understood.

*Your unworthy yet eternal servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Veṇugopāla dāsa

## Canberra

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Who is more merciful than Your Divine Grace? You have said, out of intense compassion, “I do not know where I will go, but I do not mind if I go to hell if I can preach about Kṛṣṇa consciousness there.” Such is your love for Śrī *Guru* and Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are just like the great devotee Prahlāda Mahārāja, who prayed to His Lord, Nṛsiṁha-deva,

*naivodvije para duratyaya-vaitaraṇyās*  
*tvad-vīrya gāyana-mahāmṛta-magna-cittaḥ*  
*śoce tato vimukha-cetasa indriyārtha-*  
*māyā-sukhāya bharam udvahato vimūḍhān*

**”My dear Lord, I have no problems and want no benediction from You, because I am quite satisfied to chant Your holy name. This is sufficient for me because whenever I chant, immediately I merge in an ocean of transcendental bliss. I only lament to see them \[the conditioned souls\] bereft of Your love. They are rotting in material activities for transient material pleasure and spoiling their lives toiling all day and night simply for sense gratification, with no attachment for love of Godhead. I am simply lamenting for them and devising various plans to deliver them from the clutches of** ***māyā*****.” (*****Bhāg*****. 7.9.43)**

These are the symptoms of a completely selfless *kṛṣṇa-bhakta*. You are always thinking how to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, how to trick the materialists to take Kṛṣṇa. You even told one drunkard Indian, “If you must drink, yes, you can say Hare Kṛṣṇa before you drink the wine!”

Not only did you tolerate our bad habits and poor service, you actually changed our hearts and made us aspiring first-class men. Just as a lotus is untouched by the murky water within which it grows, so you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, are also untouched by the degradation you associated with in order to liberate so many materialists.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always so busy with many activities - preaching, chanting, translating, traveling, accepting our service, serving us, teaching us - but every action was for Kṛṣṇa’s mission, to deliver His fallen parts and parcels. Not one moment went by when you did not have that sense of intense urgency to deliver and force us to try harder and harder to give our lives to take up Kṛṣṇa’s mission. So urgent was your message that you slept little and translated when all others were asleep. You know how precious every moment is. Your personal servants often had to give up their state of ignorance to be awakened by you in the middle of the night, as you instructed them in some vital issue for the welfare of the world.

Although Lord Caitanya advises not to take many disciples, you still accepted so many *mlecchas* and *yavanas* as your charges, not caring for the troublesome reaction. Your only desire is to deliver us to our real father, not caring for your own comfortable situation. You traveled so far and wide at much inconvenience to yourself - even renouncing the mango season in India to preach in Russia, which you described as being sweeter than any mango.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most perfect devotee - a *mahā-bhāgavata*. Although you are the greatest personality in the world, you consider yourself very small. Once one disciple brought you some nice foodstuff she had made. You opened the door, and your eyes opened wide: “For me?!” you exclaimed in all humility. Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, our hearts are melting as we hear of your wonderful qualities. We pray that this thought of you will never leave our minds.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were so astutely compassionate to one small gathering of puffed-up Indian hodge-podgers in Djakarta. I was amazed at the humble yet cutting way you preached - in real *sādhu* style. When quoting from *Bhagavad-gītā* you asked your servant to read verse 7.23. “What was that?” you queried, “Men of *intelligence* worship the demigods?”

”No, Śrīla Prabhupāda,” was the reply. “Men of *small* intelligence.” In this way you cut down these proud men by your humility and transcendental trickery.

So here we are in Canberra Yātrā, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a small temple in the capital of the meat-eaters’ land, Australia. We beg and beg for your mercy that we may become pure enough to attract the fallen souls of this cold place and bring them as an offering to your lotus feet. May we please you in this way lifetime after lifetime.

*We beg to remain, your humble disciples and grand disciples*,

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Mahārati dāsa / Rāsarāṇī-devī dāsī

Pauruṣa dāsa

## Central America

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Let us offer our respectful obeisances unto your divine lotus feet.

You are the spiritual master of the universe, master of masters, immaculate swan. What can we know about your qualities? You are our eternal master, and we are your servants. Our only aspiration is the shelter of your lotus feet. That person who receives your mercy easily makes advancement in spiritual life. Without your mercy, no one can attain pure love of Kṛṣṇa, even if he worships Kṛṣṇa for hundreds of years.

You are our eternal father, our perfect guide, and our true shelter. Who can understand your greatness?

Kṛṣṇa put all His opulences in your in your divine hands. You are the great *ācārya,* expert in all arts and perfect in every act.

Magnificent orator, relentless with the atheists; tireless fighter, free from illusion; deep knower of the *Vedas;* of resounding voice like thunder, and words like arrows direct to the heart. Who can remain indifferent to your arguments?

Like Lord Caitanya, you are the savior of the most fallen. That is why you have come to the Western countries and later to all corners of the earth, challenging all obstacles and difficulties to unite the entire world with the light of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. You are like the bright gold sun that comes in springtime, which gives new life everywhere, and like the full moon that refreshes in the summer. Your appearance is like the favorable rain at the time of drought, which is absorbed rapidly by the earth. Your transcendental books have been, are, and will always be the rain of mercy that falls over the garden of the heart and germinates the plant of devotion.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Wherever you go, take us. Whatever you want us to do, we will do it. Whatever you want us to say, we will say. Our only wish is to have your blessings.

Practically none of the devotees of this zone knew you personally, but they all feel as your sons, because you gave them life and shelter with your books and with your sons the *ācāryas,* from whom we can take initiation.

You have left everything perfectly arranged. How can we thank such great mercy, of which we cannot even conceive?

You fought with the determination and bravery of Bhīṣma, “till the last breath,” as it was your desire. With your own example you left the path open so that the confused souls of this complicated age could go back to the spiritual world.

To remember you means to forget *māyā,* and to serve you means to serve Kṛṣṇa. We want to serve you better and get to know you better, so that we can glorify you better and serve you better. One cannot know what love is and what humility is if one does not know you.

One cannot understand spiritual life or maintain oneself free from illusion without serving your divine lotus feet. That is why we ask to have pure intelligence to serve you without deviation, and to also serve those who follow you faithfully, in these moments when so many clouds are crossing by the pure sky of ISKCON.

We will put our best effort into maintaining the security of this movement that is so cared for by you and by Lord Kṛṣṇa. We will not permit any currents different from your mission to develop inside ISKCON, stirring up the atmosphere of purity that you left. Your fundamental instructions of “working cooperatively” will be always present for all of us, before everything else.

*Your humble servants of Central America* (*El Salvador, Guatemala, Costa Rica, and Panama*),

## Chaitanya College

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Vyāsa-pūjā day is an eternal function, an opportunity to pause and glorify the words, the qualities, the activities, and the transcendental behavior of you, our spiritual master. Those activities and that service which have been dedicated to you throughout the year for fulfilling your desire now become manifest in the form of transcendental sound vibration reflecting the quality of the heart of your followers.

But it is a challenge, not an easy thing, to glorify someone whose glories are unlimited. How can we describe one of unlimited opulences other than by words that simply hint the nature of such wonderful characteristics? As a small child may stand at the foot of a mountain and try to describe its breadth and width and height with fanciful gestures and words, so in the same way we, who are awestruck by your unlimited qualities, try in our own humble and insignificant way to describe our meager vision of your greatness.

One can start anywhere to describe your transcendental nature, for, like sugar candy, it is sweet wherever one tastes. You can also be glorified from whatever angle of vision one may approach. However, as soon as we begin to describe one quality, it immediately unfolds into an unlimited variety of transcendental opulences. To do it complete justice seems impossibility. And yet, it is our duty to describe to the best of our ability all those opulences that we realize through our own vision. And each devotee will realize that vision according to the service that he may perform to Your Divine Grace. Such a devotee may begin glorifying the spiritual master with one particular characteristic that he considers most prominent. For according to circumstance and the revelation of the spiritual master, different features of his transcendental qualities may become manifest at different times. Such then is our glorification of you, for we will try to approach you through that avenue which is most immediately connected with our devotional service and that aspect in which we are experiencing you at the present time.

We have seen many times in the past how you have put your disciples through the test of devotional service. That test of devotional service is none other than a test of love, a test of obedience of the disciple to the spiritual master, a test of faith in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, a test of determination to serve the Supreme Lord. Spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not an easy thing; rather, it requires selflessness in dedicating oneself to establishing a movement that is not much sought after by the general mass of people. However, you taught us by your own personal example that despite all personal inconveniences one must dedicate his life to the vision of the predecessor *ācāryas*, whose sole desire it was to establish this Kṛṣṇa consciousness as a worldwide movement to save humanity. Through your unlimited vision and wisdom, you have calculated the most expert means of distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to reluctant audience. Book distribution, *prasādam*, Diety worship, great festivals, *hari-nāma* - these are the weapons by which you break down the fortresses of false ego and pierce through the impenetrable armor of Kali-yuga to touch the hearts of the bewildered living entities.

To do this needful requires men, money, buildings, and above all great devotion and hard work. Plans and schemes according to time, place, and circumstance must be developed in order to achieve the mission, and as you drove yourself on from the transcendental abode of Vṛndāvana to the demoniac back streets of New York, so you also drive us on and on and on to relentlessly pursue our service, which seems to always expand the more we do. That service and the responsibility are certainly a great burden, a great weight for those who are serious in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for the burden of love is to see that all our endeavors are successful in order to meet your transcendental smile. But your expectations are high, and you ask us to shoot for the rhino, the rhino that becomes a insignificant mouse for those who fully take shelter of your lotus feet and become empowered by you, the incarnation and preaching the potency of the Lord.

Twenty-four hours a day is not enough time to fulfill all the service that needs to be done. We may decrease our eating, decrease our sleeping, and still it seems that there is so much to be done that it lies beyond our grasp. The anxiety to liberate conditioned souls, to increase the number of books that are distributed, to protect our temples and properties against the onslaught of demons, to defeat atheists, impersonalists, scientists, “deprogrammers,” brainwashers, demons who try to attack this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement from all different sides, even makes a few hours of sleep restless ones. But the anxiety is not like that of the materialist; neither is the burden one that crushes, as the load of sense gratification ultimately leads all materialists to destruction, for your service elevates us to ecstatic platforms of devotional service. Liberation does not mean to become free from anxiety, but it is actually the beginning of real pressure. All liberated souls, both in the material and spiritual worlds, are always feeling anxiety on behalf of service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, for in the anxiety of propagating devotional service to the fallen conditioned souls, one forgets about one’s own anxiety for sense gratification and quickly rises to the platform of selflessness, where one is freed from the bodily conception of life.

In 1976 in Toronto you wanted the devotees to purchase an enormous church, which would increase the overhead many times above that of the small facility in which they live. The devotees were protesting that such vast amounts of money would be very difficult to procure, and finally the temple president said that it would put everyone in too much anxiety in order to maintain such a facility. But you said, “Anxiety is good for by anxiety we advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness,” and with that the matter was settled and the building was purchased.

So we are seeing how you are so expert in making arrangements for placing us in anxiety, for forcing us to accept a responsibility of pushing on Lord Caitanya’s movement and thus forgetting about our own mental concoctions and petty anxieties. Big projects were always your desire. Doubling book distribution, big temple complexes in Bombay, build a city in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur, set of books in every home in America - you laid down the challenge and expected us to take it up. But your tactics are so kind and so loving, so encouraging, that only those who are blind and foolish fail to risk their life and soul to achieve your transcendental glance of pleasure. But truly there is nothing more satisfying than taking up some greater responsibility that you have offered and executing it to your satisfaction. Personally, here at Chaitanya College, we are feeling the weight of this great project. A large *gurukula,* and an enormous building which can either become a great preaching tool or break our back as a financial burden, a place for simply eating and sleeping. All facility for pushing on Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the U.K. and attracting the minds of even the aristocrats is here. Sometimes we may strain and groan under the weight of this burden, but please do not think that it is because we resent it. Jus as Mādhavendra Purī carried the load of sandalwood for Śrī Gopāla and felt transcendental anxiety, to, although we are also carrying a burden of financial and preaching responsibility, still we consider every day to be another challenge, another opportunity to please Your Divine Grace. Coal is soft, black, and unattractive; of what value can it be as an ornament or decoration? But if it is placed under a great pressure for some time, then according to the scientific method that same coal can turn into a lustrous diamond, the most precious amongst all valuable gems. Everyone values diamonds; for they know that their luster and sheen are without equal. So we are like that - raw material in your transcendental hands. But under pressure we can also transform into gemlike devotees, as transcendental ornaments to decorate your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. This is our experience at the present. We are seeing how so many devotees are advancing by taking up this responsibility that you have placed on their shoulders. We see the prime example of your qualified disciple Śrīla Bhagavān Goswami, who has become like a unique gem amongst other jewels by shouldering the burden that you have left him. We see how those who surrender to your movement surrender to the responsibility, become polished in time, and their gross characteristics change into the wonderful characteristics of pure devotees. In this way we are all trying to shoulder the burden, so that one day we may also all shine as tiny diamonds to decorate the transcendental region around your lotus feet and make you proud of the service of your disciples and grand disciples in this part of the world, where you so much wanted Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the one who tests us and you are the one who gives us the strength to pass the tests. You are the one who gives us intelligence to fulfill your vision and you are the one who bless us in the end. We pray today that you shower your mercy on all those who are trying to sincerely fulfill your mission and give us strength to shoulder the burden which you have left us in the form of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and Chaitanya College.

*We remain your humble servants,*

## Chandigarh

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We were dead men; you gave us new life. We were fools; you gave us transcendental knowledge. We were uncivilized; you gave us this divine spiritual culture of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We were homeless; you gave us hundreds of exquisite palaces. We were hungry; you gave us the most wonderful *prasādam*. We were crippled; you taught us how to dance. We were cursing; you taught us how to sing. We were angry and frustrated; you gave us peace. We were morose; you made us happy. We were envious; you taught us to be merciful. We were atheists; you gave us Kṛṣṇa, Makhanacora, the butter thief.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us never forget how much we were suffering before we met you, lest we become ungrateful of your unlimited causeless mercy. Please let us never forget how much you sacrificed to spread this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, lest we become lazy. Please let us never forget your compassion, lest we become callous and indifferent. Please let us never forget your instructions, lest we become lost. Please let us never forget to cooperate with your representatives the G.B.C., regional secretaries, temple presidents, temple commanders, *saṅkīrtana* leaders, etc., thus displeasing you and becoming liable to be rejected like sweet rice into which sand or salt has been added. Please forgive us for our innumerable offenses and allow us to continue to render service to your lotus feet.

*Your eternal servants,*

Ādiyajña dāsa / Sākṣi-gopāla dāsa / Jagannātha Rathayātrā dāsa / Rādhā-vallabha dāsa / Śyāma-kuṇḍa dāsa / Oṁkāra dāsa / Sārvabhauma dāsa / Bṛhaspati dāsa / Nanda Mahārāja dāsa / Hare Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Rādhā-vallabha dāsa / Nākādi dāsa / Mādhava dāsa / Bhakta Gauranga / Bhakta Ramesh / Śaktimatī-devī dāsī / Bhaktilatā-devī dāsī / Rati-devī dāsī / Yaśodā-devī dāsī / Śacī-devī dāsī / Caitanyapriyā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Meera / Bhaktin Lalita

## Colombia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear lord and master,*  
Please always accept me and allow me to remain in the association of your most dear disciples. O Jagad-*guru*, please help us to maintain ourselves in Kṛṣṇa consciousness by eternally speaking of your merciful glories that are enabling the conditioned souls to progressively leave this labyrinth of material existence.

Your relation with the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, is our greatest benediction, because by your example you are uninterruptedly showering us with *bhakti* and enthusing us in this way to become situated in our constitutional position.

Our dear master, please do not ever let us become separated from your example, so that some day we will attain the platform in which virtuous souls swim in the blissfulness of eternal knowledge of your divine service.

*Your servants at Cali Mandira*

## Dallas

**The Śaktyāveśa-avatāra**

Who else would do it?  
Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura had predicted it.  
All the śāstras pointed to the necessity of it.  
The world was desperately in need of it.  
Lord Kṛṣṇa desired it.

Who else would do it?  
How else would the Lord’s holy name spread all over the world?  
How else would Lord Caitanya’s mercy of delivering the fallen souls back  
to Godhead actually be made available to them?  
How else would the soothing nectar of the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam reach the  
hearts of those burning in the fire of material existence?  
How else could a real peace on earth be established without there being a  
singe cause to unite the activities of men of all races and nations - the  
completely nonsectarian cause of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa?

Who else would do it?  
Anyone could have done it, but ...  
The government leaders were interested only in their own position of  
power, not in the real welfare of the citizens.  
The religionists were interested only in their own sectarian views, not in  
eternal religious principles.  
The scientists were interested in proving that they could be God,  
not in appreciating the Original Scientist.  
The common men were completely bewildered by these false authorities.

Who else was there that could do it?  
No one else was completely free from selfish interests.  
No one else had such complete faith in the words of Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
No one else took up the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta with such  
enthusiasm and patience.  
No one else could have done it.  
No one else can take the credit for saving the world from its hellish  
destination.

We only want to carry on what you have started.  
We only want to fill the structure you have formed.  
We only want to lead our lives by your directions.  
We only want to preach what you have taught.

Please give us shelter at your lotus feet.

*Your humble servants at Rādhā-Kālachandjī Dhāma,*

## Denver

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Once again we are celebrating the anniversary of your most glorious appearance on the face of this planet, and again we are feeling that words cannot express our heartfelt sentiments, nor can they describe your sublime activities.

When we were young *bhaktas*, we frequently heard many tales about your struggles in India prior to your coming to America. When we heard these tales, they would cause so many emotions to arise. Sometimes we would feel angry, sometimes very happy, sometimes very sad; but most important was that these loving emotions simply helped us to become more attached to your lotus feet. After all these years, we are now very fortunate that all these previously only vague pastimes have been revealed to us in your *Līlāmṛta*. Reading these pastimes has significantly helped us to deepen our love and attachment for you. Your tireless efforts to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness at every opportunity are the perfect example for neophyte devotees such as ourselves.

But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your activities are certainly mysterious. We never thought that you would leave us in such an inexperienced position. One day we hardly know what *tilaka* is or how to even hold a broom, and the next day we must accept so much responsibility for preaching, for book publication and distribution, for training new *bhaktas*, and for beautiful Deity worship. Being confronted with such enormous tasks, we feel completely helpless. In the beginning we heard that is was a momentous achievement to distribute just one book, but you gave order to distribute millions of books, and your first disciples responded successfully through your inspiration and training. Those same disciples who stayed faithful are now pushing on this message. We see the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement expanding in more *bhaktas*, new centers, more books distributed, and new frontiers opening all around the world.

We pray that we will never become illusioned and that you will give us this transcendental vision to remain fixed in your mission. We know you have not left us, because you are manifest in the hearts and activities of your most sincere disciples. We know we can do wonderful things, but only if we take the humble position of menial servants and never become puffed up.

Let your personal example stimulate us to the point where we will give up our false pride, mental concoctions, and selfish desires, and simply desire your cause of bringing everyone to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

*Your humble servants at New Badarī-nārāyaṇa,*

## Detroit

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance. Unfortunately, I don’t have the vocabulary or proper intelligence to be able to glorify or appreciate you properly. Due to so many sinful activities, any condition is certainly lamentable. Even now while writing about your glories and trying to evoke some emotions, this stone like heart of mine is like some deep, dark cave, with so many undesirables in it. Anyone - except myself, who is so conceited and puffed up - can appreciate how you appeared in this world, by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s request, to deliver the most fallen. Just see how forgetful we are - even we have been given the shelter of your lotus feet, we are thinking ourselves so qualified and independent. One time you said, “How can I teach you if you cannot remember?” I began to reject, but then thought, Yes, I cannot remember or do anything right. I am only made of your mercy.

We’ve come from some gutter, and now after some position and fame, we are thinking we are so complete. One time when asked by a disciple why you were crying, you replied, “I’m simply praying to Lord Kṛṣṇa that I’ll not fall in *māyā*.” What then is our position, struggling as we are just to maintain what you have given us? So simple is the process, but so complex due to our desires. Just like the frog that is croaking while the snake of death is coming nearer, we have become so enamored, actually maddened, by our so-called abilities, forgetting that everything can be taken away by the Lord just as easily as it is given. One morning in Dallas on your morning walk, while walking back and forth in the parking lot of a park, you stopped and asked, “What are these men doing?” It was explained that they were playing golf. After hearing this, with a tear in your eye you said, “See how they are wasting their time, hitting this little ball.” The compassion you were exhibiting astounded all the devotees. We don’t have very much time! At any moment our lives could be over, and yet we are so absorbed in this bodily comfort. You were sold out to your *Guru* Mahārāja. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us to be sold out to you. Just as Lord Caitanya was in great anxiety for the conditioned souls, you also have exhibited this same anxiety, and we should also, not this anxiety for gold or *capātīs*. Of course, we can “dovetail” this toward Kṛṣṇa, but should we be so distracted? Therefore we are praying to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please engage us on the right path. Help us to fix our minds on your heartbeat, “the distribution of your books,” not on ours, which is palpitating with so many desires. When asked in San Francisco what will happen when you die, you replied, “I will never die.” Please Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not let us forget it.

## Dominican Republic

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

This wonderful time each year allows us to glorify you in a worldwide way. In this offering we declare to the world our obedience to your will and desire to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement according to your instructions. It is our life’s work, and the many obstacles sent by māyā provide the difficulties that make it relishable to take shelter at your lotus feet. We remember so clearly how we were rotting in this material world on the verge of self-destruction and untimely death. Then by your causeless mercy you appeared and distributed your mercy to us conditioned souls.

Here in this country (Sant Domingo) we have seen your causeless mercy working to maintain Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is eternally inspiring to chant your name. Whenever someone says, “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!” or “All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!!” a great surge of energy passes through the body just by remembering your name. As far as this country and the devotees are concerned, it’s like the story of Hanumān and the spider. Hanumān was throwing large boulders, and the spider was throwing small pebbles, but Lord Rāmacandra accepted both. So although in Santo Domingo we’re not great, erudite scholars or great *brāhmaṇas* and preachers, still we have placed your instructions upon our hearts, so therefore we know you will guide and protect us. And what more can any person ask for in life but to be firmly fixed in service eternally, internally, and externally at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami (Śrīla Prabhupāda)?

So humbly we ask again this year to forever continue instructing us to distribute books, make preaching programs, and to distribute the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Please be patient with us; let us stay under your lotus feet. And please drag us to the next preaching battleground.

*Your servants at Santo Domingo,*

## Durban

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to your unlimited kindness in allowing us to continue rendering service to you.

Today is a most historic occasion in the history of your South African Yātrā, as today we are offering you your first truly appropriate place of residence in this country, the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha temple. We are all most joyful that after so many years of turbulence and improper standards there is finally a suitable base for you to live in and send your preachers out from in a concerted effort to spread your mission throughout the land.

This temple has directly sprouted and grown from the seed of your desire, which you implanted in the hearts of the devotees here during your visit to South Africa in 1975. You said that we should build a temple “similar to the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira in Vṛndāvana,” and now it is done. We might be so bold as to say that perhaps it is one of the nicest temples in your whole movement, and certainly it is the first building of its kind built in the middle of a city in the Western world, expressly designed, from the foundations up, with preaching in mind.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please do not feel that we are being brazen enough to try to take the credit for this accomplishment. That is completely out of the question. The only secret of success in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that one has to become like an instrument in the hands of one’s spiritual master. You once said that if your disciples would follow your instructions to the letter, your movement could take over the planet in eighteen days. However, it has taken us nearly ten years to build this temple - and what a struggle it was!!!

Actually, it was not really a struggle, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but a labor of love, and during all the straining and pushing to fulfill your desire we have been forced on so many occasions to see our own weaknesses and inabilities. As a result, we have increasingly realized the absolute necessity of being under your shelter and care. Those who were unable to appreciate that point fell by the wayside - and there were many, both big and small personalities by our limited estimations.

We sincerely hope that you are happy with your new residence. Before you, you see the eternally blissful form of Your Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha, as well as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, His arms held high above His head as He dances in ecstasy. Of course, you know Them very well, as you are always carrying Them in your heart. Out of your mercy you have asked Them to step out into the open and take Their places on the altar, so that engaging in Their service can purify us, your lowly servants. At Their feet are the other members of the *paramparā,* who have come just to witness this glorious occasion. On your left is the sitting place of your dedicated disciple, Bhagavān dāsa Goswami, and before you are your disciples and grand disciples, anxious to please you and glorify you to the best of our abilities.

And many generations of people will gather in this very place and hear and chant your astounding feats. The time will doubtless come when everyone on this planet will understand the significance of your appearance and activities. All will accept your unparalleled position as the most effective distributor of God consciousness of all time. Your fame will eclipse the reputations of Muhammad and Christ. The directions of everybody’s lives will be defined by your instructions.

Now we must make it our business to utilize this temple in the way that will please you the most. You chastised your God brothers for turning Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s Vyāsa-pūjā day into merely “a festival of fruits and flowers.” Although the formal observance of the spiritual master’s appearance is undoubtedly of great importance, we pray that we can take to heart the essence of the occasion - that along with the appearance of such a great personality as yourself is the appearance of his mission, and that it is for his mission that he appears.

We pledge ourselves as your property, to be used as you desire in bringing fallen souls to surrender here at your lotus feet. We will work hard to spread your movement far and wide throughout this country until every person of every type is conversant with your message. We are fully confident that this is what will please you the most, and we know that this will be the real crowning glory of this great day.

*Your servants in South Africa,*

## El Salvador

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda*,  
Please accept our most worthless obeisances at your holy feet.

After two years, I’m sending you my humble offering of Vyāsa-pūjā. There is no existing reason to justify my prolonged absence; on the contrary, now I realize that I have offended your lotus feet. Nevertheless, my dear master and preceptor, you ought to know that not even for a moment have I forgotten my obligation towards you and towards the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. I have been thinking of you during all this time, just as I still am now, for I would like to offer you so many harmonious words and fruits of my sincere devotional service. In my past offerings my humble words were based on Lord Caitanya’s words of the potency of the *saṅkīrtana* movement*: pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma.* \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\]

From *Bhagavad-gītā* we have learnt that we have a right to devotional service, but not to the result; nevertheless, I became attached to the results, in a way that when my sandcastle was destroyed by the waves of the sea, I felt ruined. But I took strength from the example shown by you when you came to the West. I have fought again to remake it, to be able to offer you realities, not illusions or unreachable mental plans.

Beloved *Guru*deva, forgive me for not having the humbleness of Sudhama Brāhmaṇa, who offered chipped rice to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. On the contrary, maybe due to my arrogance I have wanted to imitate my spiritual brothers who have been able to offer you the glory of their harmonious work and the opulent physical effect of their devotional service.

Beloved *Ācārya*deva, for all this time I have considered that good fortune has abandoned me, because for the past four years my preaching field has been a battlefield for opposing materialistic forces.

Our temple visitors say that just as the *karmīs* send bombs and mercenaries to this zone, why does ISKCON not send books and preachers, answering the many petitions made to the English-speaking temples?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this odd offering of Vyāsa-pūjā, which totally lacks practical words or harmonious composition. Instead it aims to express our intense attachment and determination to devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, hoping that it may please you completely. You maintain the advancement of this spiritual movement; on the occasion of this auspicious day, when we celebrate the beginning of your transcendental pastimes in this material world, now we wish to ask you that you protect us, because we depend exclusively on your mercy, and you will be in reality the one who will satisfy the demand of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “In every town and village My name will be chanted.”

We are begging to remain always fixed as your eternal servants.

*Your servants of “New Kurukṣetra,” San Salvador Mandira*,

## Fiji

To glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, where can we begin? To glorify a pure devotee of the Lord one has to have the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. When this mercy invokes proper realization, then the activities of such a great personality can be narrated. The difficulty is how to sufficiently glorify someone like Your Divine Grace, whose glory is unlimited and ever expanding.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, every action in your life has been an exhibition of your transcendental qualities and expertise. Practically you are the personification of complete Vedic culture, which culminates in its purest form of love of Godhead. Your unlimited accomplishments in this degraded age must be making even superior beings like demigods wonder at your great, transcendental identity.

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you intimate disciples are revealing a fraction of your unlimited pastimes in a biographical form. Who can fathom the glories of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda?

To explain your position, Śrīla Tamal Kṛṣṇa Goswami once commented, “Śrīla Prabhupāda demonstrated his expertise in all fields of activities - the best in cooking, accounting, managing, etc., because he is a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.” Śrīla Tamal Kṛṣṇa Goswami also commented to devotees in Māyāpur that “Just as among great scientists one scientist is greatest, and among great artists one artist is outstanding, similarly, among all great devotees, Śrīla Prabhupāda stands out as greatest.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, your position is exceptional.

The greatness of our spiritual master is inconceivable to us. Instead of sitting and pondering on each and every respect of your greatness, we pray at your lotus feet for your mercy on us. With clear understanding and knowledge, let us simply surrender unto you in the form of ISKCON, the spiritual master, and your representatives, and thus surrender ourselves in the ocean of nectar you have presented in Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

*Your disciples and grand disciples,*

## Finland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

The essence of spiritual life is to gain he merciful glance of the spiritual master, and that merciful glance of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will not rest until you see this whole world delivered and ascended to the spiritual world. *“Para-duḥkha-duḥkhī kṛpāmbudhiḥ.”* You are very much sorry to see other people suffering, and you cannot rest easy knowing their plight. Kṛṣṇa is compared to the ocean and the spiritual master to the rain cloud. The rain cloud, which is unlimitedly merciful, gets its water from the ocean and pours water unconditionally, not only where there is blazing fire but even in remote corners of the world like Finland, where the people are not so much burning with material desires but instead frozen with cold-heartedness.

Once on a morning walk in London you stopped and cracked some ice with your stick, saying, “Ice is *māyā.* Water is by nature liquid, but now in contact with freezing weather it has become hard like stone. We are originally Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but now we have become hard. The melting process is the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa.”

Our preaching center is in Helsinki. The very name alone speaks for itself - a place where everyone is sinking into hellish existence - but we have seen practically, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that by your preaching tools of *prasādam*, the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* and the distribution of your books, even the frozen hearts of the Finnish people are melting. In fact not only you are melting our hearts, but we see how by pushing forward this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement the influence of Kali-yuga is being checked so much that snowfall is being replaced by your mercy-fall of books dropping on the laps of the conditioned souls. Thus we will soon change the name Helsinki to “Heavensinki.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are magnanimous without reservation, and so we thank you from the very core of our hearts and pray that we may always remember your lotus feet and remain fully surrendered to your divine instructions.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Gītā-nāgarī

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our fallen obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to the anniversary of Your Divine Grace’s supremely auspicious appearance in this world. You have built a house of bhakti, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in which the whole world can live. And the land for that house is your ISKCON farms like Gītā-nāgarī.

By example, and precept, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taught us how to preach at Gītā-nāgarī. Gītā-nāgarī Press - through Śrīla Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami’s *paramparā* books and the ISKCON Farm Newsletters - publishes the precepts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The devotee farmers - by cow protection, ox power, and agriculture - show the example of plain living and high thinking in devotional service. And your *gurukula* grandchildren grow up appreciating both and loving Kṛṣṇa. The world has a bright future, by your divine grace.

Reading *Līlāmṛta*, volume six, recently, we came across this passage: “If the people were disciplined to austerity (of temple life), he said, then let them come to the farm with their wife and children and live comfortably in their own house. And on the farms they would find plenty of employment. More and more, Prabhupāda said, people would become jobless and would be obliged to take shelter of Kṛṣṇa conscious farm community.” (p.221)

If a devotee comes here to retreat into “goodness,” though, he suddenly finds himself in the vanguard of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Gītā-nāgarī, as outlined in your 1948 scheme, is a pioneer Kṛṣṇa conscious village, the keystone of a spiritual society based on plain living and high thinking. That society, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is your ISKCON (the backbone of a respiritualized world-in-the-making), whose charter contains this purpose: “To bring the members closer together for the purpose of teaching a simpler, more natural way of life.”

What could be more natural than living at a holy place like Gītā-nāgarī - “the village where the *Bhagavad-gītā* is sung and lived?” The pure taste of water, the light of the sun and moon, the original fragrance of the earth - all somehow inspire us more in devotional service when we live as Kṛṣṇa lives, working the oxen and protecting the cows.

Gītā-nāgarī is just like a cow, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you, like a cowherd boy, are milking this cow. The milk is the essence of the *Vedas,* and the ISKCON farmer just like a calf. The wise men, the great sages, and the pure devotees are to drink - and distribute - the nectarean milk of your Gītā-nāgarī.

Jaya, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants at Gītā-nāgarī,*
