## New Māyāpura

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your fearless lotus feet. We feel ourselves most fortunate to be engaged in serving you here in the holy *dhāma* of New Māyāpura, which became auspicious due to being touched by your soft lotus feet. On two separate occasions by your presence here you graced this land and all living entities who would take birth in this part of the world for thousands of years. You led us in chanting, invoking the holy name of Kṛṣṇa to descend. It is by your mercy only that today we are regularly chanting the holy names in great ecstasy, in loud *kīrtāna*, accompanied by *karatālas, mṛdaṅgas* and transcendental dancing, as well as daily chanting our sixteen rounds, attempting to clear ourselves of all offenses.

By your grace also, we have the opportunity to daily serve their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, who so kindly appeared here by your transcendental call alone. How wonderfully astonishing it is that they brought you here and then you brought Them. This was your transcendental pastime wherever you traveled. Now They have been joined by Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda-Mādhava, who are so beautiful and all-attractive that They steal the hearts of whoever sees Them. We feel ourselves very fortunate to be engaged in their service and yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Here also is Giri-Govardhana, who was given to us by you. He is being served very nicely with daily offerings of sweetly scented flower petals and Tulasī leaves, and He is seated on a golden throne.

You are here also, Śrīla Prabhupāda, seated at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. You are playing *karatālas* and chanting the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. Your radiant face appears very pure, and you are fully dependent on the mercy of Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda and Their most confidential servants, in whose presence you are rightfully seated. It is very difficult to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this world, where the people are almost all devoted to *māyā* and averse to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But you very humbly came here, sat down and preached, and tried to convince us to surrender to Kṛṣṇa. You explained Kṛṣṇa to all who would hear. Your attempt was wonderfully successful. We have all been completely convinced by your transcendental qualities, dealings, mercy, chanting and preaching.

You once said, “The disciple is eternally indebted to his spiritual master. The debt to the spiritual master can never be repaid. But if you want to try to repay your debt to me, then preach vigorously, as I have.” The devotees at New Māyāpura Dhāma are fully dedicated to this instruction, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You once told us, “Go out in the villages here, give people Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” By your mercy, we have become the most fortunate. So now we have to make others fortunate.

When you visited New Māyāpura, you gave us many instructions on how to develop the project here. We promised to work harder than ever before this year, to try to understand and carry out all your instructions. Certainly one of the most significant came one day when you remarked, “When I look out my window up here, and I see so many boys and girls taking *prasādam* and chanting, my heart feels very satisfied because I know I have a place where I can give so many people shelter.”

You will be happy to know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that gradually more and more people, fatigued by material life, are coming to this transcendental community and are finding the shelter you envisioned for them, which comes only by strictly following your instructions. The devotees are trying to make this a very happy and famous community by profusely distributing your books and preaching. The real offering of a disciple to his spiritual master is how he accomplishes in a wonderful way the mission of his spiritual master. We vow that this year we will follow your instructions so carefully and preach so vigorously that this New Māyāpura community will appear to the people of France like the sun wonderfully rising in the midst of Kali’s dark night.

Finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the devotees here want to thank you for what we consider your greatest gift. You gave us so many temples, the Deity of Kṛṣṇa, *prasādam*, the holy name, your books - so many gifts, but the most important of all for which we want to glorify you and thank you, infinitely, is the association of a pure devotee, Śrīla Bhagavān Goswami Mahārāja, who as your representative is continuing in your absence to inspire and guide us as you would like.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to your wonderful contributions to this world, which would have been meaningless without your all-auspicious appearance. All glories, all glories to you Śrīla Prabhupāda - ISKCON Founder*-Ācārya, Jagad-guru* ki jaya!

*Your disciples and grand disciples at New Māyāpura Dhāma*

## New Orleans

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Whenever we stop and try to imagine our whereabouts, had you not descended to this awful place, our minds feel, and we shudder at the thought. We were like piteous, bereaved mongrels, strayed from home into the bowels of some depraved, squalid, impersonal city, running down dark alleyways of promised enjoyment, hurtling headlong toward repeated dead ends of horror and despair; shamelessly begging from door to door searching for a master but finding only those who would seek to subjugate us to further kill our souls; sometimes crying for good counsel and unfailingly receiving the intoxicated braying of puffed-up asses disguised as pundits. In this reoccurring nightmare, from which there seemed to be no release, into the darkest corner of the universe where the hopeless congregate to pace through meaningless Kali-yuga lives, the Supreme Lord sent a miracle in the form of the incarnation of His own mercy. You appeared, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just like the cooling moon to erase the pain of a million hellish births; to wipe our tears and brace us upright again; to give us the courage and the hope which we had forgotten for so, so long; to give us back ourselves, our long-lost identities as servants of the Lord; to give us the chanting of the holy name; to wake us from our horrible dream of death and give us Kṛṣṇa, the life of our lives. And there is no justice we can do to properly express our gratitude or the wondrousness of that moment when we understood that we were being rescued from this place. Up till then we did not know if anyone was there to hear our wailings into space.

Prabhupāda, you are more than everything to us. There are no words to describe how we feel. On this auspicious day we fervently pray, “Please never let us forget your lotus feet. Please allow us to remain your servants for ever and ever.”

*Your aspiring servants of New Orleans ISKCON,*

## New Vrindaban

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It is Kṛṣṇa’s eternal promise that whenever and wherever there is a decline in religious practice, and a predominant rise of irreligion - at that time He descends Himself.

*yadā yadā hi dharmasya*  
*glānir bhavati bhārata*  
*abhyutthānam adharmasya*  
*tadātmānaṁ sṛjāmy aham*  
*(Bg. 4.7)*

**”Because the living entities are My servants and I am their Lord, if I do not help them, they will never get free from material entanglement.”**

Thinking in this way, Lord Caitanya advented Himself to freely distribute love for Kṛṣṇa without consideration of who is suitable or not, and without consideration of time, place or circumstance. Soon after His disappearance, however, His *dhāma* - Māyāpur - was lost under the Ganges river. The saṅkīrtana movement became almost lost, and Kali-yuga again flourished.

But Lord Nityānanda predicted that in the future one great devotee would rediscover the *dhāma*, and devotees would again build temples there. He also predicted that one especially wonderful temple would appear, from which Lord Caitanya’s *vāṇī* would establish the religious principle for the world. Later, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura predicted that soon one great personality would spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have fulfilled these predictions. We see that you are empowered by Lord Caitanya to fulfill His desires of spreading the holy names to every town and village of the world. You finally carried out that mission, predicted by Lord Nityānanda, begun by Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and ordered by Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

You were living as a saint in Vṛndāvana. Then at the age of sixty-nine on the order of your spiritual master, you left Vṛndāvana and displayed the compassion of Prahlāda Mahārāja, who said, “My dear Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, I see that there are many saintly persons indeed, but they are interested only in their deliverance. Not caring for the big cities and towns, they go to the Himalayas or the forest to meditate with vows of silence (*mauna-vrata*). They are not interested in delivering others. As for me, however, I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals. I know that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without taking shelter of Your lotus feet, one cannot be happy. Therefore, I wish to bring them back to shelter at Your lotus feet.”

When you first appeared in New York, chanting and preaching the glories of the Supreme Lord, no one would accept your message. But by your conviction, devotion, and pure expression of love to Kṛṣṇa, gradually you stole the hearts of your followers. You induced them to assist you more and more in your great mission, and soon they achieved the ecstatic state of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* devotees.

We, the residents of New Vrindaban, cannot claim any special qualification for your mercy. Rather, our lack of qualification was the very reason for our coming here. Still, we feel we are receiving your greatest mercy, and this is very wonderful. Your mercy is descending from Lord Caitanya, the deliverer of the most fallen; therefore it is like a great river that flows especially towards those who are the most spiritually thirsty.

The first time you came to New Vrindaban, you blessed this land as a *tīrtha* and ordered us to build seven temples for the glorification of Kṛṣṇa. But first, Kṛṣṇa made the arrangement to manifest a glorious palace for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And now thousands of people are coming to take your *darśana.*

Although your *vapuḥ* has turned to *vāṇī*, we still experience your association and guidance. You are manifest in your Palace of Gold and reside there as the king of the *brāhmaṇas*. There we worship you, because without your mercy none of us would have a chance of obtaining the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.

Lord Nityānanda was once thinking, “As long as Jagāi and Mādhāi reside in Nadīyā, I won’t go anywhere else to preach.” But you left Vṛndāvana and traveled around the world fourteen times, searching out and saving the Jagāis and Mādhāis of this world. We have never seen this kind of mercy and compassion in any personality, nor have we heard of it in any scripture. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to give us just one drop of your great ocean of mercy. Your mercy is all we are made of.

*The inmates of New Vrindaban,*

## New York

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

When you sat in your room in Vṛndāvana, you would see the *samādhi-mandira* and *bhajana-kuṭira* of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī from your window. Contemplating with great depth and realization, you would carefully write and edit your own purports on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, which you based on the commentaries of the predecessor *ācāryas*.

The words of your spiritual master were one with your heart, and you would consider how you would go to the West and spread *bhāgavata-dharma* all over the world. You wanted to go to New York. Others had gone to London and Germany, but they had accomplished little, so you were thinking how you could go to New York and from there spread your movement all over the world.

After a year of hardship and ordeal in America, gradually some New Yorkers came forward to become your followers. Kīrtanānanda Swami, Satsvarūpa das Goswami, Brahmānanda Swami, Mukunda Mahārāja, Harikeśa Swami, Jayādvaita Swami, etc., etc. (the list is endless), all joined in New York. Who would have thought that a *vṛndāvana-sādhu* could come to such a hellish place and develop it as such a wonderful preaching field? But you are no ordinary *vṛndāvana-sādhu*, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In Vṛndāvana there are many *sādhus*, but they go there in old age to prepare for death. There is no question of leaving Vṛndāvana for them, not even to go to Agra, what to speak of Delhi. What then to speak of their going to New York! But you always thought of coming here to New York to deliver us from the clutches of *māyā*. O, Śrīla Prabhupāda, what a wonderful thing you have done for us! Let us therefore surrender unto your lotus feet.

People wonder why the ISKCON devotees are so attached to you as their all-in-all. But the sincerity of the genuine devotee is his attachment to the *guru* as the life and soul of disciple. As Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura writes, “The mercy of the spiritual master is all that I am made of.” This is especially the mood of the Western devotees, because you brought Kṛṣṇa consciousness from Vṛndāvana for us. You taught us how to cook, how to eat, how to worship, how to chant, and even how to wear a *dhotī.* Everything that we know about spiritual life comes from you! You taught us what is real and what is bogus, and you demanded such a high standard that we had to work until we collapsed.

The conclusion is that we do not know anyone but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This mood is expressed nicely as follows: “I do not know Kṛṣṇa, I do not even know Lord Caitanya or Lord Nityānanda. I do not know anything. All that I know is the mercy of my spiritual master.” In that mood, we pray for your blessings that we may ever increasingly please you.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your unlimited mercy!

*Your humble disciples and grand disciples in Rādhā-Govinda Mandira,*

## Norman, Oklahoma

*My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances.

You have so kindly picked me up out of the stool of hippie life, giving me the real happy life. I am eternally indebted to serve you with every ounce of my physical, vocal, and mental energy. The best service I can render is to as far as possible propagate your transcendental teachings for the upliftment of the fallen conditioned souls. I must always, twenty four hours daily, be pushing myself “to the max,” squeezing as much service out of myself as possible to be made as an offering unto you. Especially it is my duty to successfully distribute your books as widely as possible. I am very enlivened going out on the college campus and door to door dressed as a devotee for preaching and distributing your books. I pray that I may be steady and successful in this and may be able to convince people to purchase entire sets of your books. Many years ago, in response to a question I had written you, you sent me a copy of a letter you had written another devotee, wherein you stated, “I am convinced that if you simply glorify Kṛṣṇa and our books in the best descriptive manner, that anyone and everyone, no matter even atheist or otherwise, they can be convinced to purchase.” So with full faith in your instruction, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I dedicate my life to regularly going out to preach and distribute your books. I know that by taking this burden upon myself of delivering the fallen, conditioned souls that my life will be free of anxiety.

These words are not really a suitable offering. The real Vyāsa-pūjā homage is to distribute your books as widely as possible.

So that book distribution may increase, I am praying for your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that many devotees can be made who can then dedicate their lives to distributing your books and making more devotees who can distribute your books and make more devotees who can distribute your books and make more devotees who can distribute your books and make more devotees who can distribute your books and make more devotees - on and on ad infinitum.

Please bless us, you spiritual children and grandchildren, that we may be successful in making our quota of turning the whole world to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We can never love you enough, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we can always endeavor to serve you with ever-increasing vigor.

*Your servants in the land of Okies*

## Ottawa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our small offering of gratitude to Your Divine Grace on this auspicious day of your glorious appearance in this world.

Your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is now clear.  
To mail a letter in India is a difficult task,  
Yet you built great temples,  
That continue to last.

In the Western countries sold out to maya  
You built even more temples,  
Your spiritual master’s desire.

Anyone who has tried even to write a line  
Knows the difficulty in trying in this Kali time.  
But you wrote eighty volumes on transcendental knowledge  
And arranged translation and distribution in global volume.

When you saw the children being educationally slaughtered,  
You instituted a school system  
Based on Vedic knowledge.

Whey you saw the hungry, you distributed food  
Offered to Kṛṣṇa, blessed by guru.  
Returning the land to a God-conscious state.  
You opened farms with varṇāśrama to solve the world’s wastes.

When you saw the drugged and hallucinating youth,  
You taught them to chant and gave them the truth.  
Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is nothing without you.  
Whatever we say, whatever we do,  
With thousands of tongues we cannot glorify you.

We pray that the world and all its people,  
We pray that Lord Kṛṣṇa and all of His sweetness,  
Are both brought together by kīrtana and dance,  
As Lord Caitanya’s mission continues to advance.

All by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your fallen servants of Ottawa Yātrā,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Ghoṣa Ṭhākura dāsa / Nīla-Mādhava dāsa / Girijā-devī dāsī / Padyāvali-devī dāsī

## Pakistan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the inexhaustible gem of spiritual wish-fulfillment. Can anyone be happy in this miserable age of impersonalism without contacting your lotus feet? We think not, and therefore - if we have any compassion - we must give you to everyone. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are the panacea for suffering humanity, and you have created ISKCON as a transcendental dispensary to make sure that everyone gets his prescription filled.

”In our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement,” you wrote in 1977, “all our activities are concentrated upon distributing Kṛṣṇa literature. This is very important. One may approach any person and induce him to read Kṛṣṇa literature so that in the future he also may become a devotee... Such activities will always remind the devotees of the Lord’s lotus feet. By fully concentrating on distributing books for Kṛṣṇa, one is fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa. This is *samādhi*.” (*Bhāg*. 10.3.37purport)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the demons have now become very afraid of your book distribution, and we see that they are especially trying to stop your American BBT. But they are too late. Your books have already defeated the so-called scientists’ materialistic mythology that “life comes from matter,” and you have already exposed that the “democracy” of power-mad Kali-yuga politicians is really “demon-crazy,” a system to rob and cheat innocent citizens. Your *kīrtanas* and *prasādam* have already proven that real happiness and pleasure do not require sinful sense gratification, and your farm communities have practically demonstrated that simple living and high thinking bring the freedom and prosperity which “economic development” cannot. As your books are distributed more and more, *Vaiṣṇava-rājarṣis* will come forward to rebuild their nations as God-centered *varṇāśrama* societies on the solid foundation of your books, and your Bhaktivedanta Institute scientists will establish the science of *bhakti* as the proper subject of human education.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we cannot understand how you are so merciful to allow us to take part in this wonderful transcendental movement of yours, which is your own body and which is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. We can only beg at your lotus feet, on this auspicious day when you personally must be listening to our Vyāsa-pūjā offerings, “Please do not kick us away, although we deserve it. We will carefully chant our rounds, we will follow the rules you have given us, and we will concentrate all our activities on distribution of your books. Thus we will give up all selfish considerations and will remain tightly fixed up together at your lotus feet, and thus your ISKCON will quickly conquer the universe.”

*Your servants in Prahlāda-deśa*

## France

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It is a well-known fact that the vast majority of people in this country have grown accustomed to a very poor standard of cleanliness. They bathe very irregularly, and to compensate for this, they have mastered the art of making various types of perfumes and colognes. Thus they never remove the filth; they simply hide it beneath a thin veneer of artificial fragrance. In the same way, the people of this once-reputed “glorious” nation, like everywhere else in *māyā’s* kingdom, enjoy seeing themselves as aristocrats, preoccupied with and expert at obtaining the most sophisticated and refined forms of sense gratification. Consequently, the filth of the bodily concept of life and ignorance of devotional service are hidden beneath a thin veneer of wealth, beauty, heritage, and education. One may observe this by walking through the corridors and chambers of the Louver, the most famous art museum in the world, only to observe that it is almost exclusive glorification of the female form.

Certainly this ignorance, the cause of sinful life and suffering, pained you to see, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And like Lord Kṛṣṇa you were intolerant of the unhappiness found here as a result of such ignorance. You blessed this country as you did so many others with the pure teachings of Lord Caitanya and the transcendental sound vibration of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name. You stole their hearts and captivated their minds with the exquisite beauty of their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Parisīśvara, and you soothed their minds and intelligence so frustrated by useless philosophies and speculations with your immaculate presentations of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā.*

All the devotees here in New Dvārakā Purī would like to firmly dedicate their lives solely and wholly to the mission which you began here, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in France many years ago. Under the expert guidance and shelter of your dedicated disciple, Śrīla Bhagavān dāsa Goswami, we vow to work incessantly for the rest of our lives attempting to transform their unlimited desires for sense gratification into pure love for Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants,*

## Perth

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Who else could to what you have done?  
Your love for the conditioned souls so pure,  
Your compassion shining like the sun.  
Prabhupāda, you have the cure.

Kṛṣṇa’s name has gone far and wide,  
Many souls are being reclaimed.  
Prabhupāda, I’m on your side,  
And Kali-yuga is being tamed.

Lord Caitanya’s divine mission  
To deliver us back to home  
Became your life’s ambition.  
Prabhupāda, you were the chosen one.

Leaving Vṛndāvana, the holy land,  
You ventured to the West.  
Prabhupāda, you took us by the hand  
And led us to Kṛṣṇa, who is the best.

For one year you struggled all alone,  
No shelter but the holy name.  
Prabhupāda, you had no home,  
Then one by one your disciples came.

To all the corners of the world  
This mahā-mantra has spread.  
Hare Kṛṣṇa is now a household word,  
And impersonalism is dead.

O Prabhupāda, divine swanlike sage,  
Kali-yuga’s darkness you have lit.  
You have ushered in the Golden Age.  
At you feet all masters sit.

*Your eternal servants in Perth,*

**Grand disciples**

Puruṣottama dāsa / Dvijadeva dāsa / Mahā-buddhi dāsa / Bhakta Steve / Ambikā-devī dāsī / Pṛthā-devī dāsī

## Philadelphia

**A Garland of Prayers at the Lotus Feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I write with a singular voice to express what all your devoted followers feel within their hearts. You are the most merciful Vaiṣṇava great soul who entered *māyā’s* stronghold, and in an amazing display of transcendental daring you saved me from the unrelenting wheel of repeated birth and death. In that attempt you suffered many inconveniences and setbacks on my behalf. May I never forget my indebtedness to you. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

The vision of you absorbed in love of Kṛṣṇa is enough to cause a wayward conditioned soul to regret all his past misdeeds and accept your mercy as his life’s all in all. Any contact with you, even indirectly, brings all auspiciousness into a conditioned soul’s life, and he feels soothing relief from the vice like grip of the illusory potency. Only an unalloyed devotee like you can give such a perfect benediction. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Once when someone praised your worldwide preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you replied, “What did I do? I am like Arjuna. Kṛṣṇa said, ‘You go and fight this battle.’ It was already decided, but because Arjuna was a devotee, Kṛṣṇa wanted him to get the credit. So Caitanya Mahāprabhu wanted this movement spread, and I did my duty. That is all.” Bless me Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I may have the same dutiful attitude in my service to Lord Caitanya’s mission. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You are the dearmost of the dear devotees of the Supreme Lord. A mere hint of your satisfaction is sufficient to deliver any wretched conditioned soul into Kṛṣṇa’s good grace for all eternity. Kṛṣṇa is captivated and controlled by your love. Who can understand even a fraction of the intimacy you share with Him? My entire existence hangs on the hope that some day you will be pleased to give Him your nod of approval in my regard. Thus I may be accepted as one of the liberated souls who serve Him perpetually in transcendental bliss. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Who could understand the depth of your love for Lord Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu and the intense transcendental emotions your incomparable service to Him must have awakened? The *śāstras* enumerate the glories of many great and astonishing devotees of the Lord, but none of them equal or surpass you. Indeed, you are the master at whose feet all others sit. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

By the power of your causeless mercy the most miraculous deed was done. You changed the hearts of thousands of entities who had no idea of the purpose of life, nor were they even desiring to find out. Now they are engaged in your devotional service with purified hearts. My heart is a storehouse of material desires. All these desires are detrimental and worthless to me. By the power of your causeless mercy, let all these material desires go unfulfilled. Let them just wither, dry up, and die. Let my only desire be to worship and serve your lotus feet life after life. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

With you as our commander in chief, we neither feared nor cared for any opposition to our preaching. Nothing could stop our “fighting spirit” for preaching. Though we met many obstacles, a few inspiring words from you - even a few humorous quips from your lotus lips - was all it took to dispel any gloom, doubt or reluctance we may have felt. And we always relished seeing you pleased with the results. You told us, “If you preach and there is opposition, that means your preaching is effective.” May you continue to lead us in the same undaunted manner from within our hearts. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Your only concern, you said, was “that they do not waste their valuable human form of life.” Such a noble desire, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In the deep recesses of my heart I know that I, too, should make that my only concern. But I am so feebleminded and miserly that I am not even concerned about wasting my own life! Forgive my foolishness, and help me to rise out of dense darkness of self-pity, fear, illusion, lamentation, attachment, and laziness. Do not hesitate to chastise me until I willfully accept my position as the servant of the servant. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You are the most merciful representative of the most merciful incarnation of Godhead, yet in your absence some opportunities have emerged who seek by conniving words and party spirit to undo your work and obscure your glories. Thus I have learnt something of the treacherous nature of the material world. Help me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to feel compassion for the sorry plight of these proud and envious people. I pray that all those who desire to love you may go unsullied by the devious ploys of these over intelligent persons. May your faithful followers be always protected by you and forever bask in the limelight of your mercy. I offer my humble obeisances again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You said our love for you will be shown not by sweet words and praises, not by offerings of fruits and flowers, not by a competitive spirit reinforced by pride and personal ambition, but by how well we cooperate to serve you. “Personal ambition,” you said, “will ruin everything.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to be counted amongst those who love you. Please give me strength to endure whatever slings and arrows I must in order to love you and cooperate with your devotees. Let me suffer my lot gladly and willingly for the reward of loving you. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I have so little realization of you, so little realization of your mission, so little desire to serve only you, and so little love for you; how will I be able to follow your peerless example of selfless dedication to the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Gaurāṅga? Many claim to be doing good for others, but only you have given the highest good: chanting and preaching the glories of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You dispensed the prime benediction for all humanity and smiled in transcendental glee to see the result. Your mind and body thrilled in ecstasy as you watched your “white elephants” bathe and frolic in the *kīrtana* of the holy name. How I long to see you again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I yearn to see you again with your steady, regal stride leading your band of faithful followers out of the fearful, dark forest of material existence to the safe shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s reddish lotus feet. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You taught that the highest position is to become the servant of the devotees. I hanker so much to be respected as a great devotee, but I have great difficulty feeling myself as the servant of the devotees. Instead I foolishly think the devotees to be my servants. Be patient with me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Teach me that lesson again and again. If you forsake me in this cold, lonely material world, what recourse will I have? Without your merciful glance upon me, my existence would be null and void. I offer my humble obeisances to you again and again. Kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon my head.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please heed my prayer. You are a great devotee of the Lord. Whatever you desire becomes a fact, because Kṛṣṇa fulfills the desires of His great devotees. By your grace and desire may these prayers be surcharged with spiritual potency so that whoever reads them, hears them, repeats them, or remembers them will experience an ever-increasing desire to serve your preaching mission and become overwhelmingly attached to your lotus feet. And, may you kindly place your soft, soothing lotus feet upon our heads.

*Your disciples and grand disciples at New Nīlācala,*

## Philo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

The disciple should always feel himself to be the insignificant servant of his spiritual master. There is nothing in his life but the causeless mercy of his spiritual master. He owns nothing, has accomplished nothing, and aspires for nothing but humble, unmotivated service to his spiritual master. I regret to say that most of this is only theory to me. I am living in a world of duality. The distance between my understanding and my life is very wide and very deep.

But on this auspicious day let us reflect on your causeless mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let us reflect that, despite our most innate disqualifications, there is in fact nothing in our lives but your most generous personal gifts. Despite our deep-rooted Kali-yuga madness, your ever-well-wishing kindness is stringing together our hopes, loves and very lives, like so many pearls on a thread.

We are living on a hundred and sixty acres of breathtaking, soul stirring land - enough land to raise children, cows, crops, temples, and cities on. Is this not your gift to us? The place, the people, the possibilities - it’s all the direct emanation from your profound surrender to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. Are we living in a political world of barter and bargain, or are we living in a spiritual world of causeless mercy and surrender? You have given us true mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Had you not given your life to deliver Ātreya Ṛṣi prabhu, Bhakti-svarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja, Madhusūdana prabhu, and so many others, how would we be here worshiping Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and chanting the holy names?

Let us pray to have the eyes to see your causeless mercy in our lives, day to day, moment to moment; in our dealings with one another (your parts and parcels); in all our successes and failures. And through our faltering attempts to render humble, unmotivated service to you, may your vision for a Kṛṣṇa conscious world unfold, and be pleasing to you.

*Your servants at Gaura-maṇḍala-giri (Philo, California)*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Port Harcourt

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

All glories to your lotus feet. All glories to the *ācāryas.*

By your causeless mercy, through your foremost disciple Śrīla Kīrtanānanda Swami, you have made it possible for fallen conditioned souls like us to become your grand-spiritual disciples. Please accept this, our humble offering of your eternal glorification, on this auspicious day of your appearance. The *saṅkīrtana* movement you initiated is really expanding to engulf the entire universe. The day-to-day realizations of the potency of this movement have given us the courage and the direction to preach the transcendental message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu against all odds.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have actually fulfilled the desire of your most dear *Guru*deva. In every town and every village practically throughout the world, and especially in Nigeria, devotees are amazed to see how you are still preaching so vigorously through your transcendental literatures. Nothing can surpass this sublime knowledge you have given the world from the archives of India’s great spiritual wisdom. You have virtually delivered Lord Kṛṣṇa at the doorstep of every conditioned soul. We only pray to Your Divine Grace to bless Śrīla Kīrtanānanda Swami to effectively steer us across the ocean of birth and death. Not only that, but also to catch as many souls as possible into this transcendental eternal boat you have built single-handedly.

How can we serve Śrīla Kīrtanānanda Swami to satisfy Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Oh! When shall we lose attachment to material sense gratification and attach ourselves to the transcendental loving service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa? We are at a loss to know. Until you give us your causeless mercy through the transparent via medium of our dear spiritual master, we are lost.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *jagad-guru* of the entire universe. Your divine name will be glorified along with that of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the three worlds. You are the most dear to Kṛṣṇa because you have been able to explain this supreme secret to the devotees. Not only the *suras*, but also the *asuras* like us have also had the chance to enter into this sublime subject matter. We are very grateful, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but unfortunately, rascals as we are, we cannot surrender. *Māyā* is so strong to overcome. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *mahā*-captain of that transcendental boat you have built yourself. Please, with fatherly love take us across this ocean that has always hindered our progress back home, back to Godhead.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are fully inviting you to the humble ISKCON temple in Port Harcourt in the heart of Africa. When will you arrive so as to enable us to prepare the garlands and also give you the red-carpet reception that you received in other parts of the world? Will you come by plane or by sea? Or will Your Divine Grace risk the desert and jungles of Africa and traverse the “Dark Continent” by land? Your grand disciples are pleading for your divine association. So what should we do, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have a dream. Why not help us create a transcendental home for you in our hearts? Yes, that is the brightest idea so far. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that is not so easy either. Our hearts are as black as natural coal. There are no clean spots to rest your *vyāsāsana* on. What a shame! O Māyā, thou art very cruel! Why prevent us from our grand-master’s association? But lo! A voice is speaking! Śrīla Prabhupāda is speaking. Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, pardon us. We cannot hear you well. You mean you have solved the problem for us? A ray of hope indeed!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you mean we should place our individual vyāsāsana in the heart of Śrīla Kīrtanānanda? That’s wonderful. And, Śrīla Prabhupāda, what do we do next? Oh! You mean we should serve you through service to our spiritual master? Wonderful. You are simply wonderful. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the benediction moon, shining your soothing rays on every conditioned soul.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have shown us the way. We will try to make the effort and serve you through our dear spiritual master, Śrīla Kīrtanānda Swami.

*From your worthless servants,*

**Grand disciples**

Mahā-mantra dāsa / Mādhava dāsa / Bhagavata dāsa / Mādrī-devī dāsī / Bhakta Paul / Bhakta Boniface / Bhakta Michael / Bhakta Benjamin / Bhakta Victor / Bhakta Clement / Bhakta Asipha / Bhakta Shah / Bhaktin Grace / Bhakta Rava & family / Bhakta Bhadwaja & family / Bhakta Ram Shuvani & family

## Potomac

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I wish I could have been there  
When you appeared that autumn morn;  
I would have somehow welcomed you  
To this world of souls forlorn.

Amidst the roar of world events,  
Your advent seemed so small;  
But in your heart you bore the mace  
To conquer māyā’s thrall.

I wish I could have been there  
When you frolicked as a boy;  
I would have bought you something nice,  
Like a bike or string-pulled toy.

I would have seen you growing strong  
Within your parent’s care;  
Full faith in Kṛṣṇa nurturing,  
Heedless of māyā’s glare.

I wish I could have been there  
With your class in college days;  
I would have carried books for you  
Or served in other ways.

I would have seen your Vaiṣṇava faith  
Hold strong before the test;  
No college books or Gandhian ways  
Would keep you from your quest.

I wish I could have been there  
When you first saw Purī’s streets;  
I would have fetched the Lord’s prasādam  
From the shops that sell His sweets.

I wonder now - perhaps you knew  
Someday the Lord would come;  
Lord Jagannātha and Baladeva,  
In the San Francisco sun.

I wish I could have been there  
As you rode the trains alone;  
I would have carried all your bags  
And kept nothing of my own.

How dutifully you bore the weight,  
Like one attached to wife;  
But in your heart you kept the form  
of Him who gave you life.

I wish I could have been there  
As you formed your worldwide league;  
Through Jhansi’s streets I’d do your work  
To relieve you of fatigue.

Both Sastri and the Prabhakar,  
Though fond, gave little aid;  
They’d surely stand in wonder now  
To see the house you’ve made.

I wish I could have been there  
When you boldly left your wife;  
I would have cheered to see you free  
To bring the world to life.

Though moralists might criticize  
You broke from worldly ties;  
Without remorse you left it all  
And came before our eyes.

I wish I could have been there  
When you moved to Vrindavan;  
I would have seen your manuscripts  
As soon as they were done.

Bringing forth the Bhāgavata  
From Vedic ages past;  
In densest Kali-yuga night  
You shed the light at last.

I wish I could have been there  
To see you board the ship -  
An old sage bringing love of God  
To lower East Side’s hip.

Determination marked your face;  
There was no doubt you’d win;  
Delivering the Western world  
From wickedness and sin.

I wish I could have been there  
At Boston’s sea-worn pier;  
I would have shown you everything -  
The things we held so dear.

No doubt you must have been appalled  
By the wasted lives you saw;  
A land of stark materialists,  
Mindless of Kṛṣṇa’s law.

I wish I could have been there  
When you finally found your place,  
A front on Second Avenue,  
A start - by Kṛṣṇa’s grace.

I would have seen you raise your child  
With tenderness and care,  
To grow to full maturity,  
For preaching everywhere.

I wish I could have been there  
As you toured around the world;  
A few short years, so many miles,  
Your massive plans unfurled.

I would have seen you every day  
Through morning walks and class;  
To grasp your words within my heart  
And never let them pass.

I wish I could have been there  
When you called us to your side;  
To bring our love to fever pitch,  
With nothing left to hide.

I would have seen your wondrous act  
Of going to your Lord;  
So strikingly you made your point!  
I could not have ignored.

I wish I could be with you now,  
At home in Kṛṣṇa’s land;  
I beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Extend your lotus hand!

*Your servants in New Hastināpura-dhāma,*

## Puebla

*Our Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We know that the perfection of transcendental life can be achieved simply by touching the holy dust of your lotus feet. In the *Bhāgavatam* it is said, *mahat-pāda-rajo-‘bhiṣekam,* which means to be blessed by the holy dust of the lotus feet of a great devotee. Therefore we pray,

*Ohe!*  
*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura doyāra sāgara*  
*e dāse koruṇā kori*  
*kiyā pada-chāyā śodha he āmāya*  
*tomāra caraṇ dhori*

**”O Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, Ocean of mercy, be merciful to us your servants, and purify ourselves by the shade of your lotus feet, your lotus feet we humbly hold.”**

As stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā, mahātmānas tu:* those who are great souls like yourself are under the spell of spiritual energy. Their symptoms are that they fully engage in Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the service of the Lord. Therefore you are called mahātmā. Unless we are fortunate enough to have the dust of your lotus feet on our heads, we know there is no possibility of perfection in spiritual life.

We know the *paramparā* system of disciplic succession is very important as a means of spiritual success. One cannot be fixed in this succession without your divine grace. If one takes shelter at your lotus feet, there is every possibility of becoming successful in spiritual life.

When Mahārāja Rahūgaṇa asked Jaḍa Bharata about his wonderful achievement of spiritual success, Jaḍa Bharata replied to the king that spiritual success is not possible simply by following the rituals of religion or simply by converting oneself into a *sannyāsī* or offering sacrifices as recommended in the scriptures. These methods are undoubtedly helpful for spiritual realization, but the real effect is brought about by the grace of a *mahātmā* like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s eight stanzas of prayers to the spiritual master, it is clearly stated that simply by satisfying the spiritual master one can achieve the supreme success in life. If one cannot satisfy the spiritual master, one has no access to spiritual perfection. It is our duty to seek shelter of your lotus feet, and if we try to please you, that will make our life perfect, because we cannot rise to the topmost stage of spiritual perfection simply by following the rituals and religious principles. Therefore we take shelter at your lotus feet, and we try to work under your direction faithfully and sincerely. We know that is the perfection of our life, without a doubt.

*Your humble servants of ISKCON Puebla Mandira*

## Puerto Rico

We offer our prostrated obeisances at the lotus feet of our beloved spiritual master His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It was not so long ago that each of us was just one of countless conditioned souls undergoing the ”hard struggle,” while enduring a sterile, meaningless existence. As we reflect on the poignant history of our tortured lives, we now realize we were veritably in hell. Steeped n ignorance, we were bent on enjoying the hellish pleasures of a godless life, unwittingly paving the path to increased bondage. According to cause and effect, we were sure to sustain unimaginable suffering.

Now, however, that has all been changed - changed by the merciful intervention of Your Divine Grace, our beloved spiritual master. By some superior arrangement you entered into our lives and altered the course. You so compassionately halted our headlong plunge toward increased degradation and initiated our first step back home, back to Godhead. That dark most chapter of our lives is over and gone forever. Within one profound moment you spawned within us the most wonderful transformation; your sublime presence melted our stone like hearts, and we became receptive to hear the truth - loving devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. At that moment the whole universe appeared to unfold, revealing its divine plan. What was previously a meaningless place of death took on significance and the quality of life. Everything appeared resplendent in the transcendental light of *bhāgavata-dharma*. No longer were we looking through the “glass darkly.” We were now seeing through the eyes of a self-realized saint. With his every penetrating word, the purpose of life, devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, became increasingly more luminous and clear. It was then that we were delivered from the cruel charge of the material world to the protective shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

Now our future is brilliant. Our lives are filled with such happiness and love that we sometimes think we must be dreaming. This has all been possible only by your unlimited mercy. Remembering that distinguished moment, that first contact with Your Divine Grace, moves us to tears - tears of love and gratitude for you who have saved us. Some of us first met you many years ago, others barely weeks ago. Yet, each of us is struck with wonder at the extraordinary power of your association and by the unfathomable ocean of mercy that you’ve shown us. It is by this mercy alone that all of us, senior and junior alike, are happily situated on the same boat - your ISKCON, under the able guidance of the same captain - Your Divine Grace, and proceeding toward the same destination, loving devotional service at the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the eternal truth of *kṛṣṇa-prema* pervades your every thought, word and action. Although we are most undeserving, you have allowed us to catch a glimpse of this truth, and it has overwhelmed us with love for you. Our only prayer is that we may become fully surrendered souls at your lotus feet. We want only to assist you eternally in your glorious *saṅkīrtana pastimes,* following you wherever you may go.

Your grateful disciples and grand disciples in Puerto Rico,

## Rio de Janeiro

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet.

Your appearance in this world is the principle event that took place since the departure of the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The fact that mundane history does not register this event in its full magnitude clearly proves what is explained in the scriptures: ordinary minds cannot understand God’s plans.

When the worship of the Supreme Being becomes degenerated and turned to mere ritualistic ceremony, religion changes into formality, and in this way the purpose of life simply becomes a search for enjoyment in this world. That materialistic mentality reaches perfection in the worship of demigods, and it covers the true meaning of the scriptures, which can lead us to bodily detachment and establish Lord Hari, the Lord in the heart, as the conclusion, motivation, and goal of life.

Because of that, the Lord appeared in the form of Buddha teaching dry philosophy but attracting the people back to spiritual life. However, there was an enormous and empty abyss, which was changed into an ocean of nectar by His next incarnation, the *Mahā-vandānyāvatāra,* with a complexion like molten gold - Gaura-Hari Caitanya, who over flooded the world with the torrential rain of pure love for Kṛṣṇa.

At that time, the Western world was lost in the decay of ignorance. People there were becoming frustrated in their search for pleasure, and so appeared fabulous and legendary Oriental mystics, attracting the minds of people unto spiritual life, but also without delivering the ultimate goal, love of Godhead. At that crucial moment, with the same merciful inspiration of Lord Gaurāṅga, you appeared and disclosed the greatest mystery to the materialistic and godless world: Kṛṣṇa, service for Kṛṣṇa and love for Kṛṣṇa.

In one of your purports of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* you explain that the imperfect knower simply speculates about the Absolute Truth, but the perfect knower engages himself directly in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, devotional service to the Supreme Lord.

So it is obvious that spiritual life without love for Kṛṣṇa cannot be possible. To deny the personal feature of God is very foolish, because such delusion simply promotes pride and vanity. A neophyte cannot see this, but the saintly person understands that this kind of religion is irreligion. Again and again you emphasize this point, because we are infected with this godless consciousness, without knowing how much it affects us. Impersonalism is just like those modern chemical medicines, which apparently cure the ill but actually soon cause other ills. Bhakti is like the natural medicine; it acts slowly, but it causes the organism to produce its normal balance.

So there are many who purport to speak the truth and some may even seem to be saints; but the real symptom of the genuine leader is that his followers regain their original spiritual health - *kṛṣṇera nitya dāsa*, to be eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa. Only you brought the medicine. You brought *Bhakti-devī* in your hand and passed her to all people. You are the example of a perfect devotee, and so you are the goal of the sincere seekers of the truth. Lord Kṛṣṇa showed you as the real *ācārya*, the last link of the *kṛṣṇa-bhakta’s paramaparā.* All who follow you fix their love for Kṛṣṇa and those awakening who faithfully serve you accept you as the only consolation in life.

Fools do not understand the importance of the reestablishment of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the world, and so they simply study material history, which is supposed to give the solution of their problems. But the Bhāgavatam gives perfect and potent instructions for the reestablishment of peace and happiness in the world.

Lord Caitanya was the preacher of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and you popularized it around the world. You are elected by Lord Kṛṣṇa for teaching *Bhāgavata-dharma* in this age, because you are the *Bhāgavata* devotee.

Your instructions and teachings day after day open to us the doors of knowledge, and the more we advance, the more we can appreciate you in your glory. For this reason, I think that life is too short to glorify you properly.

All glories to you, *jagad-guru*, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Always seeking refuge in the shadow of your lotus feet.

*Your insignificant servants in Rio de Janeiro,*

## St. Louis

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It is six-thirty in the morning. The birds sing sweetly, and peacocks sound their exotic meow. The air resounds with *japa,* chanted by your faithful disciples. Two devotees softly sing the *Brahma-saṁhitā* on the outer veranda, upstairs in your Vṛndāvana quarters, waiting. You ring the bell, and one devotee enters the inner veranda, offers obeisances, and enters the mosquito net on the bed to massage your heart. You speak softly about ISKCON matters - Bombay, Māyāpur, New Vrindaban, etc. Management is *arcanam,* you once said. Your servant helps you to your bath and personally bathes your transcendental form. Then he brings you to the indoor room. You sit in a rocking chair as he helps you with your *kurtā,* and you apply *tilaka* from your Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma compact. The other servant changes the linens and brings the bed on the outer veranda with pillows, scented garland, bouquet, handkerchief, *loṭā,* snuff, incense, and *cāmara.* He has also brought fresh cloth and *kaupīna* for you. You are then aided to the outer veranda, where you sit on the bed, garlanded by your loving disciples. The incense is sweet as one devotee fans you with a *cāmara,* in a figure-eight fashion, not for cooling but for keeping the numerous flies from your thin form. A few *sannyāsīs* come, and you speak to them shortly. One is sent to Nepal and told a story of having the courage of an Englishman and the heart of a Bengali mother. A letter is read from Śrī Lanka. Your disciples have challenged the scientists. You create one fly, one mouse, or one cat, and we’ll give you so many lakhs of rupees; otherwise you must pay. You approve heartily. A report of a new farm in South Africa is read; you comment, “when I hear of the achievements of my disciples, my chest swells out of pride for them.” A *Time* magazine article shown, discussing a Hare Kṛṣṇa marriage in Detroit of a former labor union leader’s daughter, costing $8000. You raise your brows and shake you head, transcendentally responding. *‘Acha.’* Next a report from an Indian library party - one hundred standing orders of five thousand books sold in two weeks. Then another letter from two of your men from Sweden, traveling in Yugoslavia, following your program of festival, feast, and book distribution. Fifteen hundred books sold, and the people all left with stomachs full, hands full, and purses emptied. You laugh strongly and reply, “This news is making me forget my disease.”

All the devotees go except for the fly chaser, and you close your eyes for a very long time, silently taking in the August Vṛndāvana breeze and reflecting on the news. The attendant constantly fans, and from time to time you ask for the top electric fan on, or the spittoon, or a desired disciple. At different times the *kavirāja* is announced, and before he comes you motion for your socks and your *cādar* to cover your lap. He had requested you to wear them constantly, and you act like you are doing just that. By nine-thirty, devotees come up the stairs with a *tulasī* garland and help you into your rocking chair palanquin. As they carry you down the stairs for *darśana*, the *gurukula* boys have *kīrtana* all around you. As you enter the temple room, you chant, “Nitāi-Gaura, Rādhe Śyāma, Jaya Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma.” After greeting the Deities, you are brought under the *tamāla* tree, as sixty or so devotees chant at your feet and the *gurukula* boys dance before you. You motion to your assistants to give the boys the *tulasī* and other fragrant garlands, as you mercifully glance upon the entire scene, tears coming from your eyes while you swallow repeatedly. One very aristocratic, saintly-looking gentleman, with white hair, joins the *darśana*. He is acknowledged and begins to cry as he meditates on your form. To look at him also makes one cry. Soon you are carried back to your ground room by the dayitas, and you spend the day meeting with guests, *kavirājas*, and disciples. Massage is also given during the day. In the early afternoon there are more letters and news. The scientist in Ceylon had written in the paper how they have created so many new chemicals, as he tried to scientifically disprove the existence of God. You reply, “Who has created the basic elements to make up their so-called creation? God will kick on his mouth with shoes. We have given them a good proposal. Rascals, they think they can create life, but they cannot. Fools, why they deny God?” And looking at your garland, “Who could have made such a nice flower? God has made.” Cleveland had sent a report of their restaurant, and the leader of the Bombay temple wants to be with you for Janmāṣṭamī and Vyāsa-pūjā in Vṛndāvana. “Why not?” was the reply. You are so kind, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

At about seven-thirty you are again carried upstairs to your bed on the inner veranda, where an elder reads from *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* with a small lamp. You talk of going to London and America to give the Western devotees a chance to be strengthened. Please strengthen me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Take me with you, please. Allow me to do something nice for you and enter into your transcendental pastimes.

*Your eternal menial servants,*

## San Diego

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* it is stated, ”In this endeavor there is no loss or diminution, and a little advancement on this path protects one from the most dangerous kind of fear.” Whatever little service a person may offer to the Lord, knowingly or unknowingly, is his eternal asset. It guarantees him a birth in the human species of life and another chance to again come in contact with the process of devotional service.

By understanding this verse in light of your unparalleled preaching work in this world, it can be understood that your preaching activities are creating a universal flood of love of God that will, in due course of time, inundate the entire universe.

When the individual soul gets the opportunity to engage in some form of devotional service, even in some small way, by tasting a little *prasādam*, speaking with a devotee, seeing the temple, or chanting the holy name, he has “deposited” eternal spiritual assets in his transcendental “bank account.” Even if he only accumulates one percent advancement in devotional service, it is guaranteed by the Lord, *nehābhikrama-nāśo ‘sti* - there is no loss. This means, as you have explained, that he gets the opportunity to again associate with devotees and devotional service in his next life in order to make further progress.

Thus it cannot be estimated how many hundreds of thousands of people have been eternally benefited by your preaching work. Similarly, it cannot be estimated how expansive your movement is becoming as souls, purified by devotional service, take their birth all over the universe to again take up the process of devotional service. We are therefore glorifying you, singing the words of Narottama das Thakur, *ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana:* “Your glories are sung throughout the three worlds.” In this way, the inconceivable, torrential flood of love of God has been, by your mercy, unleashed upon the face of this sordid material world.

Who can stop it? The ISKCON preaching caravan is rolling on unimpeded. It is Lord Caitanya’s movement. His personal project, directed by you, His most confidential servitor. The fools bark their insignificant criticism in a vain attempt to justify their own sinful lives. Their impotent analysis is of no consequence; let them waste their breath.

Your ISKCON is moving on past the false conclusions of such bewildered pseudospiritualists, sense enjoyers, and gross materialists. It is rolling on far past their envious attacks and false suppositions. Following you, we are confident that this movement you have started will surpass not only the petty material obstacles this world will inevitably present but will transcend the entire material creation and ultimately take shelter at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. There you await us to continue your guidance. We are your servants eternally. Our service to you has nothing to do with the limitation of this body. You have captured our hearts and souls. Please continue to lead us. We are prepared to follow you eternally.

*We beg to remain, your eternal servants,*

## Sao Paulo

*Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious event  
we fall at your feet,  
we worship you,  
we love you, and above all,  
we listen to you and  
try to preach on your behalf.  
We follow your orders -  
you are the supreme commander in chief of  
this great movement.

By doing that, under the shelter of  
your ISKCON movement,  
we feel near to you.  
We feel you close by your  
transcendental instruction.  
You are living still in sound.  
But as no one can  
ever be near the sun,  
even though it appears to be  
always upon us,  
in the same way  
we cannot be really close to you.  
You came, stayed among us,  
left your divine instructions, and  
remembrance of you enthuses us.  
But then you departed.  
Where are you now?  
It really does not matter, because wherever you are,  
there is Vṛndāvana.  
And Lord Kṛṣṇa will always shine through you.  
We want to be eternally with you.  
We will try to make all future generations follow you,  
and for ourselves, when we give this body,  
let us go where you are. That is the only safe position:  
to be always with you.  
If we do not do that,  
we will forget about Kṛṣṇa  
because of this terrible world of māyā.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!  
All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants from Sao Paulo, Brazil*

## Spain

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your lotus feet!

On this most auspicious day of your blessed appearance in this mortal world, you offer us a special opportunity to refresh and stabilize our Kṛṣṇa consciousness simply by remembering and glorifying your activities and pastimes of establishing Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement in the West.

In this way we may deepen our realization of the significance of the ISKCON branch of the Caitanya tree, which has sprouted and blossomed by your fervent desire to fulfill the desire of the Lord and all predecessor *ācāryas*. Therefore, on this day we may reflect on your mission, appreciate our good fortune, judge ourselves in the light of your will, and renew our commitment to assist you by cooperating amongst ourselves, thus demonstrating our love for you and keeping your ISKCON branch intact and healthy by supplying it with all necessary nourishment so that it may grow and expand unlimitedly, until the scent of its fruits and flowers of love of God permeate all nooks and corners of the planet.

Whenever we reflect on your mission and the purpose of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement to provide shelter to all conditioned souls from the onslaught of the illusory energy, we are inevitably reminded of our own great fortune to have attained this shelter by your causeless mercy. Without your unflinching determination to fulfill the order of your *Guru* Mahārāja and to come to the West in order to save us fallen souls, where would we be now? If you would not have come to us, we hardly would have come to you, but would be rotting by now in most abominable conditions of material existence. Having no direction, we were wandering aimlessly. We did not know who we were, where we had come from, where we were going to, what we were supposed to do - we had no idea. Or maybe we had some idea, but this idea had sprung from our imaginative mind, no more than a fanciful concoction, and at any rate was very prone to be changed by the next book we would read, the next psychedelic experience we would have, or the next friend we would listen to. Apart from that, our way of living and our personal behavior were so obnoxious that to use terms which were used for the lowest classes in Vedic times such as *“śūdra,” “mleccha”* or *“caṇḍāla”* would almost be flattery. Nevertheless, you did not mind coming for our rescue by giving us your association, leaving behind the pure and blissful atmosphere of Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma.

And not that it was an easy task. Some magic. As you said once, “Don’t expect smooth and easy going. Kṛṣṇa did not give Arjuna some magic ash. No, he had to fight!” Similarly, your endeavor to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness - whether in India or in the West - was accompanied by constant struggle: no initial capital or manpower, no cooperation from your God brothers, no support from private or public institutions or wealthy businessmen. Even to get your passage to America you practically had to go on strike in front of Śrīmatī Morarjee’s office, and when you eventually boarded the *Jaladuta* no pleasant sea journey, but seasickness and heart strokes were awaiting you. Finally America - the promised land, the land of plenty, the land of unlimited possibilities. Your spiritual vision, however, cut immediately through the veil of this glittering phantasmagoria, identifying the so-called heaven as hell and a place of plenty as misery and unlimited possibilities to entangle oneself in horrible activities.

And to such a place you had come alone, at an advanced age, without any assistance or means and without being willing to compromise the principles of devotional service. Consequently there was no quick following of rich old ladies who readily would have spent their money on keeping an Indian Swami as a pet *guru,* nor did hosts of well-educated, well-situated young Americans come flying to your door, being promised to improve their fitness by some dubious *yoga* technique. Instead you had to struggle a whole year alone in New York, tolerating the clumsy assistance of crazy people, until you finally picked your first disciples out of the garbage cans of the Bowery and the Lower East Side. This was real magic - how you transformed rat like creatures into residents of Vaikuṇṭha. We all pray that we may never forget that your mercy is everything we are made of - literally - and that we may always cultivate the deepest feelings of gratitude in our hearts, preventing us from ever abandoning the great fortune of your association. The greatest misfortune would be to go back to the rat like existence and disappear in the garbage cans of this world, never to be seen again. Undoubtedly this would have been our fate, and if we look at ourselves now and we see that we are in a different position, we should understand very clearly that it is not due to our own prowess. If we took you out of our lives, it would be like turning off the light, and everything would sink into darkness. Our very existence as devotees of Kṛṣṇa, i.e. Kṛṣṇa consciousness per se, would simply dissipate into nonexistence; there would be no chanting of the holy name, no *prasādam,* no books, no temples, no Deities, no Vṛndāvana or Māyāpur, no Vaiṣṇava association - only a gloomy race of rats waiting to vanish in mutual destruction.

You have given us everything: Kṛṣṇa in unlimited forms such as His name, His Deity, His *prasādam,* etc. our real self, our real life and activity, the goal of our life... How is it possible to list all he gifts we have received from you? It is not possible, because they are all Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa is unlimited and inconceivable. And there is no way to ever repay you. Kṛṣṇa is priceless. The only thing that can match Kṛṣṇa is Kṛṣṇa Himself. And that is the secret - to give Kṛṣṇa. But not to you; to others. “Do as I am doing.” You want us to give away all the gifts we have received from you, and those who receive them are supposed to give them to others, and those will give them to others, and in this way all living entities will eventually receive Kṛṣṇa. Then only will you be satisfied and “repaid,” and only by at least trying to do so can we please you and remain in your association.

You live forever by your divine instructions, and your followers live with you.

*Your eternally indebted disciples and grand disciples,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## State College

O Lord and Master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we offer our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. You are the confidential servitor of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, the merciful messenger of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You came to awaken the comatose *jīvas* by the pure transmission of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. You came to teach us that unless we stopped the endeavor for sense gratification, we cannot stop the process of rebirth in material bodies, and that as long as we continue to have material bodies, we cannot stop suffering. You also taught us, moreover, that the artificial endeavor to give up sense gratification by force, through yoga or other means, will always fail ultimately, because we are living, willing *jīvas* for whom happiness is a birthright, the supreme science of performing spiritual activities - not mere religious rituals, but activities on the Brahman platform, even while within the material realm. In other words, your purely spiritual teachings have manifested the possibility for everyone to engage in the unambiguous functions of the soul.

By this merciful offering, you have relieved us from an interminable nightmare. Practically, this is the proof of your exalted status as the bona fide *jagad-guru,* that by accepting your lotus feet we have become free from anxiety, ignorance, and fear. The shelter of your service we see to be factually effective in producing all auspicious things, and thus we can confidently preach the glories of the process of *bhakti-yoga* and of Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, from our tangible realizations, as causeless knowledge and detachment awaken in us.

We know that all things in spiritual life are possible only by your mercy, O master. We pray, therefore, that we may be granted unflinching faith in you. You can easily kick out all remaining obstacles in our path if only we will let you, and your association will quickly awaken the essential mood of great eagerness for Kṛṣṇa, which may not be found after thousands of pious lives.

O master, we pray for your blessings that we may please you by our preaching endeavors. We see that *jīvas* here taking the Lord’s *prasādam*, hearing His transcendental teachings, and chanting His holy names, becoming purified in so many ways and awakening to spiritual life. That this is all your mercy can never be doubted, for who could ever have been imagined such thing had Your Divine Grace not delivered these gifts to us?

We pray, therefore, that we may always be your chaste messengers, carrying your glorifications of devotional service to the fallen *jīvas* and to the devotees, just as Śrī Uddhava so selflessly and expertly carried Lord Kṛṣṇa’s message to the beautiful damsels of Vraja.

*Your disciples and grand disciples,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Stambha dāsa / Giriyādava dāsa / Rudrāṇī-devī dāsī

**Grand disciples**

Yamunā-pati dāsa / Dattātreya dāsa / Kurukṣetra dāsa / Bhakta Steve / Bhaktin Linda / Bhaktin Dana

## Switzerland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. My indebtedness to you as well as my inability to formulate this offering in glorification of your divine qualities is expanding year after year. I will nonetheless try my best to serve and worship you as my eternally beloved spiritual master.

Only by your causeless mercy have I been able to catch a glimpse of your divine character. You appeared as the rising moon in the night of inauspicious Kali-yuga, and with you cooling and soothing rays of transcendental knowledge, you have dissipated the darkness of ignorance. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am praying to you that my mind may become attracted to your moonlike personality just like a cakora bird, which subsists only upon moonlight. Your mercy is all that I am made of.

There were and will be so many shooting stars, but you will always remain distinctly fixed as the most attractive and merciful well-wisher of all the living entities.

As the stars are reflecting their light due to the presence of the sun, so I am reflecting and receiving your causeless mercy due to your eternal presence in your instructions. I am an insignificant star in this universal movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, with a poor and feeble understanding of Lord Caitanya’s universal mission of benedicting the worlds with love of Godhead.

The intellectuals who are guiding this movement and who are fully engaged in serving your heart’s desire are your brains, by which you have given us the ability to understand things in their proper perspective.

The *saṅkīrtana* soldiers, who are very dear to you and who are fully engaged in distributing love of Godhead to fulfill your heart’s desire, are your arms by which you slay the demoniac mentality.

The farmers who are fully engaged in growing food for the strength of your arms, to fulfill your heart’s desire are your legs, on which you are carrying this movement in the right direction.

The books are your heart which pumps life into all of us. Your heart’s desire is to pump books into this world and flood it with love of Godhead.

Your mercy is your love - giving Kṛṣṇa to the victims of Kali-yuga.

Those who are giving that love in the form of your books, your disciples and grand disciples, are very dear to you.

ISKCON is an international society for your book consciousness, a revolution to spread your mercy. Your instructions and divine character are forever present in your books. The figures of book distribution are the most important signs of the success of your movement, for your books bring Kṛṣṇa, your books bring a revolution, your books bring religion. Your books dissipate the darkness of ignorance, your books make movies, your books make humans from animals. Your books are booked for ten thousand years. Your books make us dance and give us a higher taste.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this childish attempt of offering a ghee lamp to the sun.

You are so merciful - more than I deserve.

You have washed me, you have dressed me, you have educated me, you have cared for more than I care about myself. The very words that I am speaking, the things I am using, the temple I am serving - all are borrowed plumes of your mercy. Your mercy is all that I am made of. I take shelter at your lotus feet.

Jaya Oṁ Viṣṇupada paramahaṁsa parivrājak*ācārya* aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

*Your servants,*

## Sydney

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

On this your auspicious appearance day, as I meditate on your pure devotion, I always feel overwhelmed at the magnanimity of what you did - you left the association of the devotees and the security of the holy dhāma to come to a new culture in a far-off place.

When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya, “Of all kinds of distress, what is the most painful?” Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “Apart from separation from the devotee of Kṛṣṇa, I know no unbearable unhappiness,” Also, we know from experience how difficult it is to preach alone - even for a day. But Śrīla Prabhupāda, you did what no one has ever done, and you alone spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Even after you had made many devotees, you continued to travel all over the world to enthuse your disciples who “alone” could not do what you had done.

Out of your causeless mercy Śrīla Prabhupāda, you even came to Australia - the place you named “Mleccha-deśa.” You personally brought us Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha and asked Them to teach us how to worship Them, as you could not stay. At your request They have done the needful, and They are now being worshiped opulently in a beautiful new temple here in Sydney. Hundreds of people come to take Their *prasādam* daily.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for everything. Please give us more and more opportunities to serve you.

*Your servants at Sydney, Australia,*
