## North American BBT

Our hearts must ever be thoroughly prostrated at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda, else we shall be spoiled and shall again assume the mentality of the living dead. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s every action and reaction, and his unceasing efforts to awaken us to our real self-same reality, and his undivided devotion to the order of his spiritual master - these are the prime characteristics of his exemplary life, emulating which we are enabled to overlook death, thus entering the absolute realm. It is by his divine grace alone that the four facts of bodily existence have receded to the status of secondary annoyances. And by his divine grace alone we are able to leave aside the pursuits of profit, adoration and distinction. By virtue of his presence amongst us, the devotional imperative has become manifest in our minds and hearts.

We each must ever recognize that that devotional imperative constitutes the entirety of our personal conscious spiritual existence. Without the transcendental fire of true enthusiasm (*enthusiasm*: from the Greek *enthusiomos,* meaning “inspiration in God”), our spiritual existence will not be perceivable by us, just as hogs and dogs have no conscious spiritual life.

Further, we must recognize that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preaching mood forms the archetypal standard that lends structural and directional form to the devotional imperative, which he alone has reawakened in our hearts. Tragic and sad experiences tell us that one who deviates from the determination to follow in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preaching mood, while maintaining airs of holiness, becomes a *sahajiyā,* an “all show, no go” pseudospiritualist.

It is therefore incumbent upon us to remain ever immersed in the mood and mission of Śrīla Prabhupāda. We must not, however, consider this necessary immersion as a passive contemplation. In this degraded world the devotional imperative demands devotional action in order to keep the senses out of death-bound engagements, and devotional action for us is summed up singularly: PREACHING.

We are but specks of dust swirling in the wake of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s journey through this world. While such personal insignificance to be a relief and to be the key to understanding our own substantive, eternal significance.

Unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we offer ourselves in the hope that despite our insignificance and manifest inebriety, you will continue to allow us to reside beneath your lotus feet and help your disciples and grand disciples for the glorification of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī Dvārakādhīśa.

Your servants at the North American BBT,

Mahendra dāsa / Jāmbavān dāsa / Kadamba-kānana dāsa / Kaumodakī-devī dāsī / Nirākulā-devī dāsī / Śatadhārī-devī dāsī

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakty-ānanda-vidya Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, most merciful eternal father.  
Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You gave me birth in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, and after my having been imprisoned for many millions of lifetimes in hellish existence, devoid of any love, peace or happiness, you brought me to the blissful, spiritual realm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Dear father, your gift of eternal spiritual life has not stopped amazing me from the first day you opened my eyes with the torch of transcendental knowledge. In trying to think what to write, I feel completely incapable of expressing my gratitude and appreciation for this great mercy.

Now we have started a program that is going to revolutionalize the effect of preaching with amazing results. This will especially please you, because with this system, all the devotees in the temple are becoming engaged in direct Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching, by collecting donations, cultivating and engaging people, making members, and in this way taking responsibilities for the spiritual advancement of the fallen souls.

We call this program “Contact Saṅkīrtana,” which means making sure that everyone gets connected with the ocean of love of godhead, your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You gave us a treasure, and the more we distribute it, the more it increases.

To not distribute your books means that our preaching is a speculation. What is the meaning of expanding and managing unless the books are the basis? Your books and instructions are the only two tracks back home, back to Godhead. How can anybody get the higher taste without them?

Your books are the condensed sweet nectar sample of the spiritual world, the only medicine for the bitter taste of the jaundiced condition of sense gratification. How can we expect that the diseased people be inspired to take the treatment regularly unless the doctor visits them regularly? And how many patients are there? Billions. And how many doctors? Few. So no one should stay back? Every devotee out. Part time or full time. Everyone should take responsibility for seeing the people advancing. After we meet a person, we are obliged to save him. Not that our business is finished after he gives a donation or hears a lecture. That’s when our real duty of preaching begins: this person has somehow or other accepted Kṛṣṇa: I have to save him.

The remedy for the false pride of the conditioned soul is to hear about the transcendental glories of Kṛṣṇa’s activities and qualities and serve Him. But how can Kṛṣṇa praise Himself and demand devotion for Himself? Who will listen to Him and obey Him in Kali-yuga, the age of hypocrisy and independence? Therefore Kṛṣṇa asked Arjuna to speak on His behalf and to fight for Him, and the *yuga-dharma* of the age is taught by a devotee, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. And what did He do? *Saṅkīrtana. Yajanti hi sumedhasaḥ: Saṅkīrtana* is for the most intelligent. The more we advance, the more we should preach, because the more we understand about Kṛṣṇa, the more successful we will become by our example and purity in saving the fallen souls by inspiring them in devotional service.

Our mission is to become the living example of religious life, the personification of pure devotional service, walking holy *tīrthas*, in whose association everyone will become inspired to hear and chant about Kṛṣṇa’s glorious position as the Absolute Truth, dance in ecstatic happiness, and relish the sumptuous feast of devotional service, and becoming liberated from the laws of material nature. Normally this experience is considered the most confidential information of transcendental knowledge, which Kṛṣṇa will never reveal to anyone but His pure devotees and friends.

The sign that Kṛṣṇa is feeling pleased with us and relieved of His anxieties is that we are feeling the pressure of the concern for seeing Kṛṣṇa consciousness steadily and rapidly expanding all over the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for having opened my eyes and having taught me how to serve you. My only desire is to see you pleased and happy with the progress of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, the eternal movement for preaching devotional service, the highest religion. And you being the greatest devotee, you cannot be satisfied by anything else.

Śrīla Prabhupāda Ki Jaya!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Bhakty-ānanda-vidya Goswami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. Please allow me to offer you my meager appreciations of your fathomless glories.

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*

Thank you for saving me from the blazing forest fire of material existence. For millions of births, in various species, I was being constantly scorched by the burning flames of lust, anger, attachment, and anxiety. Up to the point when you found me twelve years ago in a body barely human, I had still not learned that fire burns. It is said that an intelligent persons learns by hearing and a foolish one by experience. What am I, then, who could not learn by either? But simply by your compassionate personality and smiling glance, you were able to loosen that hard knots of material attachments that kept me, like a condemned snake, from escaping that forest fire.

Nor did you merely relieve this afflicted soul by throwing me in the dangerous waters of the ocean of conclusive truth. The Vedantic knowledge which you so powerfully presented and preached acted as favorable breezes which propelled me in my human “boat” and protected me from the voracious crocodiles of various speculative theories.

*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Only you are delivering the Western countries (and the entire world) from the gaping jaws of impersonalism and voidism.

*mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-*  
*vaditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*

You instructed me that by constantly chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa I could be saved from *māyā’s* influence. You knew, as the embodiment of religious principles, that I could not break my attachments to intoxication and mundane sound vibration by any other method. Therefore, you gave me Śrī Hari-nāma. Kṛṣṇa, in the form of His holy name, is yours - you are always relishing Him - therefore you can give Him to anyone. I was even chanting so-called “Hare Kṛṣṇa” before, but to no avail:

*sampradāya-vihīnā ye mantrās te niṣphalā matāḥ*

But for your grace, I could never have entered the kīrtana-rasa. Thank you.

*śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-*  
*śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-marjanādau*  
*yuktasya bhaktāṁs ca niyuñjato ’pi*

You knew my eyes were ten thousand times lustier than Sura das’s (Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura) and that I was too timid and insincere to pluck them out. Therefore you introduced me to your Lord in His worshipable Deity forms of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. How could anyone fail to be attracted to the Supreme Attractive? Please help me to remember that Their worshipable Lordships are your transcendental friends, not mine. I am merely attempting to assist you in Their service.

*catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-*  
*svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅhān*  
*kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva*

Knowing the immensely voracious appetite of my tongue, you realized I would still fall into another hellish birth. Therefore you gave me Lord Kṛṣṇa’s purifying association in a sugarcoated way - *mahā-prasādam*. You are yourself inspired seeing your disciples relishing *prasādam*. I think, at least in this way, I can give you some pleasure. Thank you again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you knew the extent of my fallen condition, my being very much a Kali-yuga soul, and thus you knew that one “weapon” was not enough to conquer my unlimited attachments and illusions. Just as Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu came with so many *aṅgas* and *upāṅgas*, so you wield the most formidable array of spiritual weapons and strata-gem the world has ever seen. Please allow me to render some small service to your *saṅkīrtana* movement, thus pleasing you. Without executing your transcendental order, I have no life of shelter, for your association exists only in your order.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

I offer my respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Today, on this most auspicious occasion of your appearance within this world, I am intensely feeling separation from you, remembering your pastimes amongst us. By your grace you gave us such close association and those precious moments remain fixed within my heart, veritable jewels of remembrance that I pray may forever illuminate my service unto you. Your glorious achievements attest to your greatness and will be sung throughout all time. In the past great devotees anticipated your appearance, which would unify and solidify the service of generations of Vaiṣṇavas and firmly establish a worldwide movement of devotees to fulfill the promise of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your divine appearance, heralded by devotees and nondevotees alike, touched us and blessed us with the vision to appreciate the profound and unique gift of devotional service, which you so mercifully gave us. There is no doubt that in the future humanity will praise you as the greatest ambassador of the Lord this world has been graced to receive. O to see that day within our lifetimes! The very seeds you planted arose unrivaled, yet the world has still to see the full fruits of your efforts manifest by our determined desire to carry your orders on to perfection.

O my lord and master, somehow I play a small part in the greater plan to manifest your hopes and aspirations. Crippled as I am, by lifetimes of forgetfulness, through service unto your lotus feet I now stand willing and able to serve your every command. Although my attempts are feeble due to my infancy in spiritual life, I have promised to give up all that is unnecessary and have vowed to dedicate my body, mind and words fully in your service. Please bless me with the strength and vision to fulfill the real purport of *sannyāsa* under your firm direction. Allow me to always remain under the shelter of your lotus feet, serving you and those dear to you, and thus attain my desired goal, unalloyed devotion to Your Divine Grace within this very life. I pray I may always participate within your great mission, defeating all opposition and helping to present you at last with the inevitable triumphant victory you deserve: the establishment of your International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness as the real salvation of all mankind, in all places and for all times.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*yasya deve para bhaktir yatha deve tatha gurau*  
*tasyaite kathita hy artha prakasante mahatmanah*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You once said that Kṛṣṇa consciousness (although “difficult for the crooked”) is “simple for the simple.” And you proved it too, because you resolved all difficulties simply by your instructions, your example, and your presence. “Is everything all right?” you would ask. And just by your asking, it would be.

You gave us a simple *mantra -* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. And you chanted in a simple way that all of us could follow.

You taught us to worship the Deity in a simple manner: Be clean, be punctual. Do everything with great care and attention and Kṛṣṇa will bless you.

Don’t be over intelligent, don’t over endeavor, don’t make matters overly complicated. But don’t be dull-brained either, and don’t be lazy. Not simply eating and sleeping. “Do something wonderful for Kṛṣṇa.”

”Something wonderful” means doing as you did, following faithfully your footsteps. You kept your message simple and sublime. And you asked us to deliver it on every street, at every door, into every person’s hand. “I am simply following my *Guru* Mahārāja,” you said. And we simply want to always follow you.

*Your servant,*  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānānjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

O my lord, dear Prabhupāda, please hear my plea,  
For you are the ever well wisher of everyone that be.

You came directly from Goloka, by our Lord’s sweet will,  
To deliver us from evil forces we thought were a thrill.

While here on earth your pastimes inspired all with awe;  
Compared to you the biggest men appeared to like straw.

Roaming the earth, you appeared to be a roaring lion;  
When you spoke ignorance fled like animals in every direction.

No illusion, mistakes or cheating could stand in front of you;  
Your form and words revealed the spiritual world in clear view.

You are situated eternally in an all-knowing spiritual bliss.  
Please accept this faltering praise, your mercy I am made of this.

Despite a life devoid of good, of selfish thought and deed,  
You delivered me, dearest Prabhupāda, in my hour of greatest need.

This, your compassion, for the lowest fallen souls  
Is actually your greatest glory, the acme of your goals.

Now that you have left us, in your vapuḥ form at least,  
We need you more than ever, ere we turn again to beast.

Despite our constant fighting spirit, you were always there  
To pacify, rectify, force us to lay threads bare.

An ocean of humility, you showed us how to be  
Parts and parcels of your body, the ISKCON society.

By giving constant support to whomever took your flag,  
Disputes were always settled in a way no one could lag.

Taking every opportunity to glorify the Lord,  
Your cooperative spirit always won across the board.

You never took our mistakes as personal transgression -  
Māyā has only entered, you’d say, to give us all a lesson.

In this way you kept intact the loving family mood  
While correcting us constantly without causing us to brood.

Because you were fully Kṛṣṇa conscious we all accepted you  
As the ultimate authority of all, the true jagad-guru.

Lord Caitanya predicted, every town and every village.  
Now that vow is coming true, due only to your visage.

Don’t be surprised who went, you said, but be surprised who stayed.  
To prepare us for the future, our fears you have allayed.

But despite the obvious clarity of your transcendental life,  
Some rejected, even chose to follow others, causing strife.

”If, you’re ever disturbed, just take any of my books.  
The nature of these thoughts,” you said, “will destroy māyā’s looks.”

You have given me everything, but still I ask for more:  
I beg you to force me to accept my given chore.

To reject the crescendos of my senses and my mind,  
Keep me bound until I’m pure, one of yours in kind.

Without your mercy my fate is sealed by the patterns of my mind.  
Like lines in stone, more indelible sin you’ll never find.

I don’t know what to do -  
I have no real love for you!!

I only know that for me you are both cause and the effect,  
So cause me please to surrender, don’t permit me to reject.

The relief I felt often leaves me due to my offenses.  
When will my offenses cease? When will I come to my senses?

I want to follow strictly, reduce my eating and sleeping,  
Hear and chant, manage and preach, to do as you are doing.

But without that taste of nectar, which only you can give,  
I’ll always fail to reach the goal as long as I shall live.

Please help me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me shelter at your feet;  
Allow me to chant the holy names with your devotees on the street.

To go out and preach tirelessly, that is your saṅkīrtana.  
Give me strength and inspiration, remove my ignorance with your dawn.

I have no love, no devotion, speculation is my cloak,  
And due to lust my chant of praise is empty like a croak.

Those without are beggars, so I have no other choice  
But to plead my case, please my lord, give me transcendental voice.

I want to enjoy the best of both worlds, material and divine.  
Please have mercy on me, Prabhupāda, force me to draw the line.

Take away whatever I maintain which keeps me in illusion;  
Bestow whatever knowledge I need to preach without confusion.

Aspiring for any place in the shade of your lotus feet,

Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

## Śacī-suta Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, when Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda together with Their associates come to take part in this beautiful *kīrtana,* they see how year after year the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda is becoming a reality and how so many people are coming to our centers to know more about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which Śrīla Prabhupāda, with great effort, brought to the Western world, so full of atheism and material desires.

His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, with his immaculate devotion, over flooded this continent with transcendental literature, giving us the opportunity to take part in this beautiful movement, and to become his disciples and engage in devotional service.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who lives forever in his books, which are like touchstones. When people read a book, they receive this rain of mercy and become his devotees.

Remembering Śrīla Prabhupāda and falling down at his lotus feet, I pray so some day I can become a sincere and faithful disciple of His Divine Grace, and following his example I may add my grain of sand to his aim of life: to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this distant place of the world, and in this way attain the success of life.

*Your last servant,*  
Śacī-suta Swami

## Śivarāma Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In 1971, I recall flying over New York on the way to Miami, a hippie on some unmentionable business, *Kṛṣṇa* book in hand. As I turned to the back cover, you are holding a flower in your hand and a transcendental compassionate smile on your beautiful face. I turned to my companion. “I would like to ask him what the purpose of life is. I wonder where he is now?”

I don’t know where that idea came from (an echo of a timeless struggle to cast off this veil of illusion?), but I felt a great attraction, a great longing to be with you. By Kṛṣṇa’s grace I knew that you were the teacher to free me from illusion, to answer all my questions, eradicate my doubts and establish my identity as an eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa. I never doubted that you were the only transparent medium to God, that you were a perfect spiritual master, and that you were beyond the modes of material nature. Where were you then? On one of your countless trips around the world, while I kept flying on into illusion, with a faint cry for help echoing in the recesses of my consciousness. Years melted away into uselessness, while the next memory surfaces in Montreal. In 1973, Bhakta Peter finally pulled out of material life, left his wife and parents behind, university, hippie-LSD consciousness, and a hopeless paranoia that haunted me day and night, an insecurity and fear that was pushing me on the verge of insanity, for everywhere that I turned I could see that human endeavor was simply one selfish, exploitative attempt for sense gratification after another. Now some hope, your shelter, a desire to become a *sannyāsī,* and an addiction for simply wonderful spurred me on in a new experience called Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Days, weeks and months go by, and the impetus for all our service is “Śrīla Prabhupāda said.” Bhakta Peter, in a *kurtā* three sizes too small, polyester *dhotī*, still a bit bewildered, bathroom cleaner, book distributor, *brahmacārī,* and your servant.

Standing on St. Catherines Street one summer noon with an older God brother, learning to distribute BTGs, a struggle for the first few weeks (they told me Kṛṣṇa would tell me what to say), then I turned to look at your picture for some reassurance, and released some wonderful potency, the urgency and bliss of saving conditioned souls from their hopeless illusion and revealed a drop of the nectar of *saṅkīrtana*. Racing from person to person, book in hand, tears on my cheeks, feeling absorbed in the powerful connection where you, the *ācāryas* and Lord Caitanya abide bestowing benediction on all those who dedicate themselves for pushing on the *saṅkīrtana* movement. It’s ecstasy: “Distribute books, distribute books, distribute books.”

Summer, 1975, you came to Montreal, and eager and foolish I told you how difficult it was to distribute books at the airport, how they were persecuting us in our endeavor to please you. And you smiled and said gravely: “So if it is too difficult, you can come with me to India.” I was bewildered and embarrassed. I took it as a reproach. Now, ten years later, Brahmānanda Swami explains that you were canvassing for Canadians to come help you build India, but I missed out, standing at the top of the escalator, watching to get a last glimpse of you as you entered you plane.

Memories are surfacing, but where is the place to write them all? I am not even sure if this is the proper for this forum (maybe my *sannyāsī* God brothers will call me a sentimentalist or a *sahajiyā*?). Through these memories your presence is becoming more and more intense, but there is nothing new, just something growing, blossoming as the years go by.

Through the years, as my God brothers, in your books, in dreams and in your service, you have remained with me, as I feel your presence now. Actually all these memories are just evidence of your presence in our hearts. Years have gone by, in your presence and now in your absence, but in both absence and presence you are equally there. For those who carry out your order distributing books, building temples, making devotees, your *pūjā* is a daily affair, and those who take that up make every day a *vyāsa-pūjā* day, a celebration of your glories through not only words but actual service. The memories expressed above and others are but fruits of service, your kindness in revealing yourself to us. But the same experience through chanting, hearing, remembering, praying - all the nine processes of devotional service - is always there to the surrendered disciples and to future generations who never deviate from your order. As you have attested to the fact that “I was never alone, for I always felt presence of my spiritual master,” so I pray that I may always feel your divine presence, and through my service glorify you who can never be glorified enough. Today is *vyāsa-pūjā* day, to put in writing what we experience and perform through service throughout the year. May you bless us to unlimitedly increase our service to Your Divine Grace in the security of your eternal association.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Śivarāma Swami

## Sundararūpa dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You have said that if someone reads even one line of your books, then he may be saved from material existence. From this statement, we can understand that each of your instructions is so valuable that it contains the ultimate benefit of all of them, just as Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has summarized the entire body of spiritual principles to mean that one should always remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him; or similarly, amongst the nine processes of devotional service according to Prahlāda Mahārāja, the perfection of at least one is considered to be as good as perfection in them all. In other words, because every instruction expands from the same source, the essence of the Absolute Truth is fully present within each one. *Oṁ pūrṇam adaḥ pūrṇam idam.*

Simply hearing regularly from you purifies our intelligence so that we may adjust our own behavior and even the policies of ISKCON accordingly. You certainly expect much more of us now, both quantitatively and qualitatively, that what we were capable of when you were physically present. Undoubtedly, we greatly appreciated your practical tolerance of our limited capacity at that time, but at least now we should be aware that many things were not as you wanted them to be or to become. In other words, we cannot judge you entirely by what took place then. Just like there is a difference between what Kṛṣṇa allows and what He actually wants. Therefore, the status quo, which was attained prior to your departure, should not be equated with your original intent, nor your ultimate desires, nor the limits of your vision. In fact, if we expect to fulfill your great vision for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we must abandon all previous misconceptions.

Actually, none of us can accurately realize the proper imports of your teachings without following in your footsteps, doing as you have done. Failure to share your experiences in this way will render the full appreciation of your purports inaccessible to us, just as the taste of honey remains hidden for those who insist upon licking the outside of the bottle. That means that we must behave as well as you did and preach like you did. Otherwise, even we may claim to know you and promote our misconceptions in your name, our realizations will remain insufficient. We must pay the price, at least in some small way, of performing sacrifice, undergoing the same difficulties as you did, which have endeared you so much to Kṛṣṇa. *Mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.*

It is a matter of relevance that this knowledge is only fully relevant to one who has firmly embraced your mission of preaching within this heart, being factually motivated, as you are, for the spiritual benefit of everyone. Full relevance means absolute in the sense that to serve you requires the employment of our entire being, through the instruments of our body, mind, and words. Our dedication to your service should not be incomplete in any way; therefore we must exercise our total capacities to be uniformly engaged as your servant, not that we engage our mind and words only without performing the proper action.

Neither is good intention or sentiment devoid of willingness to make enough effort to accomplish your orders to be considered real devotion. Real devotion means to never tire before we achieve the objectives that you desire. Similarly, real humility and sincerity also mean to be willing to forgo our inadequacies either in ability or desire in order to respond to the practical necessities of being your servant. Doing the needful to satisfy your wishes ultimately demonstrates the greatest devotion for you.

In any case, every year we should examine our adherence to your teachings, taking account of which ones we follow and which ones we do not. Accordingly, we should measure our progress by analyzing how much we have improved or diminished from the year before. Hopefully, amongst all of us, every word of yours is being respected. At least by our cooperation (although individually we may be deficient), each of your instructions is being followed somewhere by someone, and they will enable the rest of us to make up for what we lack.

At all costs, we must avoid the misuse of the philosophy that you have given us to justify our misbehavior, or to simply defend ourselves in order to maintain some status quo, or to establish our particular designation, even within a spiritual society, as superior. To preach is definitely what you desire of us, but not beyond the degree of our realized experience. It’s one thing to speculate and another to bring back the fruits of our preaching and offer them at your lotus feet. Your instructions are meant to fulfill your purpose and that of the previous *ācāryas*, not an independent purpose of our own.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my most cherished desire is that after this life I will have the chance to witness your response in this respect. I am anxious to experience the opportunity to perform the most intimate personal service to you, which I missed when you were physically present. I prefer this future much more than to go to Kṛṣṇaloka or receive whatever other spiritual benefit there might be.

*Your most unqualified servant,*  
Sundararūpa dāsa Goswami

## Vipramukhya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

With a straw in my teeth, I bow down at your lotus feet. Please accept my *daṇḍavats*.

Now all of the G.B.C.s, *gurus, sannyāsīs*, and temple leaders are writing their Vyāsa-pūjā offering to you, and I have also been asked to write one. But in the light of such great personalities as these, I have nothing significant to contribute. The only thing that remains for me to do is surrender completely unto you.

We fallen jīva-souls, while wandering in the material world, are always lonely and distraught. Although we were searching for broken pieces of glass, by the Lord’s mercy we achieved your association instead. You are the proprietor of the great treasure house of love of God. How much you have given, Śrīla Prabhupāda! When we were in our darkest hours and everything appeared to be utterly hopeless, you came like the rising sun on the eastern horizon - the pure, bona fide representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Nityānanda. While others sat in Māyāpur or Vṛndāvana, you came out in search for us. Who knows what would have become of us had you not come? Before you came to the West, everything here was inauspicious, but now that you have come, there is a glimmer of hope, even in the midst of this dark age of Kali.

In spite of that, my stone like heart does not change. I am so wretched that I cannot properly appreciate all that you have done for us. May this occasion of your appearance-day celebration become a great transcendental fortune for all of the people of this planet, so they may meet with devotees, take *prasādam*, and become happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your mercy!

*Your useless servant,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Bhaktivedanta Institute, Bombay

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our all-knowing master,  
Savior of mankind from disaster,  
Your mercy rained and love spread  
Your master’s message in your heart,  
Transported you to a sphere complex and vast,  
A lifetime preparation with teaching divine,  
Kṛṣṇa’s song was diamond’s mine;  
Tranquil within by devotional service,  
Transcendental beyond every caprice,  
The past, present and future wisdom  
Through Bhaktivedanta bestowed is divine vision;  
A fellowship of science and religious custom  
By your foresight and wisdom,  
Entrusted to your servants most lowly  
Will bloom through your mercy only.  
Your Institute is unique without a rival,  
Eschews evil and works for revival,  
The gift of devotion, knowledge and renunciation  
Illuminated glows, self-realization.  
The scientists’ conference at your door  
Got message of love your bust could pour,  
Life comes from life undiluted and uncompromised.  
Feeble was your frame before disappearance arrived,  
Who says, Prabhupāda, you have departed from us?  
You have come closer by going away thus.  
In your spiritual abode your fellowship divine  
For the love of Kṛṣṇa we sing and pine,  
For your mercy vast  
With Rāsavihārī’s glowing present and past,  
On whose beautiful face your gaze dwells  
With Rādhārāṇī, gopīs’ lifted veils,  
This society of saints with no greed or passion  
Draws us to the fold of merit and lesson,  
The chanting of devotees and taste of prasad  
Binds us together with śāstras fast,  
Your clarion call will break all attachments  
And bring together scholars and merchants.  
O teachers of ‘māyā,’ know the real secret!  
Break the demoniac company and attachment’s weight,  
Come to Bhaktivedanta, the real seat of holy one,  
Know the difference between matter and atman.  
Without divine fellowship, desolate is the earth.  
It’s all mirage in the worldly desert.  
Quench the thirst of human restlessness;  
By your causeless mercy, reclaim the useless.  
O Prabhupāda! We remember your every moment.  
Without you there is no real substance.

*Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Institute*,

Bhaktiśāstrī Swami / Kapila dāsa / Dhruva dāsa / Bhakta Dr. Srinivas / Bhakta Edwin / Bhakta Dr. Raghu / Bhakta Sukheja / Bhakta Shrikanta / Bhaktin Mrs. Raghu / Bhaktin Munim

## Bhaktivedanta Institute of Religion and Culture

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we offer our humble obeisances at your feet. You established a comprehensive vision for inundating the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and through your writings and pastimes you continue to show us how Vaiṣṇava philosophy and practice can be presented to all people at all levels of society in all cultures. It is the greatest blessing to have you as our spiritual master, for you are the very paradigm of the preacher of *bhakti*.

By your teachings and example, we have learned that preaching is a transcendental art, and that there are many ways to present Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Since this preaching field is so vast and variegated, you teach us that an *ācārya* is one who “cannot be expected to conform to a stereotype, for he must find the ways and means by which Kṛṣṇa consciousness may be spread” (*Cc. Ādi-līlā* vol. 2, p. 23). You have shown us that an expert preacher presents the Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta* unchanged, but he does not always present this Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta* in exactly the same way. His behavior and speaking varies as the requirements of each preaching task are revealed to him by Kṛṣṇa.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu also exemplified this transcendental art of presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness in particular preaching situations, for He desired to entrap everyone in His “unique loving network.” Keśava Kāśmīrī was a Sanskrit *paṇḍita,* and therefore Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu used the language of a scholar, specifically that of Sanskrit poetics, to establish His superior knowledge in order to relieve the *paṇḍita* of his academic arrogance and make him receptive to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭ*ācārya* was a great scholar who espoused *navya-nyāya* logic and the philosophy of impersonalism. Śrī Caitanya behaved as the most submissive student and simply listened for seven days and seven nights to his misconceptions and afterwards proceeded to defeat him by the method of simple inquiry. The impersonalist Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī would accept Kṛṣṇa consciousness only after witnessing Śrī Caitanya’s effulgence and humble behavior.

We have directly witnessed this same transcendental art of preaching when you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the globe. But you taught us that this transcendental art of preaching is not merely a skill to be mastered, but an ability bestowed only upon the pure devotee by Śrī Śrī *Guru* and Gaurāṅga. You once prayed to your spiritual master and to Kṛṣṇa while on board the ship Jaladuta, “...if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding” (*Markine Bhāgavata-dharma,* verse 12). Indeed, you biographer has recorded numerous instances in which you caused the ears of the conditioned souls to open by using language that was understandable and familiar to them, and in this way won their hearts. So powerful is your transcendental art, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that it transformed all of us into your eternal servants.

Your comprehensive plan for preaching includes the presentation of Vaiṣṇava philosophy and practice to the intellectual community. Within the intellectual community today, modern science and its method dominates the entire realm of scholastic endeavors, even in philosophy and religion. In 1976, the Bhaktivedanta Institute was formed in order to defeat key scientific theories that support the destructive and exploitative philosophy of materialistic secularism, which is rapidly engulfing the world like a cancerous disease.

Several years before the formation of the Bhaktivedanta Institute, in a letter to Girirāja Swami, you described another area of intellectual preaching. Therein you mention that a doctorate of theology in comparative studies would equip one to “be able to present philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in comparison to other theological presentations” (Los Angeles, July 10, 1969). You go on to say that the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the highest theological presentation among all religious conceptions of the world, and that if one can prove this, “it will be a great accomplishment.” This specific task of preaching to the intellectual community is the essential purpose of the Bhaktivedanta Institute for Religion and Culture.

This Institute is a committee of devotee scholars and researchers that will serve ISKCON internally by functioning as a channel of advanced studies for the students in the ISKCON educational system, and externally by presenting knowledge of the ISKCON movement to the academic community. At the present time, we are working on several books. There will be a comprehensive bibliography of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, a book on the history of Western culture from the Vaiṣṇava perspective, a book on the religions of the world in comparison to Vaiṣṇava thought, and a book on the complete history of the *bhakti* movement, from its ancient Indian roots to its worldwide manifestation as ISKCON. Thus we are attempting to fulfill your desire for preaching *bhāgavata* philosophy to the intellectual community by presenting the *bhakti* movement from the perspective of various academic disciplines.

It is only by your mercy that the transcendental art of preaching to the intellectual class will be possible. We feel that you may be pleased with our efforts, and yet we feel completely unqualified to serve. Please bestow upon us the purity and intense determination to serve you so that we can know the ways and means by which to attract the hearts of even the most covered of conditioned souls. If we are a little successful in our preaching work, then perhaps we will be able to perform some service worthy of your attention.

*Your eternal servants,*

Garuḍa dāsa / Ravīndra-svarūpa dāsa / Śubhānanda dāsa / Śukavak dāsa / Dayānanda dāsa / Gaura Keśava dāsa / Brahmā-muhūrta dāsa / Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī / Haripriyā-devī dāsī

## Central USA College Preaching Centers

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Your divine presence in our hearts is the sole motivating force keeping us on the spiritual path. Remembrance of your pure devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa is our only source of courage. The sublime instructions, which you impart to us through your books, are our only means of sustenance. Each day we may have to tolerate persecution, ridicule, disappointments, and the multifarious temptations of *māyā*. All of these apparent inconveniences give us meaningful inspiration when we offer our humble obeisances unto you.

In the midst of this great ocean of Kali-yuga the waves of *māyā* are constantly trying to conquer us, both individually and as a worldwide movement. The only hope to remain pure is that our exclusive ambition is to please you. Pleasing you is the essence of our spiritual existence. To please you is the most priceless treasure. To attain this treasure we should be prepared to sacrifice everything of this world. For pleasing you we live, and for pleasing you we are willing to die.

Our service is very insignificant, and we are very fallen. Like lowly beggars we stand before you, begging you to please allow us to assist you in your most compassionate mission - the great mission of establishing the holy name of Kṛṣṇa as the supreme goal of life.

*Your servants,*

Rādhānātha Swami / Kṛṣṇa-katha dāsa / Karūṣa dāsa / Janakanātha dāsa / Tulasī dāsa / Cintāmaṇi dāsa / Buddhi-yoga dāsa / Śyāmasundara dāsa / Caitanyacandra dāsa

## Ministry of Architecture

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu states in the *Śikṣāṣṭaka* that, “...the holy name alone can render all benediction upon the living beings.” But, without the *bhakti-latā-bīja* offered by the all-merciful spiritual master, the actual connection between the conditioned *jīva* and Bhagavān, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is never established. Therefore, the *guru* is the paramount factor in attaining the *para-gati, prema bhakti*.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no higher principle than yourself.*

Lord Kṛṣṇa states in the *Bhagavad-gītā (*9.27), “O son of Kuntī, all that you do, all that you eat, all that you offer and give away, as well as all austerities that you may perform, should be done as an offering to Me.” And again in the *Gītā* Śrī Kṛṣṇa explains (3.15), “...the all-pervading Transcendence is eternally situated in the acts of sacrifice.” But, no act of devotional service will be accepted by Lord Kṛṣṇa without your approval. In fact, all acts of sacrifice, *yajñārtha-karma*, must be offered for your pleasure.

The fact that we can offer you anything at all is another aspect of your mercy. As Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s perfect servant, you are complete, *ātmārāma,* and personally in need of nothing. Yet you engage us. As the empowered *ācārya*, you command. You created ISKCON, and by your grace our very lives took on definition.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda, you alone give meaning to our actions.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you yourself have stated, “When you wash the floor, actually it is I who am washing, and you are helping me.” Or as you also gave the example regarding BTG magazine, “Actually it is I who have written all the articles.” Let us try to understand your position, and then automatically we will know our own. Without following your order we immediately become the stringed puppets of *māyā.*

*Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us never think we have any position independent of you.*

As for our fallen selves, we can only wonder at our supreme fortune. Indeed, the spiritual master’s benediction is *acintya*, as it has no cause and its benefit is immeasurable. We can only wish that our service will please you.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda, your satisfaction is our only measure of success.*

*Your servants in the Ministry for Architecture*,

Surabhīrabhipālayantam Swami / Guṇārṇava dāsa / Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Bhava-bhūti dāsa / Raṇajit dāsa / Vrajajana dāsa / Jayantī dāsa / Tek Chand / Mahāvīrya dāsa / Rāghavānanda dāsa / Rāma Tulasī dāsa / Gopāla-devī dāsa / Sunītā-devī dāsī / Maṅgalā-gopī-devī dāsī

## Ministry of Public Affairs

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Although I was not present at the time of your passing from this world, I will always remember a description of that historic occasion by one of my more fortunate God brothers who was there. He said that at the instant of your departure, every devotee in the room felt that Śrīla Prabhupāda had entered into his heart.

Your powerful transcendental presence is being increasingly and more intensely felt by all of us in the ISKCON Public Affairs Ministry as we daily observe how your International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness is growing at a rate none of us could have ever imagined. Nor can we fully understand, even now, exactly how this growth actually takes place. Certainly ISKCON is nondifferent from you, and this is borne out by the fact that every new ISKCON accomplishment or success and every fresh phase of ISKCON preaching is but one of the millions of brilliant facets that constitute your jewel like vision for the ISKCON of the future. Most of these facets we have yet to see manifested. We ever hunger to see and experience the new and yet undiscovered adventures that are the bulwark of your collective instructions for propagating Lord Caitanya’s sacred mission.

As ISKCON reaches more and more deeply into the public consciousness during the 1980s, we can’t help but stand in awe of your infinite kindness in allowing us not only to witness and report on this miraculous transformation firsthand, but also to participate in this process ourselves as much as we desire to. This privilege is your causeless mercy upon us.

Just as Lord Ananta Śeṣa never reaches the limit of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s qualities even though His millions of mouths have been describing them since time immemorial (and will continue to do so for eternity), we can never know the limits of your transcendental nature, even though we see it constantly unfolding before us. And even though we make some attempts to describe it or even some meager claim to know it, we will always fall far short, for no one can understand the mind of a pure devotee.

*Your servants in the ISKCON Public Affairs Ministry,*

Mukunda Goswami / Bhūtātmā dāsa / Uddhava dāsa / Bhārata dāsa / Nandinī-devī dāsī / Yaśodāmāyi-devī dāsī / Saṅgītā-devī dāsī / Yaśodā-priyā-devī dāsī / Karlāpati-devī dāsī

## USA Library Party

Oh, ‘er well wisher,  
This holy day  
A servant counts  
The gifts you gave.

Eighteen chapters,  
Kṛṣṇa’s song,  
Read and reread  
The whole life long.

Seventeen volumes,  
Caitanya-līlā,  
Bhakti treasure  
At last revealed.

Sixteen rounds  
Of Harer Nāma,  
Hare Kṛṣṇa  
Hare Rāma.

Fifteen verses,  
Vaiṣṇava jewels,  
Composed aboard the  
Jaladuta.

Fourteen days  
Before he left,  
Siddhānta bade you,  
”Save the West.”

Thirteen years  
A lone sannyāsī,  
’Fore opening the first  
ISKCON house.

Twelve short years  
In our midst,  
Who but you  
Would take the risk?

Eleven months  
in ’77,  
Vaiṣṇava death,  
Your final lesson.

Ten cantos  
Preserve your preaching;  
Nine methods  
Of bhakti teaching.

Eight verses,  
Gurv-aṣṭakam;  
Seven purposes  
Of ISKCON.

On six continents,  
Vaiṣṇava galore,  
Including five  
Untouched before.

Four principles  
Set us free,  
From the grip of  
Guṇas three.

Dancing, chanting,  
The name sublime,  
Two holy brothers,  
Gaura-Nitāi.

Kṛṣṇa-prema,  
Kṛṣṇa the One,  
By whom our zeros  
Billions become.

Prabhupāda, your gifts  
Who can count?  
Not even Śeṣa,  
Of endless mouths.

Count us, Prabhupāda,  
Our fervent pray,  
Grateful disciples,  
Ne’er to stray.

*Your servants on the USA Library Party,*  
Kalakaṇṭha dāsa / Jitāmitrā-devī dāsī
